# Experimental, alternative and optional behaviour: the switch register

Reference list of every switch that is off by default, exists for comparison, or can be changed from Settings or a DEBUG launch extra; [architecture.md](architecture.md#switches-and-experiments) explains how they fit together, and [decisions](decisions/README.md) holds the evidence.

This is the single register of switches that are off by default or exist for comparison. The rule behind it
(design rule, 2026-10-08/09): an experiment, or the losing arm of an A/B comparison, is kept, off by default and
labelled Experimental, unless it is impossible by first principles. [decisions](decisions/README.md) records the evidence
for each.

How to read it:

- DEBUG switches are launch extras named `test_*`; benchmark tunables are in square brackets. Release builds ignore
  them unless an entry names a Settings switch: a release launch is never a benchmark launch, whatever extras another app
  sends (fixed 2026-10-27; before, any `test_` extra skipped onboarding, the go-live disclosure and the End-live
  confirmation in a release build, and three GATT PHY arms below were release-reachable).
- "Control arm" means the default is the new behaviour and the switch restores the old one exactly, so an A/B can
  run in either direction.
- Phones are named by alias: S24, S22, M31, ASUS, Xiaomi, A55 (the reference set) and A15s. "Untested on phones"
  means the entry has unit tests but no measured phone run yet; an entry that cites numbers was measured.
- Entries marked with a Settings name appear in Settings; the row gives the path (several sit under Experiments, Privacy > Advanced privacy or Advanced > Connection).

Promoted features leave the list of experiments. Each has one default constant (`CallFeatureDefaults`, `ConnectivityFeatureDefaults`; privacy features are on by default for user sessions since 0.2.0-beta) and, where it still has one, a switch in Settings: the connectivity rows sit under Advanced > Connection, "Hide my ID from strangers" and "New identity for each stranger" under Privacy > Advanced privacy, Messages and automatic sync under Experiments. Voice calls, ringback, camera pause at the ear, lock-screen calls, call notifications, names and titles and audio-only live are simply on (no switch since 2026-10-17). `FeatureCatalog` (pure Kotlin) holds each feature's id, preference key, default, benchmark extra and texts, and the Settings cards are generated from it. An untouched switch follows the constant, an explicit choice sticks, and a DEBUG benchmark launch (any `test_*` extra) reads only its extras, default off, so case IDs stay valid and every release-ON feature runs OFF there unless its extra is passed.

**Requires / masked by.** An arm can be inert because a parent switch is off or because another switch already decides the same thing; rows of such arms carry a "Requires / masked by" note, so an A/B is not read from an arm that cannot act. Rows marked **Not wired** name a switch or helper that nothing in the app calls yet; they stay listed (no experiment is deleted) and do nothing.

## Streaming: rate control, queues, playout and bounded age

| Switch | Default | What it does and why it is kept |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Temporal layering (`test_temporal_layers`; Settings > Experiments, shown and honoured with or without Manual video settings since 2026-11-06) [temporal_layers] | Off | Drops discardable enhancement frames under congestion. Superseded by the no-loss default (2026-10-07); it cost about 2.5 VMAF on a calm link. |
| Relay child thinning (DEBUG `test_relay_child_thinning`) [relay_child_thinning] | Off (Experimental) | From the adaptive-scaling review (P2). The source encodes two temporal layers (libaom always; MediaCodec AV1; MediaCodec VP9 only with `test_hardware_temporal_layers`), and every frame of the second layer is marked discardable only when its bitstream proves nothing references it. Each relay then decides per direct child, from that child's queue age, delivery rate and late-media report, between all frames, only the base layer (half the frame rate, no re-encode, no keyframe) and audio only (video paused for that child, audio, configuration and lifecycle records still flow; the child shows "Video paused: weak link"). It steps down at once on a severe backlog and otherwise after a hold, and probes one level back up only after a calm hold that doubles after each failed probe (8 s to 60 s). Resuming from audio only waits for a keyframe, requested with the bounded-age back-off. Hop-local; no wire change (whole records are dropped and the end-to-end sequence check already accepts gaps). The source pays the layered encoding's cost (about 2.5 VMAF at equal bitrate) for the whole stream, because it cannot know about a weak link two hops away. Off: no layer control reaches the encoder, nothing is dropped for a layer and no notice is shown. Phone-measured on relay lanes: with thinning on the frozen share halved, but the layer controller never left level=full, so the stepping itself has not run on a phone (cause unclear; not functional there). Needs a relay phone test in which thinning engages (DECISIONS "Design RT"). **Requires / masked by:** the audio-only level can trip the child's own parent-link trickle close (that close is gated by `relay_child_reanchor`, and the weak-link pause is not passed to it), which would loop; latent, because thinning never left level=full on phones (DECISIONS "Known interactions", B10). |
| Bounded media age (`test_bounded_media_age`) [bounded_media_age] | Off | Calls keep speech within about 400 ms by degrading video (rate, AV1 non-reference drops, skip to keyframe, audio-only pause). Streams shed beyond about 2 s. It is under A/B against no-loss; the loser stays here. |
| Relay-child media age (Advanced "Relay: slow viewers skip to keyframes"; `test_relay_child_media_age=false` to disable) [relay_child_media_age] | On (design decision 2026-10-09) | Applies the bounded-age shedding to a forwarding viewer's child queue only. A child slower than the stream gets skip-to-keyframe video (audio kept) on its own branch instead of being withdrawn and rerouting to the source, which had halved the source's other child in a three-phone test. Its recovery keyframe requests back off from 1.5 s to one per 15 s while shedding recurs (reset after a calm minute), because each one adds a keyframe on every branch. Off: no-loss with withdrawal. Calls and direct senders stay no-loss. Needs a relay A/B. **Requires / masked by:** `=false` has no effect while `bounded_media_age` is on, which already sheds the relay child queue. In user launches the stored choice counts with or without Manual video settings (until the 2026-10-27 switch audit, B14, a stored OFF reverted to ON when Manual video settings was turned off). |
| Link pacing off (`test_link_pacing=false`) [link_pacing] | Pacing on | Control arm for the write pacer (a GATT double-charge in the pacer was fixed 2026-10-09). **Requires / masked by:** this is the parent of the pacer arms: with pacing off the pacer seed, per-peer seed, join probe, seed raise, drain guard, relay head-only exemption and lead cap all do nothing. |
| Link pacer seed off (`test_link_pacer_seed=false`) [link_pacer_seed] | Seed on | Control arm. A child link is paced from join at the learned single-link capacity (else 200-400 kbps by transport); the seed halves every 2 s after 4 s without an arrival report (floor 150 kbps), so a link whose reports never arrive shows its backlog in the app queue rather than the Android stack. Needs link pacing. **Requires / masked by:** `=false` also disables the per-peer seed, join probe, seed raise and drain guard. |
| Per-peer pacer seed (`test_link_pacer_peer_seed`) [link_pacer_peer_seed] | Off | A child link's pacer starts from that child's own last arrival report for this link (24 h, per transport and radio state) instead of this phone's best single-link probe, which seeded a ~65 kbps ASUS -> A15s link at about 1 Mbps. Phone-measured with no effect (an overnight A55 <-> M31 A/B: equal to the control), so it stays a non-default arm. **Requires / masked by:** link pacing and the pacer seed (`=false` of either disables it). |
| Early fan-out release off (`test_fanout_early_release=false`) [fanout_early_release] | Early release on | Control arm. A fan-out child whose backlog grew by 500 ms over 10 consecutive windows (at least 1.5 s behind, receiving less than offered, no keyframe drain) while the encoder is at its rate floor and a sibling is healthy is released, instead of after 24 of 30 s. |
| Delivery reports off (`test_delivery_reports=false`) [delivery_reports] | Reports on | Control arm restoring the behaviour from before 2026-10-09, except that it also forces the learned call start (`start_rate_guard`) off, so it is no longer an exact control: no call hello or version 3 report, cuts use the old references (0.85 x min(bytes the stack accepted, arrival trains)), and every cut is remembered as the peer's start rate again. A/B arms also pass `test_reset_learned_rates` [reset_learned_rates], which clears the 24 h learned start rates at launch; every launch logs them (`BENCHMARK learned_rates`). |
| Delivery-based probing (`test_delivery_rate_control`) [delivery_rate_control] | Off | On links whose peer reports delivery (link report version 3): the climb holds at 95% of what the link delivered at its last overuse and probes 10% past it, undoing a probe as soon as the measured queue grows; failed probes back off (10 to 30 s); a measured queue of 300 ms that is not draining is overuse; decreases may be 1 s apart. Off keeps the default: calls exchange version 3 and cut to 0.85 x the delivered rate without cascading. **Requires / masked by:** no effect on streams without `stream_delivery_reports` at both ends. |
| On-time reports off (`test_on_time_reports=false`) [on_time_reports] | Reports on | Control arm (2026-10-16). A stream viewer that has not measured its parent link yet (no record large enough to time) reports every 2 s that media still arrives on time, and the parent or relay keeps its starting pace while those reports show no lateness. Off restores the earlier behaviour: the viewer stays silent until its delay moves and the parent halves its pace after 4 s of silence down to 150 kbps, which starved low-rate streams (640x360 at 500 kbps or less fell to about 157 kbps). The reports use the existing link report format, so phones on older builds ignore them. |
| On-time seed raise off (`test_on_time_seed_raise=false`) [on_time_seed_raise] | Raise on | Control arm (promoted 2026-10-16, design decision). While a viewer reports media on time but has not measured its link yet, and the sender's own queue is at least 0.5 s old, the sender (or relay) raises its starting pace by 25% per report, up to 1500 kbps, instead of holding it. Reason: a phone with no measured link starts from a cautious 350 kbps over GATT, below a 400 kbps stream plus audio, so holding that pace grew the queue for the whole stream. A link that cannot carry the higher pace makes the viewer report late, which stops the raise and lets the pace fall as before. Off restores the held seed. Planned comparison: a join-time link measurement that seeds fresh links directly (PLAN). |
| Join probe seed (`test_join_probe_seed=true`) [join_probe_seed] | Off | Experimental (2026-10-17, option C of the starting-pace decision). Instead of starting a fresh stream link from the cautious prior and climbing with the on-time raise, the sender (and a relay's child link) lets its writer run unpaced for a bounded window at link start (1.5 s or 32 KiB, audio included), the viewer times the records that arrive back to back in its first 3 s as one burst (even when each record is too small to be a train) and reports it in the ordinary link report, and the first rate that arrives within 3 s of link start seeds the pace. A sender whose own queue held at least 0.5 s took the burst as the link's rate and measures it like any report; a sender that kept up only raises its seed to the burst rate when that is higher (never lower), so the on-time raise and the silence decay still apply, and the burst is not stored as a capacity. With no report inside 3 s the prior stands (`fallback=true`). Hop-local, no wire change: an older or switched-off viewer never reports a burst, so the prior stands; both ends should run the switch for a seed to arrive. Logs `BENCHMARK join_probe_seed: role=stream_tx\|relay_tx \| peer \| measuredKbps \| seedKbps \| probeMs \| fallback \| linkBound`. |
| Seed raise drain guard (`test_seed_raise_drain_guard=true`) [seed_raise_drain_guard] | Off | Experimental (2026-10-17). The on-time seed raise only helps while the pace, not the link, is the limit. With this on, a raise is skipped when the sender's queue age has not fallen by 100 ms since the previous report and its writer spent at least 40% of that time inside blocking write calls, more than the pacer held it (a link-limited writer; a pacer-limited writer sleeps in the pacer, so the raise continues). The seed is still held, not decayed. Only GATT writes block visibly, so a CoC or Classic link keeps the raise as it was. Logs `BENCHMARK pacer_seed_raise_blocked`. Off is the raise exactly as before. **Requires / masked by:** `on_time_seed_raise`, `on_time_reports`, link pacing and the pacer seed. Shed-aware since F4 (DECISIONS "Known interactions", B3): a fall in queue age across two reports that also saw the queue drop media (a bounded-age or relay-child shed, video dropped while its recovery keyframe is awaited, thinning) is not counted as draining; `pacer_seed_raise_blocked` gains `shed=true|false`. One seed rule for the sender and the relay (PacerSeedPolicy, `BENCHMARK pacer_queue_config`). |
| Relay establishment keeps 2M (`test_relay_establish_keep_2m=true`) [relay_establish_keep_2m] | Off | Experimental (2026-10-16). Today one failed 2M connection to a child pins that child to 1M for the rest of the stream, even when the connection never came up (the stack reported a connect failure, no link existed). Connection setup runs on the advertising PHY, so 1M cannot fix that failure, and 1M halves what the relay can send to the child. With this on, a relay child setup that failed before any link to the child existed keeps asking for 2M on the next try (`BENCHMARK link_phy: ... reason=establishment_failed \| next=2M`); a failure after the link existed, while the 2M PHY update was requested or in flight, still pins 1M as before. Off is the old rule exactly. Calls are not changed. |
| Relay child guard against own baseline (`test_receiver_node_baseline_p95=true`) [receiver_node_baseline_p95] | Off | Experimental (2026-10-17). A viewer that forwards a stream detaches its child when its own playback becomes unstable. Today the limit is 1.75 frame periods or 1.35x the viewer's typical frame time before the child joined; with this switch it also allows 1.35x the viewer's own pre-child worst-case (p95) frame time, so a relay that was already a little uneven is not mistaken for one the child overloaded. A real regression still detaches the child. |
| Stream delivery reports (`test_stream_delivery_reports`) [stream_delivery_reports] | Off | Stream viewers report delivery (version 3) every second and the stream sender uses it under its own switch. Every phone in the stream must run the same build (an older parent closes the link on version 3). Reports sent only for delivery carry the delivery-only flag, so the parent's lateness trend sees full reports exactly as before. |
| Learned-limit recovery (`test_ceiling_probe_recovery=true`) [ceiling_probe_recovery] | Off | Experimental. A fresh relay or fan-out ceiling may admit one link beyond it after a 2 min cool-down (doubling per failed probe, at most 64 min); healthy traffic above it clears it, a new saturation re-records it. Bad-2M marks age out after 24 h unless re-confirmed (default 7 days). Reason: learned limits outlive the runs that taught them; stale M31 relay (450) and GATT fan-out (315) ceilings and a bad-2M mark refused four viewers for up to 6 h. `test_reset_learned_state=true` (DEBUG) clears ceilings, sustained rates and bad-2M marks; every launch logs `BENCHMARK learned_state`. |
| Encoder bitrate adjuster off (`test_encoder_bitrate_adjuster=false`) [encoder_bitrate_adjuster] | Adjuster on | Control arm. On: a port of WebRTC's DynamicBitrateAdjuster (`EncoderBitrateAdjuster`) scales the bitrate requested from a MediaCodec encoder under Automatic by 0.25-1.0x from its measured output (judged every 3 s against one second of target bytes; never above the rate controller's target; no reduction while most frames sit at the VP9 maximum quantizer), because the M31 OMX.Exynos.VP9 produced 720 kbps for a 401 kbps target. No model matching. Logs `BENCHMARK bitrate_adjuster` at most once per second. Replaces the output-rate trim for MediaCodec encoders while on. Untested on phones. |
| Encoder frame-rate adjuster (`test_encoder_framerate_adjuster=true`) [encoder_framerate_adjuster] | Off (Experimental) | A/B arm. Applies WebRTC's FramerateBitrateAdjuster within WebRTC's own scope (OMX.Exynos. VP9/H.264). The codec keeps its configured FPS and is asked for target x configured / delivered FPS, where delivered FPS is the number of encoded frames in the last second of output timestamps; re-applied at most once per second; the measured adjuster is skipped, which silently re-enables the output-rate trim (the trim is normally off while the bitrate adjuster runs), so this arm is "frame-rate adjuster plus trim". Kept as an arm, not the default, because it corrects undershoot after FPS drops while the M31 OMX.Exynos.VP9 overshoots at a fixed, capped FPS. Untested on phones. |
| Encoder rate trim off (`test_encoder_rate_trim=false`) [encoder_rate_trim] | Trim on | Control arm. While the bitrate adjuster is on, the trim acts only for native libaom. It cannot act at the maximum quantizer. |
| Keyframe shaping (`test_keyframe_shaping`) [keyframe_shaping] | Off | Lowers a hardware encoder's target for one app-requested keyframe. Not promoted: scene-cut bursts are not keyframes. |
| Forced bitrate mode (`test_bitrate_mode` 0/1/2) [bitrate_mode] | Encoder's resolved mode | An M31 CBR test (-4 to 9% burst). CQ is rejected by the OMX encoder. |
| Hardware QP bounds (`test_hw_qp_min/max`) [hw_qp_min/max] | None | Ignored by the ACodec/OMX path (M31). Kept for Codec2 encoders. |
| Learned call start off (`test_start_rate_guard=false`) [start_rate_guard] | Guard on | Control arm. A call with no learned rate starts at a share of the measured link or 400 kbps instead of the request; learned sustained rates survive app updates (format 2, 24 h) and the start applies before the first frame. Off: build-keyed rates, and an unmeasured call starts at the request. Read when a call or stream starts. **Requires / masked by:** `test_delivery_reports=false` also turns it off, so that control arm is not exact. |
| Burst-aware drain (`test_burst_drain`) [burst_drain] | Off (Experimental) | A child's lateness that follows a large frame while the link now carries everything offered is attributed to that one burst instead of congestion, and large-frame grace lasts while the backlog keeps shrinking. Reason: a single 72 KB scene cut cut M31 -> ASUS GATT 300 to 206 kbps for 12 s with an empty sender queue. DEBUG only. |
| One-step playout growth (`test_playout_burst_growth=<1.0..4.0>`) [playout_burst_growth] | Off (Experimental; unset keeps the staircase) | Media later than the burst-lateness threshold grows the adaptive playout delay by this multiple of its lateness at once, instead of lateness + 50 ms per late frame (a staircase of separate holds during one burst). Costs a longer first hold and more held latency. Streams only: call clocks keep the staircase (since 2026-10-27; before, the arm also changed calls). DEBUG only. |
| Scene-cut shaping (`test_scene_cut_shaping=0\|1\|2`) [scene_cut_shaping] | Off (Experimental) | A raw-frame luma detector finds a hard cut before the frame reaches a MediaCodec encoder; 1 requests that frame as a sync frame at a lowered target, 2 only lowers the target, and the target is restored once the frame is encoded. Only raw-fixture and ByteBuffer camera input pass the app; live Camera2 surface input does not. Off requests nothing; benchmark traces still log the detector's decisions (`BENCHMARK scene_cut`). DEBUG only. |
| VP9 quantizer headroom (`test_vp9_q_floor=<1..254>`) [vp9_q_floor] | Off (Experimental) | While the parsed `base_q_idx` stays below the floor the encoder target is lowered, so a scene cut is not coded at a stale low quantizer (`QuantizerHeadroomGovernor`). Unset leaves the target alone. DEBUG only. |
| Encoder ranking by measured behaviour (`test_encoder_ranking`) [encoder_ranking] | Off (Experimental; design decision Q3) | An encoder whose recorded sessions kept scene-cut frames within about 0.5 s of its own stream (and, for software, encode time within budget) ranks above one measured to burst, for example the M31's libaom AV1 (26 KB cuts) above its OMX VP9 (135 KB). Off: the static hardware-first order. Measurements are recorded in both arms. DEBUG only. |
| Prefer hardware AV1 (`test_prefer_hw_av1=false` is the control arm) [prefer_hw_av1] | On (design decision 2026-10-27, release 0.4.1; no A/B required) | Automatic sends with a MediaCodec AV1 encoder that reports `isHardwareAccelerated` whenever the phone has one: first for streams (a viewer that cannot decode AV1 is refused at join, as for any stream codec) and for calls when the peer advertises AV1 decode without the legacy VP9 preference (otherwise VP9). The bundled libaom and Android software AV1 are never promoted by this rule; a component named by a `NoAutomaticHardwareAv1` policy entry (none yet) is skipped. Manual and `test_codec` choices, family VP9 call profiles and thermal policy are unchanged. Off: hardware AV1 ranks after the other hardware encoders (hardware VP9 first). Each call/stream logs `BENCHMARK encoder_choice` with its reason. DEBUG only; release always prefers it.  |
| HEVC and hardware H.264 codecs, Design HC (masters `test_hevc_codec`, `test_h264_codec`; shipped constants `ConnectivityFeatureDefaults.HEVC_CODEC`, `H264_CODEC`) [hevc_codec, h264_codec] | On (design decision 2026-10-28, release 0.4.2, after the phone codec smoke and heat runs; a benchmark launch without the extra keeps them off) | Adds `BTV3` codecs 5 (HEVC) and 4 (H.264) with their hello and announcement bits (PROTOCOL "Draft: HEVC and H.264 video codecs"). Platform encoders and decoders only; Automatic uses hardware encoders only (software HEVC/H.264 appear only in the manual picker). The receiver checks every access unit with a bounded structural gate before its decoder; the sender prepends the parameter sets to each IDR and sends no config packet. Off: no HEVC or H.264 bit, picker entry or log; every byte as today. DEBUG `test_hc_vectors=true` saves short access-unit sequences from the phone's HC encoder for the Windows and Apple fixtures. |
| Prefer hardware HEVC / H.264 (`test_prefer_hevc=false`, `test_prefer_hw_avc=false` are the control arms) [prefer_hevc, prefer_hw_avc] | On whenever a master is on (masters on since 0.4.2) | Automatic order hardware AV1, HEVC, VP9, then H.264, before any software encoder; streams follow open question Q-HC1 (DECISIONS "Design HC"): VP9 ahead of HEVC. Off: that codec stays advertised and selectable by hand, but Automatic never picks it (the old order). `test_av1_soft_decode_bit=false` stops end-to-end decode-mask bit 4 ("my AV1 decoding is software"). A family `CallVp9Compatibility` entry (ASUS `qcom-msmnile-call`) keeps the ASUS's own calls on VP9. A peer's "prefers VP9" bit 7 puts VP9 first except below a hardware HEVC encoder the peer decodes; `test_bit7_hw_hevc=false` is the control arm (VP9 first even over hardware HEVC, the first draft's rule). HEVC and H.264 encoders request the lowest standard level the session needs (never above what they list); `test_hc_session_level=false` is the control arm (the highest listed level, which made Exynos and Qualcomm HEVC streams signal level 6.0). DEBUG only; Apple `-NearbyPreferHEVC 0` / `-NearbyPreferAVC 0`. |
| Bundled libvpx VP9 encoder in Automatic, Design VX (`test_prefer_libvpx=true|false`; shipped constant `ConnectivityFeatureDefaults.LIBVPX_AUTOMATIC`) [prefer_libvpx] | On (design decision 2026-10-28, release 0.4.2; a benchmark launch without the extra keeps it off) | The app carries its own VP9 encoder (libvpx, built from a pinned source) for phones without a hardware AV1, HEVC or VP9 encoder. Off: it is a manual choice only ("VP9 - optimized software", last in the picker); no Automatic, call-fallback or family-policy path picks it, and every other order is unchanged. On: calls use hardware AV1, HEVC and VP9, then libvpx VP9, then hardware H.264, then libaom AV1 and Android's software encoders; streams (Q-HC1 A) use hardware AV1, VP9 and HEVC, then libvpx VP9, then hardware H.264, then the rest: libvpx stays below every hardware AV1, VP9 and HEVC encoder because it costs about 4x their CPU. libaom stays the last resort, used only when nothing above it starts. Measured before turning it on: about the same quality per bit as hardware HEVC and better than hardware H.264, at several times the CPU of a hardware encoder (DECISIONS "Automatic codec order after the 2026-10-28 phone measurements"); see `codec_power_aware` for battery saver and warm phones. A phone with it advertises VP9 encoding in both arms, so a hand-picked libvpx stream is accepted. Its experimental temporal layering is not available (AV1 only). |
| Power-aware codec start (`test_codec_power_aware=false` is the control arm; shipped constant `ConnectivityFeatureDefaults.CODEC_POWER_AWARE`) [codec_power_aware] | On (design decision 2026-10-28, release 0.4.2; a benchmark launch without the extra keeps it off) | When battery saver is on (`PowerManager.isPowerSaveMode`) or the phone is already warm (thermal status LIGHT or above, read with the same battery fallback and forecast inputs as the thermal adaptation) as a call or stream starts, Automatic moves the phone's usable hardware encoders ahead of a software first choice, for example hardware H.264 instead of libvpx VP9 on a phone without hardware VP9 or HEVC. Only phones whose Automatic choice would otherwise be a software encoder change; it is decided once at session start, never during a call or stream; a manual choice, a family VP9 rule and a peer's "prefers VP9" bit are never overridden. Off: the plain Automatic order. Reason: libvpx costs several times the CPU of a hardware encoder (DECISIONS "Automatic codec order after the 2026-10-28 phone measurements"). DEBUG only; release always on. |
| Hardware H.264 when a software encoder cannot keep up (`test_sw_shortfall_avc`) [sw_shortfall_avc] | Off (Experimental, 2026-11-07) | When this phone's software encoder (libvpx VP9 or libaom AV1) sends under 80 % of the frame rate in force for 3 five-second windows in a row after a warm-up window (about 20 s), while the camera or raw input delivered that rate and the encoder itself was the limit (encode time p95 above the frame interval, or input frames dropped while it was busy), that encoder, size and frame rate are remembered on this phone for 7 days. The next call or stream that would start with that software encoder at the same or a higher pixel rate starts with the phone's hardware H.264 encoder instead, when the peer decodes H.264 (calls) and Automatic may use it. This changes only the codec choice: the requested size, frame rate and bitrate stay as asked and the change is logged (`encoder_choice ... reason=sw_shortfall_prefers_hw_avc \| sw_shortfall=true \| slow=<encoders>`); it is decided once at the start, never during a call or stream; a hand-picked encoder, a family VP9 rule, a peer's "prefers VP9" bit and the power-aware start are never overridden. A shortfall is logged in both arms (`BENCHMARK sw_encoder_shortfall: ... mode=record\|observe`) and only the on arm remembers it; `test_reset_learned_state` clears the memory, the emergency wipe deletes it, and the launch line `BENCHMARK sw_shortfall_policy` lists it. No wire change. Untested on phones. **Requires / masked by:** Automatic encoder choice with the H.264 master on (`test_h264_codec`; a benchmark launch without it keeps H.264 off) and a hardware H.264 encoder Automatic may use; recording works with any software encoder, a hand-picked one included. |
| Software AV1 decoding where the platform has no AV1 decoder, Design AD (Apple launch argument `-NearbyDav1d 0` is the control; Windows: its debug-switch mechanism) | Not built (approved 2026-10-27, Windows too by Q-AD1; on when built) | The Apple app decodes AV1 with the pinned dav1d library when VideoToolbox has no AV1 decoder (before A17 Pro and M3); the Windows app does so when no Media Foundation AV1 decoder starts. Either then advertises AV1 decode and end-to-end bit 4 ("my AV1 decoding is software"). Off: AV1 decode only where the platform decodes it, as today. No new wire format. |
| Hardware intra refresh (`test_intra_refresh_frames=<2..600>`) [intra_refresh_frames] | Off (Experimental) | Sets the MediaFormat intra-refresh period in frames at configure. Whether the encoder applied it is read from its output format and logged (`BENCHMARK encoder_capabilities`). Unset leaves periodic keyframes. Also a setting of the encoder bench. DEBUG only. |
| Hardware temporal layers (`test_hardware_temporal_layers`) | Off (Experimental, DEBUG) | With temporal layering on, also asks MediaCodec encoders for two temporal layers. Off: on the M31 OMX VP9 encoder the error-resilient layered stream lost about 11 VMAF at the same rate and burst harder; frames an encoder makes droppable on its own are still verified and used. **Requires / masked by:** `test_temporal_layers` (the layering switch). |
| Stream buffer/flush, playback delay override, decoder low latency, release at playout, decoder choice (`test_stream_buffer_kib`, `test_stream_buffer_bytes` (exact bytes, wins over the KiB value), `test_stream_flush`, `test_playback_delay_ms`, `test_decoder_low_latency`, `test_release_video_at_playout`, `test_decoder`) | Off / defaults | I/O and playout experiments. Output holding worsened losses and freezes. |
| GATT full-slot source-scan pause, output holding | Off | Unpromoted; kept for comparison (see DECISIONS). |
| Viewer decoder size-change handling (`test_decoder_size_rebuild=false` to disable) [decoder_size_rebuild] | On (2026-10-13) | Control arm. On: a keyframe whose coded size (read from the VP9 keyframe header or the AV1 sequence header) or codec configuration differs from the viewer decoder's makes the viewer release its MediaCodec and create one for it (a rung change of the Auto stream ladder or a call's resolution ladder, an encoder restart with new csd); new configuration is held for that keyframe instead of being queued as a separate codec-config buffer; recovery re-queues the newest csd; `KEY_MAX_WIDTH/HEIGHT` is set where the decoder reports adaptive playback; the display follows the new size. The first decoder is configured at the size of the first keyframe, not the header's maximum. Why: the decoder was configured once at the header size, a size change was only logged, and recovery re-queued the stream-start csd (a stale AV1 sequence header after a rung change); Android documents mid-stream format changes only for adaptive-playback VP8/VP9/H.264/H.265 and not for AV1. Off restores one decoder at the header size. The sender-side keyframe marker (`FLAG_DISCONTINUITY` on the first keyframe after a size-changing restart) and the late-joiner config cache that keeps only the current encoder's csd are not switched: both only act when an encoder restarts at another size. Not yet measured on phones. |

## Viewer and Watch screen

Display switches of the viewer and of a live broadcaster. Local UI unless a row says it adds a wire record.

| Switch | Default | What it does and why it is kept |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Viewer frame age: stale-picture notice and "Last update" label (Settings > Video and calls "Warn when video freezes"; DEBUG `test_viewer_frame_age` is the benchmark switch) | Promoted 2026-10-11 (default OFF until its phone test passes; one constant, `ViewerFeatureDefaults.FRAME_AGE`) | From an external review of multi-hop media (2026-10-11): with A -> relay B -> viewer C, when B leaves and returns while A keeps sending, C must show an interruption instead of a frozen frame presented as live. Local UI only, no wire change; off, no monitor exists and the Watch screen is unchanged. A picture is stale when no new picture was presented for more than max(1.5 s, 3 frame intervals), or when the parent link was lost and no picture of a newer link has been shown yet. The 1.5 s floor sits just above the largest single Bluetooth burst the adaptive playout delay is built to absorb (0.65-1.3 s scene-cut frames on GATT), so ordinary bursts never flash a warning; three intervals only matter below 2 FPS. Stale shows a light dim over the frozen picture and a badge: "Signal lost - reconnecting" when the link was lost, "Picture stalled - waiting" when the link is up and nothing arrives, plus "Last picture N s ago"; the older centre overlay is not drawn meanwhile and its message becomes the badge's last line. The Watch status chip says "Signal lost" or "Picture stalled" instead of "Watching ...". The first picture of a newer link, or of the same link after a stall, clears everything at once. A broadcaster pause, an ended stream and the app being in the background are never stale (the stall count restarts at the resume). A small label "Last update <1 s ago" (bottom left) shows the age of the last picture. It is counted on this phone and is NOT the camera-to-screen delay: the stream carries only the sender's MediaCodec presentation timestamps (no wall-clock capture time) and no clock relation to this phone exists, so end-to-end age is unknowable here; adding one would need a clock-sync design in PROTOCOL. The clock-free part is logged: both timestamps come from the sender, so newest-received minus presented is how far the picture trails the newest video held (`behindReceivedMs`), and adding the time since that video arrived gives `frameAgeMs`, a lower bound of the real age that excludes everything the sender and relays still hold. Lines (`BENCHMARK ` prefix in DEBUG): once a second `viewer_frame_age: state=live|stale|waiting|paused|ended | cause=none|no_frames|link_lost | sinceFrameMs | frameAgeMs | behindReceivedMs | playoutDelayMs | maxGapMs | thresholdMs | staleEpisodes | recoveries | linkEpoch`; transitions `viewer_stale: event=enter|cause|clear` (clear: `reason=frame|paused|ended|reset`, `staleForMs`, `gapMs` = the whole freeze) and `viewer_link: event=start|playout_base|lost|restored`. Audio and delay on recovery were verified by reading the code, not changed: every link builds a new playout clock at the profile's base delay (`newViewerLinkPlayoutClock`), audio decoder, AudioTrack and video decoder, and closes them when the link ends, so nothing queued or learned on a lost link reaches its replacement (a source or relay also builds a new per-viewer queue for the rejoining viewer, `BluetoothVideoSender` or `RelayChildQueue`, which holds only media offered after the join); `ViewerLinkPlayoutTest` keeps that, and `viewer_link: event=playout_base` shows it per link on phones. Within one link the adaptive delay still grows on late media and shrinks after 60 s calm, by the no-loss design; a stall that keeps the link up therefore replays its backlog late, and `playoutDelayMs`/`behindReceivedMs` measure how much (the bounded-age experiment above is the separate answer). **Phone test (fold into the relay loss/rejoin lane: A -> relay B -> viewer C on CoC and GATT, plus a direct A -> C control; B leaves for 10-30 s by killing its app or switching Bluetooth off, 10 cycles; switch on at C, repeat once with it off), pass when:** (1) `viewer_stale: event=enter` logs at C within about 2 s of the last picture (`sinceFrameMs` at most threshold + 0.5 s), the badge and chip show it, and the frozen frame is never labelled Watching; (2) the cause becomes `link_lost` when C notices the loss (no later than the existing 5-8 s detection) and the badge text matches the cause; (3) the first picture after B returns, or after C re-routes to A, ends it with `event=clear | reason=frame` and a `gapMs` close to the real outage, with no stale state left while pictures keep arriving; (4) over 10 minutes of undisturbed playback on a healthy link no `event=enter` appears except where `viewer_frame_age maxGapMs` really exceeded 1,500 ms; (5) across the 10 cycles every `playout_base` equals the base delay (AUTO 250 ms, a relaying viewer at most its fixed reserve) and `playoutDelayMs` at cycle 10 is not above cycle 1's bound, with `recoveries` equal to the cycles survived; (6) the receiver audio trace shows no audio timestamp older than the first one after the rejoin and none repeated; (7) a 30 s broadcaster pause never shows stale and a stall after the resume counts from the resume; (8) with the switch off the cycle draws nothing and logs no `viewer_*` line. Not yet tested on phones; JVM tests only. |
| Live media state: Muted / Camera off for viewers (Settings > Video and calls "Show Muted and Camera off to viewers"; DEBUG `test_live_media_state` is the benchmark switch) [live_media_state] | Off (until phone test T-LM1 and every phone/relay has this build) | A video broadcaster's Mute and Camera buttons always work locally (audio dropped at the microphone encoder output with the codec configuration kept; camera and encoder released, keyframe-first restart). With the switch on, each change also sends one authenticated stream-state record (values 4 to 6 of `StreamLifecycleState`, PROTOCOL "Experimental live media state") so viewers show "Muted" / "Camera off". Builds without the record drop the link on an unknown state value, which is why it ships off. Off: viewers just hear silence or see a still picture; nothing is added to the wire.  |
| Receiver UI stability off (`test_receiver_ui_stability=false`) [receiver_ui_stability] | Stability on | Control arm (2026-10-11, `ReceiverRecoveryNoticePolicy`, `receiverBrowserEmptyStatus`). A recovery that begins within 3 s of the previous "Recovering video" cover's release keeps that cover and label reveal through gaps under 1 s instead of switching the 70% black cover off and on (saved S22 viewer: about a dozen flashes in 30 s; 45% of gaps between recoveries are under 1 s); an isolated recovery clears the moment it ends, and the cover is never released while the decoder reports recovery. The browser line says "A stream is in range. Trying it again in about N s." while the only stream is cooling down after a failed join, instead of "No nearby streams yet". Off: the cover follows the decoder flag one to one and the old line. Lines `Receiver recovery UI: repeating episode, cover held` / `held cover released` and `BENCHMARK le_link_policy ... receiverUiStability=`. Control arm, not yet tested on phones: a viewer with induced repeated recoveries (relay loss/rejoin, relay lane) and a failed join; pass = no cover off/on pair under 1 s apart in the log, the label reveals once per burst, and no corrupted frame is visible while the cover is held. |
| Watch list grouping off (`test_stream_row_grouping=false`) [stream_row_grouping] | Grouping on | Control arm (2026-10-12, `groupStreamRoutes`). Every viewer that relays a broadcast advertises it again under the same logical stream token, so a phone near the source and three relaying viewers listed one broadcast four times, each row named after the advertiser. On: one row per broadcast (logical token and codec), named from the source's own offer or its title, or "Live from a phone further away" when only relays are in range; a tap joins the best route, ordered as reconnection orders them (joinable first, then routes whose establishment is not failing, then fewest relays). The other routes stay available for reconnection. UI only, no wire change. Off: one row per advertiser, as before. Needs a three-phone check (source, relaying viewer, new viewer near both). |

## Relay and fan-out admission

| Switch | Default | What it does and why it is kept |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Relay child re-anchor (`test_relay_child_reanchor=false` to disable) [relay_child_reanchor] | On | A relay whose child link carries under 50 kbps for 2 s with media waiting closes that child's connection so it reconnects on a new anchor (Bluetooth HCI logs showed a child anchor inside the parent's multi-packet window carrying 4-37 kbps for the whole connection, otherwise 349-378). At most 2 per child per minute, then the withdrawal policy decides. Stream relays only; the private-call relay is unchanged. **Requires / masked by:** this switch is the relay-side close. The child's watch of its parent link (silence and trickle close) has its own switch, `relay_parent_watch`, which follows this one when it is not given, so `test_relay_child_reanchor=false` alone still turns it off and the trickle can be A/B-tested with or without the relay-side close; the same-parent preference (`relay_prefer_parent`, with the grace) is a separate switch, off by default since build 4 (one relay-child recovery policy, logged as `BENCHMARK relay_recovery_config`; DECISIONS "Known interactions", B2). It also masks `relay_reanchor` (the Experimental child-link re-anchor): the close reconnects the child before that arm fires. |
| Relay recovery grace (`test_relay_recovery_grace=false` to disable) [relay_recovery_grace] | On | After a link loss that makes the child prefer its previous relay (the re-anchor above), reconnection waits up to 2 s for that relay's offer instead of taking the first offer heard. Manual test 2026-10-10: the A15s heard the full S24 source 147 ms after the ASUS re-anchor, chose it and waited 15 s without recovering. After 2 s any route is allowed. Stream relays only. **Requires / masked by:** `relay_prefer_parent` (the grace applies only after its link-loss preference), which is off by default since build 4, so the grace is inert unless `test_relay_prefer_parent=true`. |
| Relay cold start (`test_relay_cold_start=false` to disable) [relay_cold_start] | On | A viewer with no relay evidence (relay proven 0, no fresh relay ceiling) counts the incoming link it already carries and may forward to one child. Without it a fresh install (`probe none, prior 700 kbps`) never relays a 300 kbps stream (it would need 807) until it has been a source. The receiver-node health detach and congestion withdrawal still roll it back and record a relay ceiling. Line `BENCHMARK relay_admission`. |
| Relay budget floor without a fresh ceiling (`test_relay_no_ceiling_floor`) [relay_no_ceiling_floor] | Off (Experimental, 2026-11-07) | Design decision 2026-11-06 ("Relay admission without a measured ceiling"). A viewer that may relay counts a budget of at least 2 x the per-link load whenever it has no fresh measured relay ceiling (a saturation recorded in the last 6 hours), however small the relay load it proved before, as the cold start does for a phone that never relayed. Today a phone that once relayed a small stream cleanly is refused a larger one that a never-measured phone would take: its budget is the larger of the 700 kbps prior and its proven load plus one link, while a relay needs two links (receive and forward). The floor admits exactly one child; a fresh relay ceiling keeps the measured overload guard, and the receiver-node health detach and the congestion withdrawal still undo a relay that does not keep up and record a ceiling. Off is the existing rule exactly (the cold start alone). `BENCHMARK relay_admission` adds `floor=none\|cold_start\|no_ceiling` only while it is on; the launch line `BENCHMARK relay_floor_policy: coldStart= \| noCeilingFloor=` shows the arm. Video and audio-only relays alike. Hop-local, no wire change. Untested on phones. **Requires / masked by:** a viewer that may relay; `test_relay_cold_start=false` does not mask it (the floor covers proven 0 too). |
| Fan-out growth in planned-link units (`test_fanout_proven_links=false` to disable) [fanout_proven_links] | On | Without a fresh ceiling a source may add one link beyond its proven load; proven is measured wire kbps while a planned link carries a 20% margin, so proven is scaled by 1.2 before the comparison. Without it a 300 kbps source could never pass 2 children (ASUS proven 737 + 403.5 < 3 x 403.5). Fresh ceilings and the fan-out rollback are unchanged. Line `BENCHMARK fanout_admission`. |
| Relay parent trickle close (`test_relay_parent_trickle=false` to disable) [relay_parent_trickle] | On | A relayed child also closes a parent link that delivers under 1/8 of the stream bitrate for a whole 5 s window, not only a silent one, and reconnects through link-loss recovery. In one run M31 -> A15s trickled about 12 kbps for 17 s; the relay's re-anchor closed its socket but the L2CAP channel stayed up while the stack drained, so the child never reconnected. The 4-37 kbps collision band closes; the ~120 kbps 2M one-PDU pair does not. **Requires / masked by:** `relay_parent_watch` (the trickle is judged only inside the child's parent-link watch, which follows `relay_child_reanchor` when not given). A window in which the relay paused this child's video for a weak link (Experimental relay child thinning at audio only) counts as a pause and is not judged, because audio alone is far below 1/8 of the video bitrate (DECISIONS "Known interactions", B10). |
| Relay parent-link watch (DEBUG `test_relay_parent_watch=true\|false`) [relay_parent_watch] | Follows `relay_child_reanchor` (on) | Child side of relay recovery, split out of `relay_child_reanchor`: a relayed child closes a parent link that delivers no byte for 5 s (unless the parent said the stream is paused) and, with `relay_parent_trickle`, one that trickles; it then reconnects through link-loss recovery. Not given (and always in release) it follows `relay_child_reanchor`, so the earlier control arm keeps its meaning; given, it lets the child-side close be A/B-tested apart from the relay-side close. Why: a starved relayed link can stay up with nothing arriving, and the relay's close may never reach the child. Logged in `BENCHMARK relay_recovery_config` (parentWatch). |
| Viewer link silence watch (DEBUG `test_viewer_link_silence=false` to disable) [viewer_link_silence] | On | A viewer link that the relay parent-link watch does not cover (every direct link, and GATT links) is closed when a started, live video stream with its camera on delivered no byte for 8 s; link-loss recovery then reconnects, and the close is also what tells the source to drop the dead viewer. Why: the viewer's read has no timeout, the viewer's heartbeats keep reaching the source and the source's writes keep returning, so a parent link that stays up but delivers nothing would otherwise show a frozen picture until the user leaves. A healthy source sends a frame every 1/FPS, so 8 s of total silence (the source's own child-heartbeat timeout) is not jitter. A paused stream, a camera-off media state and audio-only live (whose Experimental DTX may send nothing) are never judged. Default on because it acts only in a state that otherwise never recovers; no such stall was found in the saved runs, so it is a safety net, not a measured fix. **Requires / masked by:** runs only where `relay_parent_watch` is not watching the link (one silence watch per link). |
| Relay same-parent preference (DEBUG `test_relay_prefer_parent=true` brings it back) [relay_prefer_parent] | Off (since build 4, 2026-10-27; followed `relay_child_reanchor`, on, before) | Child side of relay recovery, split out of `relay_child_reanchor`: after a plain link loss of an active relayed route the child tries the same relay first (and, with `relay_recovery_grace`, waits up to 2 s for its offer), so a relay's re-anchor reconnects the same child instead of rerouting it. Off by default and always in release: on a stream chain S22 > M31 > ASUS whose middle phone was killed and relaunched (valid kills only), the child re-anchored 5/5 without the preference (freeze median 2.9 s) vs 4/5 with it (about 4 s, one never recovered): waiting for a parent that has gone costs more than it saves (DECISIONS "Relay same-parent preference off"). Logged in `BENCHMARK relay_recovery_config` (preferParent, recoveryGrace with its effective value). |
| Relay child stall guard (`test_relay_child_stall_guard=false` to disable) [relay_child_stall_guard] | On (2026-10-11) | Control arm. On: an LE child link (GATT or CoC, not Classic) that delivers no inbound byte for 2 s between link-up and the end of the end-to-end handshake is closed by the relay (`BENCHMARK relay_child_stall: ... action=close`), and a stall before hop authentication is counted apart from refusals (3 stalls within 2 minutes mark the child for 30 s, then 60, 120 and 300 s; see "Relay stalls counted apart"); `... result=ok | handshakeMs | maxSilentMs` records the margin on every healthy join. Why: a relay is peripheral to its parent and central to its child, both at 15 ms with a fixed anchor phase; on the M31 (Exynos 9611) a child anchor 11.3-13.7 ms after the parent anchor gave the child link no completed exchange at all: all 9 deaths in the saved relay runs were `0x08` 5.0-5.1 s after the last completed packet (77-92 ms after the update to 15 ms in the four diag cases), and the stack's status 8 never counted as an establishment failure, so the relay held its only child slot for 5.9 s per attempt and looped. Healthy hello replies took 59-214 ms (30 links; one 1.24 s reply came over a link starving at 6 kbps). It does not change which anchor a connection draws; it shortens a dead attempt to about 3 s and makes it visible to the fallback. Off restores the 5.9 s wait exactly. Evidence and the full cause: DECISIONS "Relay dual-role scheduling (2026-10-11)"; A/B in PLAN. |
| Relay child pacing: audio exemption on the head record (`test_relay_pace_head=false` to disable) [relay_pace_head] | On (2026-10-11) | Control arm. On: a relay's child writer skips its link pacer only when the next record is audio. The child queue is strictly ordered (the child rejects a sequence that goes backwards), so audio queued behind a video frame cannot be written first; the earlier test skipped the pacer whenever audio was queued anywhere, and a backlog always holds audio (50 records/s), so a standing backlog was written unpaced and the Android socket buffer took the excess instead of the app queue. A saved relay run (S24 -> M31 -> ASUS over LE CoC, 200 kbps, about 281 kbps on the wire): the M31 -> ASUS link carried a flat 144 kbps (HCI snoop, one PDU per 15 ms event); the relay's app queue read 1.3-1.9 s behind, but bytes the app had written minus bytes the controller had sent were 57-80 KB (3.2-4.4 s, writes blocked up to 2.0 s once the buffer was full) and the ASUS saw 5.3-6.2 s of lag. Off restores the any-audio-queued test exactly. Audio at the head is never held, as before. Expected effect: the backlog stays in the app queue, where the relay-child media-age budget (400 ms once the child reports a pinned delay) and the withdrawal rules (2.5 s for 3 s, 6 s) read it, so a link slower than the stream sheds or withdraws sooner instead of hiding seconds below the app. Lines: `BENCHMARK relay_pacing` (headOnly, heldMs, exemptWrites, exemptWaitMs, paceLeadMs). Not yet measured on phones; A/B in PLAN "Relayed viewers hold seconds of media". |
| Relay pace lead cap - Experimental (`test_relay_pace_lead_cap=true`) [relay_pace_lead_cap] | Off | While the child's last report says its playout is pinned at the maximum and media is still late, the relay writes at most 250 ms of measured-rate link time ahead of the link (`LinkPacer.setLeadCapNs`) instead of letting the 20% pace gain run ahead without bound. Why: the gain lets the link be probed upward, but with a standing backlog each second adds 0.2 s that the stack holds below the app; an offline model on the diagM1 timeline (not a measurement) fills the 70 KB socket buffer in about 15 s even with the head fix. Tradeoff: if the child's train measurement reads low, the capped pace is below the gained one while the child is late; fewer stale frames reach the child and its shed starts sooner. The same model gives median lag 5.2 -> 2.7 s and 214 -> 158 video frames over 45 s. Hop-local (the child's own report about this link), no wire change; needs link pacing. Same `BENCHMARK relay_pacing` line (capMs, modelLeadMs, childLate). Not yet measured on phones. |
| Relay time-based queue - Experimental (`test_relay_time_queue=true`; `test_time_queue_budget_ms`, `test_time_queue_floor_ms`) [relay_time_queue, time_queue_budget_ms, time_queue_floor_ms] | Off | A relay's child queue sheds to the next keyframe at a target sized in milliseconds of media instead of the fixed "stream budget (2 s) minus the child's playout": target = clamp(time the last keyframe needs at the child's measured drain rate + two frame intervals + the worst drain stall of the last minute, floor, min(latency budget, fixed budget)). Defaults: latency budget 1 500 ms (300-5 000), floor 400 ms (100-1 000, the earlier fixed minimum). A stall is how long a record waited for the link beyond its own arrival and the airtime the measured rate predicts for the record before it (10% tolerance). In a sustained overload (offered above 1.1 times the drain) the jitter term is dropped, because a queue that cannot catch up only gains delay from a cushion. With no drain measurement yet, or at the latency budget, the fixed budget applies. Why: the fixed budget lets a link that is only slightly slower than the stream hold up to 1.75 s before it sheds, and cannot see a link whose keyframe alone takes longer than the queue. Tradeoff: the first stall is unmeasured and may cost one shed (a keyframe request) that the fixed budget would have ridden out. Hop-local (this relay's queue and its direct child's arrival report), no wire change; the shed itself, keyframe recovery, cooldown and keyframe grace are unchanged; audio, configuration and lifecycle records are never shed. Applies only while relay-child skip-to-keyframe is on (its default). Line `BENCHMARK relay_time_queue` (both arms: arm, queueAgeMs, targetMs, legacyBudgetMs, drainKbps, offeredKbps, jitterMs, keyFrameDrainMs, overload, shedEvents, shedFrames) and `BENCHMARK time_queue_arms`. JVM-tested with injected clocks. Phone-measured and inert: with the child reporting no arrival train the drain rate was 0 kbps, so the arm never saw an overload (not functional on phones until a sustained drain estimate exists; see the next row). A/B plan in DECISIONS "Time-based relay queues and viewer buffers". **Requires / masked by:** relay-child shedding or the global bounded media age must be on (the queue it sizes does not exist otherwise). |
| Relay sustained drain - Experimental (`test_relay_drain_sustained=true`) [relay_drain_sustained] | Off | The time-based relay queue's drain rate for a child is the relay's own sustained delivery to it, instead of only the child's arrival-train report. The train report needs one large record to cross the link as a back-to-back train (at least 8 000 bytes over at least 60 ms); on a slow GATT hop (about 100-270 kbps) records are paced by the connection events and never form one, so the child reports 0 kbps and the queue had no drain, never saw an overload and stayed at the fixed budget. The relay counts the wire bytes its writer hands the link over the seconds media waited for it (queue non-empty before and after the write, the pacer holding the writer for at most a quarter of the cycle, the first 1.5 s of each run discarded because the stack accepts tens of kB ahead of the radio, at least 3 s of qualifying time inside a 10 s window); the estimate is kept 30 s after the queue clears and then dropped, and an idle or brief-burst queue produces none. The train report stays the fallback when no sustained estimate exists, and with the switch off it is the only estimator (the control); the sustained value is then still measured and logged, never used. Why: without a drain rate the Q31 arms are inert (overload is never true). Tradeoff: the first seconds of a backlog can read high (stack buffering), and a writer that the pacer holds more than a quarter of the time yields no estimate. While the Experimental lead cap holds the writer (the child reports late) and media still waits, the last estimate is kept instead of expiring after 30 s, so the time queue no longer falls back to the fixed budget exactly while the child is late (B3); the capped cycles themselves measure nothing. Hop-local (this relay's writes to its direct child), no wire change; it does not feed the link pacer.  **Requires / masked by:** `relay_time_queue` (the estimate feeds only the time queue), and below it relay-child shedding or bounded media age. |
| Viewer time-based playout target - Experimental (`test_playout_time_target=true`; shares `test_time_queue_budget_ms`) [playout_time_target] | Off | A stream viewer's playout measures how much later than the luckiest arrival of the last minute each arrival came, relative to the media's own timestamps, and grows its delay in one step to the 99th percentile of that spread plus 30 ms, up to the latency budget (default 1 500 ms), before a frame is late; calm shrinking stops at that target. The earlier rule (grow by the late frame's lateness plus 50 ms, shrink after a calm minute) still applies and still reaches the profile's maximum, so the arm only adds advance growth and a shrink floor. Why: with a repeating jitter the earlier rule forgets the burst after a calm minute, shrinks, and is late again every cycle (the JVM model shows this; a phone has not). Shape follows WebRTC NetEq's delay manager (a high quantile of relative arrival delay sets the target level). Tradeoff: it holds more delay while the jitter lasts, and a join backlog in the window can raise it for a minute. Streams only; calls and audio-only live keep their own profiles. `test_time_queue_budget_ms` still sets both its budget and the relay time-based queue's; `test_playout_time_budget_ms` (benchmark tunable `playout_time_budget_ms`, 300-5 000 ms, masked while this arm is off) overrides the viewer's alone, so an A/B can vary the two separately (DECISIONS "Known interactions", B12). Local to the viewer, no wire change. Line `BENCHMARK playout_time_target` (neededMs, budgetMs, grows, with the playout and added delay) beside `media_age: role=stream_rx`. JVM-tested; not yet measured on phones. |
| Relay parent fallback on establishment failure (`test_relay_establish_fallback=false` to disable) [relay_establish_fallback] | On | Two link-establishment failures within 2 min (child: the parent never connected before the CoC accept timeout; relay: status 133/62 before hop authentication) mark that peer. On the relay side, with refusal escalation (the default) the marks escalate 30, 60, 120, then 300 s on consecutive marks without a success; the flat 5 minutes applies on the child side and when refusal escalation is off. The child then reconnects to the next-best route (another relay or the source, through normal admission) and ranks the marked parent last; the relay stops connecting to the marked child. Why: in a four-phone mesh A/B (2026-10-10) the M31 relay's HCI snoop showed ten LE connections to its child, each ending 0x3E after 6 events. Line: `BENCHMARK relay_parent_fallback`. |
| Relay establishment budget - Experimental (`test_relay_establish_budget=true`) [relay_establish_budget] | Off | A relay's child connect (GATT video, and the GATT control connect of a CoC child) retries a fast establishment failure (GATT 133/62 within 1.5 s, the stack's report of HCI 0x3E) in place, 300-900 ms apart, while the next attempt still fits 12 s from the first connect; the stack's own 5 s create-connection timeout is retried once. An exhausted budget is ONE failure for `relay_establish_fallback` (the control arm counts one per 2-attempt cycle), the child mark escalates 30, 60, 120, then 300 s (with refusal escalation on, the default, that escalation already applies, so this switch changes only the in-place retries and the 2M -> 1M skip; the flat 300 s control exists only with refusal escalation off too) and the 2M -> 1M swap is skipped. Why: an M31 relay (peripheral to its parent, central to its child) lost 11 of 23 child connects to 0x3E; with the A15s as child it gave up after 4 attempts in 3.5 s, marked the child for 5 minutes and so refused the child's own retry although the child advertised for 15 s. With about 0.5 per attempt, 4 attempts fail 6 %, 10 attempts 0.1 %. Line: `BENCHMARK relay_child_connect: link= | attempt= | durationMs= | status= | kind=fast_establishment/target_absent/other | result=ok/retry/exhausted/legacy | delayMs= | elapsedMs=`. Untested on phones. **Requires / masked by:** it shares the escalating marks with `relay_refusal_escalation` (either turns them on; `BENCHMARK relay_recovery_config` logs `escalatingRefusalMarks`). The PHY after a failure before any link existed is one rule shared with `relay_establish_keep_2m` (RelayChildRecoveryPolicy.setupPhyAfterFailure): such a failure keeps 2M, because establishment runs on the advertising PHY; an exhausted budget now also logs that decision (`link_phy ... reason=establishment_failed | next=2M`) instead of skipping it silently, with the same PHY as before. The four combinations are not phone-tested together (DECISIONS "Known interactions", B1). |
| Relay hold in place off (`test_relay_hold_in_place=false`; DEBUG only) [relay_hold_in_place] | On (since the connection review B1, 2026-10-27; release too) | Control arm. A relay drops a sighting of a child it will not dial (marked by the establishment tracker, or inside the stall/refusal hold-off) in the discovery callback, before the presence is stopped, instead of stopping and restarting its offer advertising set and its scan for every sighting. The mark, the hold-off and what counts as a failure are unchanged; the first sighting after they end dials at once. Why: a child that keeps advertising is rediscovered about every 120 ms, so a skipped sighting spun the restart loop until Android's scan quota (5 starts per 30 s) stopped it and left the relay blind for 29 s. Lines: the existing `relay_parent_fallback ... action=skip_connect` and `relay_child_stall ... action=holdoff`, at most one per child per 5 s with `| held_in_place=true | sightings=N`; control (`=false`): the old restart per sighting, one line per sighting. Promoted because the restart loop is a P1 outage (the relay goes blind for 29 s) and holding in place changes no dial decision. **Requires / masked by:** the relay child stall guard, the establishment fallback or the stall hold-off (it only skips sightings that those mark). |
| Relay PHY guard off (`test_relay_phy_guard=false`) [relay_phy_guard] | Guard on | Control arm. On: relays, and call endpoints whose hop is a relay, never write bad-2M memory from that hop's one-PDU windows or early losses (`bad2m=not_recorded \| hop_relays=true`). A relay's one radio carries both adjacent links, so those losses are not evidence about the pair's 2M (entries written against healthy pairs made a mesh test refuse viewers). Existing "via" entries are purged once. Off restores the old recording exactly. |
| Direct-call bad-2M memory despite a helper's relay bit (`test_direct_call_hop_memory=false` is the control arm) [direct_call_hop_memory] | On | A call endpoint whose hop is known to be the call's own peer (the hop authenticated as the end-to-end peer, or this caller dialed the callee's own offer) ignores the hop's call-relay capability bit when it decides whether to remember the peer as bad on 2M after an early link loss or a 2M setup retry. Every phone with relayed calls on and its helper running signs that bit, so before this a direct call to such a phone never remembered a bad 2M pair and every later call started on 2M again. Relayed offers, relayed invitations and a callee whose hop is a relay still skip the memory (`relay_phy_guard`). Line unchanged: `adaptive_phy: link=memory ... bad2m=recorded` instead of `bad2m=not_recorded | hop_relays=true`; the switch is logged in `BENCHMARK le_link_policy` as `directCallHopMemory`. **Requires / masked by:** `relay_phy_guard` (off records every loss anyway), `adaptive_phy_memory`. |
| Relay one-PDU PHY move (`test_relay_one_pdu_move=true` to enable) [relay_one_pdu_move] | Off | Experimental. In one run with the move, the ASUS relay's own receive fell from 283 to 119-210 kbps (ASUS 8.7 FPS, A15s 4.7) versus re-anchor alone (ASUS 15.0, A15s 5.4); one run each, A/B pending. Under the relay PHY guard, a relay's child link measured at 0.5-1.25 PDUs per event on 2M still moves to 1M (no bad-2M memory is recorded). In another run ASUS relay -> A15s carried 121 kbps against 131 kbps for one PDU per 15 ms event and the guard kept it on 2M (A15s 1.0 FPS). The collision band (4-37 kbps) still stays on 2M. **Requires / masked by:** the relay PHY guard and `adaptive_phy`. |
| Relay child-link re-anchor, earlier arm (`test_relay_reanchor=true`) [relay_reanchor] | Off (Experimental) | The child-link re-anchor of the adaptive PHY (`RelayReanchor`). Not proven: in relaylink-arms3 it never fired before the relay's stall release reconnected. **Requires / masked by:** masked by `relay_child_reanchor` (default on), which closes the starved child link first; it is also the only arm touched by the adaptive PHY's process-wide in-progress flag. |
| Relay child-link interval (`test_forwarder_child_interval_units=<6..40>`) [forwarder_child_interval_units] | Off (DEBUG experiment) | Requests this connection interval (1.25 ms units) on a relay's child link (`LeChildInterval`), to test interleaving the relay's peripheral (parent) and central (child) connections. Unset keeps the priority-based interval. |
| Relay CoC child priority (`test_forwarder_coc_priority=balanced\|low\|dck\|high`) [forwarder_coc_priority] | Off (DEBUG experiment; unset = HIGH) | The connection priority a relay requests on its CoC child link, for the same interleaving test. |
| Relay upstream DCK (`test_relay_upstream_dck=true` to enable) [relay_upstream_dck] | Off (Experimental; promoted on 2026-10-15 per Q48, then held off the same day: the full-chain run with a child, lane 11 block E, gave parent 274 vs 389 kbps and child frozen 83% vs 67% with DCK, n=3 per arm on a 15-17% S24; earlier evidence: parent-only video A/B 105%/101%, audio relay formed 8/8 vs 5/8). Re-test with a charged S24, n>=6 per arm, before promoting | A central whose just-authenticated child can relay requests DCK (Android 14+ centrals only; 30 ms by the AOSP default `gatt_dck_priority_*` = 24 units, OEM overlays may differ) instead of HIGH on that link, on GATT after the handshake and on CoC through its control connection (`BleL2capControlConnection.requestConnectionPriority`); the relay's own HIGH child link then has two events per parent event, so one of them always lies outside any parent window shorter than 15 ms minus one child exchange. Hypothesis from the M31 snoops (every dead or starved stage had equal 15 ms intervals; the 7.5 ms stage never died), not a measured fix; an explicit `test_source_gatt_priority` still wins. Line `BENCHMARK relay_upstream_priority: role=source|relay | transport | childCanRelay | priority`. Tradeoff: the parent hop adds up to 15 ms. Promotion needs the PLAN A/B (parent interval observed at 30 ms, no 0x08, child rate at least the stream rate). |
| Inbound-child probe (`test_inbound_probe=server` or `dial`, DEBUG launch extra only) [inbound_probe] | Off (Experimental, DEBUG builds only, 2026-10-14) | Measurement 7 of the stream link role-reversal design: a phone that is LE central on its parent link under media load also hosts a connectable GATT sink (`server`), and a second phone redials it (`dial`; `test_inbound_probe_dials` 15, `_hold_ms` 20000, `_gap_ms` 2000, `_kbps` 60) to show whether each dial draws a new phase, how many dials stay alive, and whether the parent link keeps completing. It writes only `inbound_probe:` log lines: no authentication, no media, no hop records, nothing on air unless the extra is set, and release builds ignore it. Pass and fail criteria are in the design record; a fail on the parent side ends the design.  |
| Source fan-out count (`test_max_children=<1..8>`) | Off (DEBUG; unset = the bandwidth-derived count) | Overrides the number of direct children a stream source accepts (clamped to 8). Release builds ignore it. |
| Minimum route cost (`test_min_route_cost=<n>`) | 0 (DEBUG) | A DEBUG auto-selecting viewer joins only a stream offer whose route cost is at least n, to force a relayed placement. |
| Require a relay-capable offer (`test_require_relay`) | Off (DEBUG) | A DEBUG receiver or relay accepts only announcements that are relay-capable, to force a chain. |
| Manual stream relay passes the end-to-end handshake through (`test_stream_relay_passthrough`, implied by `test_require_relay`) | Off (DEBUG only) | Every stream hop requires the end-to-end peer to be the link peer (PROTOCOL Phase 1b item 4, SEC-B2). The DEBUG manual relay (`test_role=relay`) forwards the handshake, so a source or viewer behind it launches with this switch to skip that equality for a version 2 hop whose link peer signed `maxChildren` above 0 (and to accept a `BGK1` group hop); a version 5 selection is never exempt. Release builds ignore it. Not a product switch: the security fix itself has none. |
| Relay child count (`test_relay_children=<1..3>`) | 1 (DEBUG) | How many BLE children a DEBUG relay waits for before it starts; the status line reads "Next N BLE receivers". |
| Receiver packet delay (`test_receiver_delay_ms=<0..1000>`) | 0 = off (DEBUG) | The stream receiver sleeps this many milliseconds after each media packet, to emulate a slow receiver. |
| Relay chain control arm (`test_call_relay_chain=false`) [call_relay_chain] | Chains on | The DEBUG call relay may take another relay's offer as its child, so a call crosses up to four opaque relays (PROTOCOL "Private-call relay chains": hop-local admission by route cost and signed relay capacity, loop-free, endpoint-authenticated end to end; a malicious relay can drop or delay, never impersonate or read). A relayed child must sign the call-relay link capability, Classic reaches only the advertising device (no bonded fallback or inquiry), a relay waits at most 180 s for its parent, and a caller refuses offers above four relays. Off restores the one-relay rule exactly. Benchmark relays use `test_relay_callee` and `test_relay_route_cost` so each takes only its requested child's offer. Untested on phones. **Requires / masked by:** relayed calls (Settings > Experiments "Relayed calls (beta)", honoured with or without Manual video settings since 2026-11-06; benchmark launches use `test_relayed_calls`): the whole `relayed*` and call-relay-chain family is inert without it. |
| Relayed-call endpoint restart guard (`test_relayed_restart_guard=false` is the control arm) [relayed_restart_guard] | On (bug fix, 2026-11-06) | With relayed calls on, a phone rebuilds its idle call endpoint when the helper flag or consent bit on its call offer no longer matches its helper state. The endpoint counted as idle while it held an accepted incoming link that had not signalled a call yet, and the offer's bits came from a helper snapshot up to 5 s old (taken before the endpoint had a transport, or during the call that just ended), so the first helper tick about 5 s after launch, and again 1-3 s after each call, rebuilt the endpoint and closed a relay's hop (or a direct caller's link) that had just connected. On: a link that is accepted is never closed by this rebuild (the stale offer is asked again at the next tick), a new endpoint reads its offer's helper bits from the state at its creation with its own transport, and the call relay stops and leaves the air when its authenticated callee link closes before its caller joins, instead of holding the dead link for up to 180 s and joining the caller to it. Off restores the old rebuild, the cached bits and the 180 s hold exactly. Lines `BENCHMARK relayed_helper: offer_restart=deferred\|done`, `BENCHMARK call_relay_child: event=closed_before_parent`. JVM tests only; phone A/B pending. **Requires / masked by:** relayed calls (`test_relayed_calls`): inert without it, except the call relay's child watch, which applies to every DEBUG call relay. |
| Measured relayed call cap (`test_relayed_call_measured_cap`) [relayed_call_measured_cap] | Off | A relayed call's video bitrate cap becomes this phone's fresh measured link x 0.2125 less call audio (a relay's one radio carries both adjacent links in both directions, half the direct 0.425 share), never below the legacy 110 kbps; FPS stays at most 15. The legacy 15 FPS / 110 kbps cap has no measured basis as a ceiling (it was a starting budget), while a one-way stream relay carried 433-458 kbps in and out. Example: a 900 kbps link gives 159 kbps. Line `BENCHMARK call relayed_cap`. Needs a relayed A/B before promotion. Untested on phones. **Requires / masked by:** relayed calls (Settings > Experiments "Relayed calls (beta)", honoured with or without Manual video settings since 2026-11-06; benchmark launches use `test_relayed_calls`): the whole `relayed*` and call-relay-chain family is inert without it. |
| Relayed first hop as a direct link (`test_relayed_first_hop_direct=false` is the control arm) [relayed_first_hop_direct] | On | Before SETUP, the caller's link to the helper it selected for an on-demand relayed route is an ordinary link to an idle phone, so it gets the direct-call 2M protections for that helper: the helper's bad-2M memory is read at the dial and written at a 2M setup stall (under the helper's recognised contact node, as for a rotating direct offer), and the 1M retry rescans for the helper's own current offer (same helper ID; the hop opening still proves the selected helper, so another relay is never swapped in) instead of redialing the old address. Reason: an A15s caller via an ASUS helper failed 4 of 4 (CoC and GATT): the first hop stalled on 2M, the helper re-advertised under a new address after its dead link timed out, and the 1M retry redialed the old address until "BLE control did not respond" / "Timed out connecting GATT video". Lines `relayed_route`, `adaptive_phy: link=call ... hop=helper:...`. **Requires / masked by:** relayed calls (Settings > Experiments "Relayed calls (beta)", honoured with or without Manual video settings since 2026-11-06; benchmark launches use `test_relayed_calls`): the whole `relayed*` and call-relay-chain family is inert without it. |
| Helper next-hop connect retry (`test_relayed_hop_retry=false` is the control arm) [relayed_hop_retry] | On | A relayed-call helper whose connect to its next hop fails within 2 s (no link formed) repeats that same connect once after 0.5 s, logged (`relayed_hop: ... event=connect_retry`), while at least 6 s of the SETUP deadline is left. A slow failure (a link that formed and then died) is not repeated. Reason: an M31 helper's CoC connect on its fresh ACL failed after 341 ms and the call failed with 14 s of the deadline unused. **Requires / masked by:** relayed calls (Settings > Experiments "Relayed calls (beta)", honoured with or without Manual video settings since 2026-11-06; benchmark launches use `test_relayed_calls`): the whole `relayed*` and call-relay-chain family is inert without it. |
| Helper next hop on LE 1M - Experimental (`test_relayed_hop_1m=true`) [relayed_hop_1m] | Off | A relayed-call helper opens its next hop on LE 1M instead of the 2M default; the difference is logged as `link_phy requested=2M effective=1M reason=relayed_hop_1m`. Hypothesis under test: the A15s -> ASUS child hop (A15s helper, ASUS callee) goes silent right after the 2M PHY update, the same signature as the direct A15s -> ASUS 2M setup stall; A15s helpers reached an M31 callee. The helper cannot use the direct calls' stall-and-retry because the ASUS keeps the dead link for 7-9 s, longer than the SETUP deadline leaves. A/B on m31 -> a15s -> asus (CoC, build b5bf8aad): off 0 of 4 set up ("read failed", ASUS EOFException), on 3 of 4. It stays the forcing switch; the measured per-link fallback below is the default. **Requires / masked by:** relayed calls (Settings > Experiments "Relayed calls (beta)", honoured with or without Manual video settings since 2026-11-06; benchmark launches use `test_relayed_calls`): the whole `relayed*` and call-relay-chain family is inert without it. |
| DEBUG static relay child hop gets the on-demand 2M guard (`test_relay_start_hop_guard`) [relay_start_hop_guard] | Off (Experimental, 2026-11-07) | The DEBUG private-call relay shape (`test_private_call_relay=true`, used by the benchmark's static relay cases) connects and authenticates its LE child hop with no stall guard and no retry today. With this switch on, a first 2M child hop gets the same 3 s hop-authentication guard and one 1M retry as an on-demand helper hop, logs `link_phy ... reason=setup_retry source=adaptive link=call-relay-start`, and records the authenticated peer bad on 2M after a stalled attempt. A connect failure is retried once on 1M but records nothing. No rescan in this DEBUG shape (the same selected address is retried after 500 ms). Production relayed calls do not use this shape: they use the on-demand path (`startOnDemand` -> `openNextHop`), which already has the guard, retry and memory; the mid-call hop-loss suppression is unchanged. Off is exactly today's static path. No wire change. Untested on phones. **Requires / masked by:** only the DEBUG static relay shape (`test_private_call_relay=true`); production relayed calls already use the guarded on-demand path. |
| Helper next hop: per-link 2M -> 1M retry (`test_relayed_hop_phy_retry=false` is the control arm) [relayed_hop_phy_retry] | On | A relayed-call helper's next hop gets the call's measured 2M protection, per link and not per device family: a first attempt on 2M whose connect fails or whose hop authentication has not finished 3 s after the link opened is closed and retried once on 1M (to the next hop's freshest advert in the helper's reach table, waited for up to 5 s) while 6 s of the SETUP deadline is left; a 1M retry that authenticates records that hop peer bad on 2M (`adaptive_phy ... reason=relay_hop_setup_retry`), so the next dial of the pair starts on 1M. Only a pair that failed on 2M and then set up on 1M inside one attempt is recorded, which separates it from the relay's dual-role scheduling losses the relay PHY guard keeps out of the memory. Lines `adaptive_phy: link=call-relay ... from=2M to=1M`, `relayed_hop: event=rediscover`, `link_phy requested=2M effective=1M reason=setup_retry`. Evidence for default on: the `relayed_hop_1m` A/B (0 of 4 on 2M, 3 of 4 on 1M) and the same 2M -> 1M setup retry that direct calls already use. Stream chain hops are not changed: the two failing chain runs show a harness raw-input failure and a mid-stream stall, not a 2M setup stall. **Requires / masked by:** relayed calls (Settings > Experiments "Relayed calls (beta)", honoured with or without Manual video settings since 2026-11-06; benchmark launches use `test_relayed_calls`): the whole `relayed*` and call-relay-chain family is inert without it. |
