| Relayed-call helper and next-hop pins (`test_relayed_calls_helper=<12 hex>` on the caller, `test_relayed_calls_next_hop=<12 hex>` on a helper) [relayed_calls_helper_pin] | Off (DEBUG lab control) | The caller uses only a reach-table route whose first relay has the pinned signed-node prefix; a missing pinned route is the ordinary "no relay route" refusal, never another helper or a direct fallback. A helper accepts only the pinned next relay, or the pinned target prefix when it can reach the target directly; anything else is the existing NO_ROUTE FAIL and never a route search. The pins only narrow routes the app would already accept (the reach table's authenticated signed prefixes and the helpers' admission are unchanged); they add no route and skip no authentication. Exactly 12 hex digits; an absent extra is off, and a malformed value matches no route. A DEBUG build reads them in every launch, a person's launch (`test_user_launch=true`) included (test geometry, like the Wi-Fi extras); release builds ignore both extras. No wire change. JVM tests only; phone A/B pending. **Requires / masked by:** relayed calls (`test_relayed_calls`); only the reach-table caller route choice and the SETUP helper admission are pinned (the Design OR on-demand search prototype is separate and is not pinned). |
| Relayed-call candidate iteration (`test_relayed_candidates=false` is the control arm) [relayed_candidates] | On | A relayed call whose route fails before READY (first-hop open or handshake, setup stall, a helper's FAIL, the helper's pre-setup timeout) tries the reach table's next-best route through a helper this attempt has not tried, inside the same call attempt: at most 3 routes, each started while a full 20 s SETUP deadline still fits in 60 s, the exact relay count under `require`, every hop authenticated and checked as before (selected-helper opening, consent and the helpers' admission unchanged). A failure after READY, a cancelled call or an answered one never moves to another route. Line `relayed_route: role=caller \| attempt=N \| helper=...`. Design C listed this as deferred; reason: one bad pair (A15s -> ASUS on 2M) failed the whole call while another helper was in range. **Requires / masked by:** relayed calls (Settings > Experiments "Relayed calls (beta)", honoured with or without Manual video settings since 2026-11-06; benchmark launches use `test_relayed_calls`): the whole `relayed*` and call-relay-chain family is inert without it. |
| MediaTek helpers over LE CoC only (`test_device_policy_off=mediatek-call-relay-gatt-helper-off` turns it off) | On for MediaTek phones (2026-10-27, build 4) | A DeviceFamilyPolicy entry: a MediaTek phone (SoC maker MediaTek on Android 12+, else a `mt<digits>` hardware name) whose call endpoint is GATT does not help with other people's calls: its offer carries no helper flag, its link hello signs no relay mark, and a SETUP or route search arriving over GATT is refused, so callers pick another helper. Over LE CoC it helps as before, and its own GATT calls are unchanged. Settings shows "Helping: paused (CoC only on this phone)". Reason: as a GATT middle the A15s (MT6765, Android 10) held 0 of 8 relayed calls in every arm (control, `test_gatt_byte_window=3072` with 120 kbps, `test_gatt_payload=244`), with single bytes changed inside queued notifications at the next phone's HCI log; never on single-link calls (228 A15s direct GATT calls, 0 desyncs); the Xiaomi Redmi 12 (also MediaTek) 3 of 10 as GATT middle; non-MediaTek middles 0 of 72; the A15s as a CoC middle held 7 of 8. Low confidence: two local phones and no MediaTek document. The protocol fix (per-chunk integrity with a GR1 re-request) is in PLAN. **Requires / masked by:** relayed calls (the whole helper family is inert without it). |
| Relay join keyframe (`test_relay_join_key_frame`) [relay_join_key_frame] | Off (Experimental) | A source honours a keyframe request from a relay-capable direct child (one that may have children) at once, bypassing the shared recovery back-off (1 s doubling to 8 s), when that child sent no request for 2 s (`RelayJoinKeyFrame`); the window starts at the child's authentication, whose keyframe is already forced. At most one forced keyframe per relay child per 2 s; a child that keeps asking more often never forces one; plain viewers are unchanged. Reason (2026-10-26, saved runs): authenticated-to-stream-header was a median 402 ms on direct joins but 1127 ms through one relay (p90 3484 ms) and 2248 ms through two, because the forwarder's regenerated request for its new child looks like a loss recovery to the source. No wire change (DECISIONS "Design JK" is the tagged alternative). A/B: a chain join (source, forwarder, child), child selection to first surface and authenticated to header; source keyframe count per minute. Lines `relay_join_key_frame: childRelays= | forced=`, `key_frame_request: honored=`, `relay_child_join: event=first_media | waitMs=`. |

## Calls and intercom

| Switch | Default | What it does and why it is kept |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Windows: call audio coalescing (`MESHLIVE_CALL_AUDIO_COALESCE=1`) | Off (Experimental) | Android's call audio coalescing rule (`test_call_audio_coalesce`, on in Android and Apple): ordinary Opus units already queued behind the one being written go out as one `BTA1`/`BTA2` record (2-20 units within 1 s). No added delay, no wire change; receivers accept both forms. Off until a Windows <-> Android call with it on is measured. |
| Voice calls (formerly Settings > Features "Voice calls"; DEBUG `test_voice_calls` is the benchmark switch) | On since 2026-10-17, no Settings switch (design decision; a constant with no stored choice). One constant, `CallFeatureDefaults.VOICE_CALLS` (true) | Intercom pillar (2026-10-09). Tapping a person offers Voice call and Video call. A voice call starts no camera, encoder or decoder and sends no video packets; it starts on the earpiece (video calls start on the loudspeaker; the Speaker/Earpiece button overrides, and until you press it the route follows the layout) and shows the peer, a timer, mute, speaker/earpiece and End. Either person can add or remove their own video during the call; adding video widens the foreground-service type to camera before the camera starts, and removing it narrows it again. It uses no new wire value (PROTOCOL "Voice calls"). A peer without it (an older build, or the switch off) sees a video call with the caller's video off and answers with its own camera; the caller then sees one notice that the other app has no voice calls. Changing a call or Messages switch rebuilds an idle call listener (about 1 s) so the next call, in either direction, follows it. Benchmark launches (any `test_` extra) read only the extra. DEBUG controls `invite_voice` / `voice_call:PREFIX`; lines `BENCHMARK call_type`, `call_type offer`, `call_mode`, `call_audio_route`. Not yet tested on phones. |
| Push-to-talk button, PTT mode (`test_push_to_talk`, `test_ptt_mode`; launch extras only, never a setting: push-to-talk is not a mode) | Off | Hold Mute to talk is part of every call and has no switch: while muted, press and hold Mute (350 ms) to talk and let go to mute again, on the existing mute path with haptic feedback. Screen readers get a "Talk" custom action that latches talking on and off, and the Mute button's description explains it. A cellular call that holds the microphone (audio focus) keeps the call muted, and the screen says so. The extras add a hold button with tone and colour cues; PTT mode starts calls muted. DEBUG controls `ptt_press` / `ptt_release`; line `BENCHMARK call_ptt`. Group push-to-talk is not built. The stored `experimental_push_to_talk` and `experimental_ptt_mode` preferences have no reader outside tests (**Not wired**). Not yet tested on phones. |
| Call FPS ladder from 0.4 x request (`test_call_ladder_from_request`) [call_ladder_from_request] | Off | The call frame-rate floor used before 2026-10-09. The default floor is 0.1 bit/pixel of the encoded geometry. **Requires / masked by:** masked by the measured Auto ladder (`test_auto_ladder`) when that is on. |
| Slow 2M hop authentication counts as a setup stall (`test_stall_slow_auth_ms`, 0 = off) [stall_slow_auth_ms] | Off (Experimental, 2026-11-07) | On a first 2M LE call attempt that still has the 1M retry, a hop authentication that finished at or after this many ms after the link opened is treated as a setup stall: the caller closes the link, takes the existing one 1M retry, rediscovers a direct callee, and records the peer bad on 2M exactly as a stalled setup does. Only the guarded first 2M attempt passes a non-zero arm; the 1M retry and the `test_phy_setup_stall=false` control arm never take it. The value is clamped to the existing 3 s setup guard, which always wins first at or above it. The throw happens after hop authentication and before any media, relay SETUP or end-to-end record; the retry re-authenticates from scratch, so authentication and encryption are not weakened. Reason: 4 of 24 A15s-dialed first attempts finished hop auth at 789-2359 ms on 2M, released the 3 s guard and died later (answer deadline or a write timeout after ACTIVE). Untested on phones. **Requires / masked by:** `test_phy_setup_stall` (default on; off makes it inert because `stallGuardMs` is 0) and `test_adaptive_phy` (default on). |
| Call 2M setup-stall guard off (`test_phy_setup_stall=false`) [phy_setup_stall] | Guard on | Control arm. On: the first 2M LE call attempt that may still retry on 1M closes its link when hop authentication has not finished 3 s after it opened (`BENCHMARK adaptive_phy: ... reason=setup_stall \| action=close`), and the 1M retry of a direct offer rescans up to 8 s for the callee's current advertisement by node prefix and route cost (the end-to-end handshake verifies the identity); a relayed offer keeps its original relay so the requested parent never changes. The authenticated 1M retry logs `BENCHMARK link_phy requested=2M effective=1M reason=setup_stall\|setup_retry source=adaptive` and records bad-2M memory except for a relaying hop. Every LE call attempt logs `BENCHMARK call_setup: ... hopAuthMs=`. Why: A15s central -> ASUS on 2M delivered nothing ASUS -> A15s after PHY Update Complete, and the old retry reconnected to the ASUS's stale random address; 1M went active in 2.5 s. 353 saved outgoing GATT setups authenticated within 1.05 s on healthy pairs. Off restores the retry only after the link dies, to the old address. |
| Private-call close waits for the peer (`test_call_close_wait_peer=false` is the fixed-400 ms control arm) | On (socket transports) | After HANG_UP/DECLINE/CANCEL the writer waits until the peer closes the link (its reader ends), capped at 3 s, instead of closing after 400 ms; the fixed drain lost the hang-up and about 1.3 s of media behind a 542 ms CoC queue. Line `BENCHMARK call close_drain`. Untested on phones. |
| Call audio headroom off (`test_call_audio_headroom=false`) [call_audio_headroom] | Headroom on | Control arm. The call send queue was 33 records for 500 ms of audio plus video. During one long GATT video write, audio alone filled it, so every queued video frame was dropped and a keyframe was forced; the keyframe's own write repeated the cycle (104 recoveries in 46 no-loss calls). Audio now has its own 3 s (150-packet) room and video counts only video. Untested on phones. **Requires / masked by:** a no-op under `bounded_media_age`. |
| Call codec fallback off (`test_call_codec_fallback=false`) [call_codec_fallback] | Fallback on | Control arm. A legacy Qualcomm phone (API 32 or older) used to advertise VP9-only call decoding. Its end-to-end hello now lists its real decoders plus a signed "prefers VP9" bit (decode-mask bit 7). Senders keep VP9 whenever they can encode it, so VP9-capable peers are unchanged, and AV1-only phones (A15s, Xiaomi) send AV1 instead of failing with "Peer cannot decode any locally available call codec". The decision uses only the end-to-end hello, which relays copy opaquely, so direct and relayed calls agree. The Android CDD requires VP9 decoding, not VP9 encoding, on handhelds. The ASUS decoded AV1 640x480 with 0 freezes in 12 stream cases. Direct A15s <-> ASUS calls worked with the first version; this end-to-end form is not yet tested on phones. |
| Call video fragments (`test_call_video_fragments`) [call_video_fragments] | On for direct BLE GATT calls in user launches (promoted 2026-10-11) | Direct calls with the bit in both signed endpoint hellos. Promoted with speech-led playout for GATT (DECISIONS "Call head-of-line A/B": GATT audio queue age p95 -38..-69%, about 4% framing overhead; CoC/Classic unchanged; benchmark launches keep the extras-only arms). Video frames over 3 KB travel as authenticated 2 KB fragments, and queued call audio is written between them. GATT audio waited up to 1.19 s behind a frame. PROTOCOL "Experimental call video fragments". Phone-measured in the call head-of-line A/B (the numbers above). **Requires / masked by:** a direct call and the fragments bit in both signed endpoint hellos; a relayed call, or a peer without the bit, negotiates no fragments. A call link renewal drops a frame its old link cut and the new link starts at a whole frame (fixed 2026-10-27: the partial frame used to end the call as "Call link lost" right after a successful renewal; not yet observed on phones). |
| Airtime-sized call fragments (`test_call_video_fragments` + `test_call_fragment_airtime_ms=40`) [call_fragment_airtime_ms] | Off (0) | Fourth arm of the call head-of-line A/B. Instead of a fixed 2 KB, each fragment carries link rate x target airtime (40 ms) payload bytes, clamped to 675-4096 bytes, so call audio waits about one short write at any link rate; a fixed 2 KB is still 80-170 ms of a slow GATT link. The rate is this call link's peak delivery goodput (last 10 s, from the peer's version 3 reports, which count bytes and survive small fragments), else the peer's arrival-train estimate, else the learned probe (which may only shrink fragments); unknown keeps 2 KB. Each fragment adds 75 bytes of framing and tags (hop record 33, endpoint record 34, fragment header 8), so the 675-byte minimum holds that at or below 10% (2 KB costs 3.5%, 4 KB 1.8%); a very slow link gets fragments longer than the target rather than more overhead. Queued audio is written before every fragment, so none starts while audio waits. Frames up to 1.5 x the chunk go whole. No wire change (the receiver accepts any chunk length). Needs `test_call_video_fragments`; 0 or absent is today's fixed size. One `BENCHMARK call_fragment_airtime` line per second (both fragment arms) with fragment sizes, fragments/s, overhead bytes and the longest audio wait behind video. PROTOCOL "Experimental call video fragments". Not yet tested on phones. **Requires / masked by:** masked by the fragments negotiation: with no fragments negotiated (the peer lacks the bit, or a relayed call) the size is never used. |
| Speech-led call playout (`test_call_speech_led_playout`) [call_speech_led_playout] | On for direct BLE GATT calls in user launches (promoted 2026-10-11) | Late call video may grow the shared call delay by at most 150 ms; late audio keeps the full 250-600 ms range. Later video is shown late (nothing is dropped) and is still reported to the sender's rate control. Every GATT call pinned the shared delay at 600 ms. Pair it with fragments, or audio itself still arrives late on GATT. Phone-measured only together with fragments (call head-of-line A/B: playout delay p50 about 100 ms lower on GATT); speech-led alone is unmeasured. It is applied to every direct GATT user call, also one where no fragments were negotiated (the peer lacks the bit, or the call is relayed): that combination is unmeasured. |
| Legacy call low delay (`test_call_legacy_low_delay`) [call_legacy_low_delay] | Off | Direct calls on a legacy-constrained route (a legacy Qualcomm phone, or a peer declaring a constrained CoC/Classic limit) send audio in 40 ms pairs instead of 400 ms batches and play with the normal call delay instead of 500-800 ms. Measured protection cost: about 0.9-1.2 s one way on M31/ASUS CoC, including voice calls. The batch's own evidence was a relayed route, where pairs sufficed. Relayed calls keep the batch. Untested on phones. |
| Call audio coalescing (`test_call_audio_coalesce=false` is the control arm) [call_audio_coalesce] | On (promoted 2026-10-27, build 4) | Overhead audit. When the call writer takes one ordinary 20 ms Opus unit while more ordinary units already wait in its queue (audio that queued during a blocking video write on a slow link), it sends them as one existing `BTA1` pair or `BTA2` batch record (at most 20 units, 1 s span; never codec configuration or an already-combined record). Nothing waits for audio not yet captured, so the added delay is zero, and with an empty queue every record is exactly as before. No wire change: every Android, iOS and Windows call receiver already splits pairs and batches (legacy routes send them today). Basis (computed from the record formats): each merged unit saves the 80 bytes of per-record framing (`BMS1` 17 + 16, `BME1` 18 + 16, packet 13) minus a 6-byte batch item header, plus one ATT value or CoC SDU and two AES-GCM operations; for example 8 units queued behind one video write at 131 kbps (15 ms interval, one PDU per event) go as one 773-byte record instead of eight records of 1,280 bytes in total. The saving appears only under backlog, which is when it matters. `BENCHMARK call tx` adds `coalescedAudioRecords` and `coalescedAudioUnits`; `audioWrites` then counts records, not units. It does nothing when audio pairs are on (a pair record is not a plain audio unit) and on relayed or legacy routes, which already pair or batch. Measured (direct GATT video calls, 25 s, ABBA x3): M31 <-> S22 control 747 sender queue drops and call audio queue age up to 3.7 s, coalescing 0 drops and at most 20 ms; ASUS <-> A15s video 13.3 -> 15.0 fps, drops 136 -> 102 (DECISIONS "Call audio coalescing promoted"). |
| Call audio pairs (`test_call_audio_pairs`) [call_audio_pairs] | Off (Experimental, 2026-10-26) | Overhead audit. Routes that send single 20 ms units (every direct call that is not legacy-constrained, and relayed GATT and Classic calls) send 40 ms `BTA1` pairs instead; routes that already pair or batch are unchanged. Cost: every other unit waits 20 ms for its partner (+10 ms mean one-way delay). Basis (computed, 80-byte Opus units at 32 kbps): call audio drops from 64 to about 50 kbps of records (-34 bytes per unit) and from 50 to 25 records per second, so half the AES-GCM operations on both layers. A pair of 80-byte units (264-byte L2CAP PDU) still needs two 251-octet LE data PDUs, so on a link that moves one data PDU per connection event the event count does not fall; that needs units of 73 bytes or less. No wire change (same receivers as coalescing). Untested on phones. |
| AEAD cipher reuse (`test_aead_cipher_reuse`) [aead_cipher_reuse] | Off (Experimental, 2026-10-26) | Overhead audit, CPU and one write per record. Hop (`BMS1`) and endpoint (`BME1`) record writers, readers, sealers and openers keep one AES-256-GCM `Cipher` for their record stream instead of a security-provider lookup for every record (about four per media record on a call endpoint: hop and endpoint, seal and open), seal the header and ciphertext into one array, and open endpoint records in place instead of copying header and body out first. A hop record then reaches the link stream as one write: on LE CoC (4 KiB send buffer) a record of 4 KiB or more no longer sends its 17-byte header as a separate SDU. Records are byte-for-byte identical; each record re-initialises the cipher with its own key and nonce, and the cipher is dropped with the key on close. Basis: host JVM 1.45 to 0.29 microseconds per 160-byte record and 2.08 to 0.95 per 2,600-byte record (Android Conscrypt cost unmeasured). Untested on phones. |
| Stream audio batches (`test_stream_audio_batch_frames=N`) [stream_audio_batch_frames] | 2 = 40 ms pairs (Experimental, 2026-10-26) | Overhead audit. A video stream source sends its 20 ms Opus units to every child as one existing `BTA2` batch record of N = 3..10 units (60-200 ms) instead of 40 ms `BTA1` pairs; other values keep the pairs. Every stream receiver and relay already splits batches (Android, iOS, Windows; audio-only live streams send 200 ms batches today), so there is no wire change. Cost: the newest unit of a batch leaves up to (N - 1) x 20 ms later; a stream viewer already plays several hundred milliseconds behind its source. A timestamp jump or rewind starts a new batch, and pausing or closing sends what waits. Basis (computed from the record formats, 80-byte units): a mesh record costs 130 bytes of framing (`BMS1` 33, `BMV1` 50, `BME1` 34, packet 13), a direct record 80; at N = 5 audio drops from 25 to 10 records per second per child, saving about 15 kbps per mesh hop link (9 kbps direct), at N = 10 about 21 kbps (13 kbps direct), and 60-80% of the per-child audio seals, envelope encodes and allocations at the source and every forwarder. Untested on phones. |
| Legacy call relief (`test_call_legacy_relief`) [call_legacy_relief] | Off | No legacy Qualcomm (API 32 or older) call protection applies on this phone: no 426x240 cap, no 110-160 kbps limits, no VP9-only rules or forced software VP9 decoder, Opus 32, no batching or 40 ms flush, normal playout. The phone also advertises its normal receive limits. Its one switch is `privateCallStackFingerprint()`. Relay and stream rules are unchanged. Untested on phones. |
| Call over the lock screen (formerly Settings > Features "Calls on the lock screen"; DEBUG `test_call_lock_screen` is the benchmark switch) [call_lock_screen] | On since 2026-10-17, no Settings switch (design decision; a constant with no stored choice). One constant, `CallFeatureDefaults.LOCK_SCREEN_CALLS` (true) | While a call rings or runs, the call screen may show over the keyguard, and an incoming call turns the screen on. An incoming call also posts a CATEGORY_CALL notification on an IMPORTANCE_HIGH channel with a full-screen intent, which is Android's documented path for incoming calls on a locked device. Before, a locked or backgrounded phone only rang under a low-importance service notice. On Android 13+ the notification permission is part of call readiness (see "Call readiness permissions"). On Android 14+ the full-screen special access is separate: while lock-screen calls are on (the default) and Android refuses it, Settings > Features shows "Android needs your permission..." with a button to the system page (`Settings.ACTION_MANAGE_APP_USE_FULL_SCREEN_INTENT`); without it a heads-up is shown. Names : over a secure lock screen the call screen shows "Encrypted call" and "Unlock to see who is calling" until the phone is unlocked (ACTION_USER_PRESENT), unless the lock-screen names option below is on. It applies to the next call. Not yet tested on phones. |
| First call frame on arrival (`test_call_first_frame_now`) [call_first_frame_now] | Off | The first decoded frame of the peer's call video is shown at once instead of after its 250-500 ms playout target; later frames keep the shared timeline. The aim is a shorter first-picture time (median 2.9-3.2 s from setup). Untested on phones. |
| First call frame with a playout ramp (`test_call_first_frame_ramp`) [call_first_frame_ramp] | On (promoted 2026-11-09; false is the control arm) | The first decoded frame of the peer's call video is shown at once, as with `test_call_first_frame_now`; the following frames then play slightly slowed (at least 67% speed) so the playout delay grows smoothly from about zero to the normal 250-500 ms target over about 2 s (longer for a lead above about 0.67 s; the shared timeline takes over after at most 4 s). Reason: the first-frame-now arm cut the time from decoder ready to first picture from 0.6-0.8 s to under 0.1 s, but every later frame kept its playout target, so the picture froze for the whole lead (about one early freeze per call). No frame is dropped; frames are only held for less time than without the ramp; audio and the shared playout clock are unchanged (lip sync converges as the ramp ends). Takes precedence over `test_call_first_frame_now`. Lines `BENCHMARK call_first_frame_ramp: phase=start \| leadMs= \| factor= \| rampMs=`, `phase=end \| reason=caught_up\|timeout \| elapsedMs= \| handoverRenderMs= \| frames=` or `phase=skipped \| reason=no_lead`. Phone A/B against `test_call_first_frame_now` and off: CoC and GATT calls, metric = decoder-configured -> first surface and setup -> first surface; guard = freezes in the first 5 s and frame timing. Measured off vs on (60 s calls, n=5-6 per arm and direction): setup to first picture -0.9 to -1.1 s on BLE CoC, -0.2 to -0.6 s on GATT, zero early freezes in every arm, every ramp caught up in about 2 s, worst start lead 161 ms (DECISIONS "First call frame with a playout ramp promoted"). **Requires / masked by:** private calls only. |
| Call pre-warm while ringing (`test_call_prewarm`) [call_prewarm] | Off | While a video call rings (the caller waiting for Accept, the callee while its ring shows, which is after the block check), each phone prepares its video encoder for the profile media start would use: the codec is created, configured and started and the camera metadata is read, but no camera device opens and no capture starts. When the peer's call header names its codec and size (sent at the peer's Accept, ahead of its camera), the decoder component is allocated off the receive thread and the first key frame configures it. Camera and microphone still start only after Accept and the media decision, exactly as without the switch; the audio route is not touched while the ringtone plays. A profile that changed before media start, a voice answer, decline, cancel, ring timeout, failure, the end of the call or 60 s without a media start releases what was prepared and the call builds its encoder as today. Reason: user-launch calls held but dial to first picture took 3.1-8.9 s against the 3.0 s gate; after Accept each phone cold-started camera and encoder (0.8-2.1 s to the first encoded frame) while the link sat ready. Lines `BENCHMARK call_prewarm: event=prepare\|ready\|adopted\|released\|skipped \| ...` and `BENCHMARK call_prewarm_decoder: event=allocated\|used\|unused`; the join stages are `BENCHMARK call_join_stage:` (DEBUG, always on). Untested on phones: A/B pending (S22 hardware HEVC with ASUS on GATT, user launch, 12 calls per arm in random order, moving scene). **Requires / masked by:** private video calls only. |
| Camera-first call media start (DEBUG `test_call_camera_first=false` is the control arm) [call_camera_first] | On (promoted 2026-11-12 on mechanism; release builds too) | After Accept and the block check (the same gate as without the switch: nothing starts earlier, and a blocked caller opens no camera, route or microphone) the call starts its camera before its audio route: foreground service, camera flags, preview, camera, then the audio session, then the microphone. The audio route open (`call_audio_open`: focus, communication mode, route) is queued on the audio command thread instead of running inline on the UI thread, the microphone start is always queued behind it (even with `test_audio_off_main` off) so the capture still opens in the voice-communication mode (platform echo cancellation), and the remote audio playback creates its AudioTrack only after the open (bounded wait 1.5 s). The camera opens while its viewfinder surface is requested; the first capture session is configured when both are ready, instead of requesting the surface first and opening the camera when it arrived. Requested and effective camera, encoder, bitrate, FPS and audio settings are unchanged; a voice call has no camera step. Reason: after Accept the call opened its audio route inline on the UI thread (80-600 ms measured, M31 caller 289-599 ms) before it asked for the camera, so the camera chain (0.5-1.3 s) started late; expected 0.15-0.35 s (Realme) and 0.3-0.65 s (M31) off the time from Accept to the first picture. DEBUG lines (always on in debug builds, none in release): `BENCHMARK call_join_stage: stage=tap\|camera_open_requested\|preview_surface_ready\|camera_opened\|camera_session_configured\|camera_first_frame` (the last with sensorLagMs, aeState, exposureUs, iso) and one `BENCHMARK camera_caps:` per call. Untested on phones: A/B pending (Realme and M31, off / camera first / camera first with `call_prewarm`, user launch, lit moving scene, 12 calls per arm and direction in random order; preservation S22 with A15s, 6 per arm). Measured with the other two call-start switches (DECISIONS "Faster call start": S22 <-> ASUS, 12 calls per arm in random order, joins 0.9-1.2 s sooner from Accept; also ASUS <-> A15s and S22 <-> Realme) and promoted together on 2026-11-12 (design decision), before the idle-advert battery number (first-principles +0.1-0.3 mA; the advert switch goes back off if it measures above +0.5 mA). **Requires / masked by:** private calls; the camera step needs a video call; the parallel open applies to live Camera2 input, not to a raw fixture or the GPU path. |
| Voice instead of a refused camera (`test_call_voice_when_camera_refused`) [call_voice_when_camera_refused] | On (design decision 2026-11-10; false is the control arm) | A video call answered or placed while only camera access is refused (the microphone is allowed) goes on as a voice call. Answering uses the existing camera-off answer, the one used at critical heat, which every peer accepts; a placed call is placed as a voice call when voice calls are on. A notice says "Answered with voice: camera access is off" or "Calling with voice: camera access is off". Control arm (off): the call does not go on and a message names what is missing. In both arms, every other refused camera or microphone request for a call (a refused microphone, turning the camera on in a call) shows that message, with "Open settings" when Android no longer asks; before, the tap was dropped and an incoming call kept ringing. A refused microphone is only ever said. No wire change. Release builds always keep it on. Line `call_media_refused`. Untested on phones. **Requires / masked by:** private calls only; `test_call_readiness_permissions=false` asks for the camera at onboarding instead, so nothing is asked at call time. |
| Quiet end of an incoming link that never rang (`test_call_quiet_pre_ring_failure`) [call_quiet_pre_ring_failure] | Off | An incoming link that fails before the phone rings (a caller's first attempt that it then retries, a relay hop that gave up, a silent stranger closed at the handshake deadline) changes no screen and shows no message. The call endpoint is rebuilt for the screen the user is on after the same pause as today, and one line `call_pre_ring_failure` records the end with its reason. It applies only on a screen where an idle endpoint waits for calls (Nearby, Home, Live browsing) and while the user is not dialling; anywhere else the ordinary path stays. Off: the ordinary call-ended path, which moves the user to Nearby and says "The incoming call couldn't connect" about a call they never saw. A failure after ringing started is unchanged in both arms. Untested on phones. **Requires / masked by:** private calls only. |
| Call encoder fit (`test_call_encoder_fit`) [call_encoder_fit] | Off | Capability-based, not family-based. If a call's native AV1 encoder stays over budget at its fastest preset with fast tools for 3 windows (the A15s encoded at 101-103 ms p95 against a 66.7 ms budget, giving 6.9-7.2 FPS while also decoding), that geometry is remembered for 7 days and later calls start at the next smaller offered size at the same FPS, shown in the status line. Live camera only (raw inputs fix the geometry). Untested on phones. |
| Sender-gap audio realignment off (`test_audio_gap_align=false`) [audio_gap_align] | Realignment on | Control arm. After a mute, push-to-talk release or dropped packet the receiver writes silence so the next talk spurt starts on its playout target, never more than the sender's gap. Before, the spurt played early with no jitter buffer and speech slowed to 0.96x for about 6 s. Decoded PCM also follows the sender timestamps now: the S24 decoder path kept output timestamps contiguous across a 6 s mute and then underran 164 times. Line `BENCHMARK audio_gap_align`. Untested on phones. |
| Low-delay voice-call playout (`test_voice_call_low_delay`) [voice_call_low_delay] | Off | Voice-only calls on unbatched links start playout at 80 ms (adaptive to 400 ms) instead of 250 ms (the voice runs measured a longest sender audio queue of 27 ms; that is a measurement, not a limit the sender enforces). Line `BENCHMARK call_playout_profile`. Untested on phones. |
| Separate call audio clock (`test_call_audio_own_clock`) [call_audio_own_clock] | Off | Call audio keeps its own playout delay, grown only by late audio, instead of the video delay (600 ms in video calls after bursts). Speech may then lead the picture by up to the video's added delay: lip sync traded for conversational delay, a design decision. Untested on phones. |
| Stale call audio after a stall (`test_call_stale_audio_drop`) [call_stale_audio_drop] | Off (Experimental, 2026-11-07) | Design decision 2026-11-06 (Q-AUDIO-STALL), Android first. After the peer's call audio stopped arriving for 2 s or more, audio still late after the call's playout clock grew to its maximum (older than the playout budget: 600 ms on a normal call, 400 ms with low-delay voice, 800 ms on a constrained link) is dropped instead of played, so the listener does not hear seconds of old speech before catching up. Normal jitter (any gap under 2 s) still plays every packet. One stall drops at most its own gap plus the budget of sender time (a link that stays slow is played late, not silenced), and the catch-up ends at the first packet that is on time. Off runs the same detection in observe mode and drops nothing. One line per stall: `BENCHMARK call_audio_stale: mode=drop\|observe \| gapMs= \| budgetMs= \| stale= \| staleSpanMs= \| maxLateMs= \| end=on_time\|cap\|next_stall\|closed \| firstPlayedLateMs=`. Video, streams and audio-only live are unchanged; iPhone, Mac and Windows follow later. Untested on phones. **Requires / masked by:** private calls only. |
| Call fast recovery after one late burst (`test_call_fast_recovery`) [call_fast_recovery] | Off (Experimental, 2026-11-11) | One short glitch kept a call late for the rest of the call: a 526 ms sender pause grew the receiver's shared call delay by about 300 ms (late audio after a gap grows it even with speech-led playout, which limits only video), receive-to-screen latency went from p50 about 150 ms to about 450 ms, and calls shrink only after 10 s of calm at 1 ms per 100 ms, so 300 ms takes about 40 s. With this on, after one isolated burst (its late media within 1 s, then 2 s without late media) the receiver leaves out quiet decoded 20 ms audio chunks inside pauses (below about -35 dBFS, after at least 120 ms of quiet, only before another quiet chunk, at most one in every 80 ms, with a 5 ms crossfade) and the call clock moves earlier by the same amount from that point on, so video shows that much earlier at the same moment and lip sync holds. Speech is never left out or time-stretched: during continuous speech it waits and today's slow shrink still applies. Repeated bursts (late media again within 10 s) keep today's behaviour. Expected effect: an isolated glitch costs about 300 ms of delay for a few seconds of pause (about 1.2 s of pause for 300 ms) instead of about 40 s. Risk: pauses inside a sentence get up to a quarter shorter while recovering, a soft word end below the threshold after 120 ms of quiet could be clipped, and video runs up to 1.33x for the length of a pause (no encoded frame is dropped; the display may merge one frame per step). A noisy room (background above the threshold) never recovers faster. Encoded media, the sender, streams and audio-only live are unchanged; voice-only calls use the same rule. Line `BENCHMARK call_fast_recovery: event=armed\|consumed\|done\|skipped_speech\|sustained \| addedDelayMs= \| droppedAudioMs= \| sinceGapMs= \| targetAddedMs=`. Untested on phones. A/B: a user-launch call pair that pauses briefly (the naturally gappy S22 <- ASUS direction, or a planned short sender pause), arm on against off, 6 or more calls per arm in randomised order; metrics: added delay over time (`media_age addedDelayMs`), receive-to-screen latency, audio gaps and underruns, video freezes. **Requires / masked by:** private calls only; attaches to the clock audio follows (with `test_call_audio_own_clock` the audio clock only, so video then keeps its own delay). |
| Call video restart after a decoder stall (`test_call_video_restart`) [call_video_restart] | Off (Experimental, 2026-11-10) | A call's video decoder that stops taking frames no longer ends the call (audit MEDIA-06). Off, a decoder that stopped completing work fills its queue, the call's single receive path waits 5 s for room (the voice freezes with it) and the call ends as a local media failure. On: the receive path waits at most the 1.5 s playout budget, and a decoder watchdog stall releases it at once; the stall takes the decoder restart a recoverable codec error already uses (drop the decoder, keep the newest codec configuration, wait for a keyframe and ask the peer for one, hop-local), with the stalled codec released off the receive thread (bounded release). Audio, the playout clock and the carriers are untouched. The first stall rebuilds the same decoder, the next excludes that component for the call and tries the next decoder that supports the stream (even in a benchmark launch, where decoder fallback is otherwise off; an explicitly requested decoder is never substituted), and the third stall without 60 rendered frames in between stops video on this phone while voice continues. On screen: "Recovering remote video; audio and call remain connected", then "Video stopped on this phone; voice continues" with the Video paused cover. With it on, `test_decoder_stall_recovery` stands aside (the call replaces the decoder). Line `call_video_restart: stall= \| decoder= \| rendered= \| action=rebuild_same\|exclude_try_next\|stop_video \| restarts= \| excluded= \| queueWaitMs= \| cause=`; the decoder_stall line says `action=call_restart`. Untested on phones. **Requires / masked by:** private calls with video. |

| Call-link pacing (`test_call_link_pacing`) [call_link_pacing] | Off | Classic RFCOMM and LE CoC calls pace writes at the peer-measured arrival rate (seeded like a stream child), and the peer's call reports also teach learned link capacity, which today only stream children do. Each call flush already waits until the stack has read the socket buffer, so only the RFCOMM/L2CAP queues (about 10 KB) and controller buffers could hide below the app. Tradeoff: the bounded call queue can fill sooner and drop video until a keyframe, as on GATT. Needs link pacing. Untested on phones. |
| Call resolution ladder (`test_call_resolution_ladder`) [call_resolution_ladder] | On for normal launches (Automatic rate control); Off for DEBUG benchmark launches unless the extra is `true` | Design decision 2026-10-10. Only bitrate and FPS adapted before; the M31 VP9 encoder at 150 kbps and 640x480 sat at its maximum quantizer for 99.5% of frames. Like WebRTC (default singlecast start limits, BSD-3: VP9 480x270 120, 640x360 190, 960x540 350 kbps; AV1 176/256/384 kbps; 320x180 floor), a call steps its camera geometry down when the video target stays below the current size's limit for 3 s, and back up one rung after the target holds 1.25 x the larger size's limit for 10 s (at least 15 s after a change). Rungs are modes every lens offers at the call's exact FPS and aspect ratio, never above the call's start geometry (so device-policy call caps and the encoder fit still bound it). FPS, bitrate target and the call header are unchanged: the header is the maximum and the restarted encoder's first keyframe carries the new size in-band (Android CDD 5.3 dynamic resolution switching); no wire change. Cost: a camera/encoder restart (as for a lens switch) per change. Exact rate control, raw inputs and benchmark launches keep the requested geometry. Lines `BENCHMARK call_resolution requested=... effective=... reason=...` and receiver `BENCHMARK decoder_output_size`. Untested on phones. |
| Measured Auto ladder (`test_auto_ladder`; optional `test_auto_ladder_class=strong\|standard\|weak`) [AutoQualityFeature] | **Off** (DEBUG launch extra only; Experimental) | With Video quality on Auto (never with Manual video settings, never with a raw benchmark input) the start rung comes from the measured link instead of the fixed 240p / 15 FPS / 200 kbps default: unmeasured plans a 300 kbps link (360p15); measured plans 85% of the goodput (a call direction gets half of the measured link) with the wire model `1.076 x (video + 0.64 x fps) + audio wire`. Rungs by link goodput (AV1; VP9 differs at the 360p and 480p steps): 60 audio only, 100 240p10 (Opus 16 only), 150 240p12, 200 240p15, 300-450 360p15, 700 480p15 (720p15 for a strong AV1 encoder), 1000 480p24; 24 and 30 FPS only above their fitted minimum. Frame rate is cut before resolution above 15 FPS, resolution before frame rate below it. The request is a climb bound, not a fixed ceiling (a call: the transport maximum; a stream: the learned fan-out budget for one link, at most the transport maximum; fan-out admission still plans at the start rate): the rate controller starts at the plan's rate and climbs only on clean evidence, the resolution ladder moves one rung up after 1.25x headroom held 10 s (15 s between changes) and down after 1.5 s under the rung's need, inside a header that is the class maximum. Calls and live streams use the same ladder; the start rests on delivered evidence (the lower of the probe and the 1M ceiling of a remembered bad-2M peer, capped at what the peer's link last carried; a probe alone starts one rung down), and a climb needs windows that carried 80% of the target. A live stream changes its picture size rarely (down: target under the rung's limit for 3 s and 3 s of continuous queue or lateness evidence; up: 30 s clean, 30 s of target headroom and 60 s since the last change) and viewers keep the last frame through a marked size change. A rise of the frame rate or picture size waits for the sender and peer queues to stay within 150 ms (and no late child) for 5 s; the arm-time start and header use the cold-start rule before delivered evidence (a probe alone: 30% of the plan's video, at most 140 kbps, 240p class, 15 FPS at most). A software encoder is offered only the sizes it can encode at 80% of the frame interval (measured encode time per megapixel; weak CPUs are classed WEAK) and steps a rung down when its live load stays over 1.0; the call frame rate has hysteresis (down after 2 s, up after 10 s, one level); call video pauses (audio first) when the controller sits at its floor and the link stays behind for 6 s. A stream restarts its camera encoder at a rung change (not while paused). Manual picker: a starved combination (bits per pixel per frame under the fitted floor) shows a warning; the request is never changed. Also makes the call resolution ladder default on for the launch, with the measured 640x360 limits (AV1 150, VP9 175 kbps instead of WebRTC's 256 / 190). Logs `BENCHMARK auto_ladder: scope=call|stream|call_start|stream_resolution|stream_fps|call_fps ...` and `call_resolution`. Untested on phones. |
| GPU scale ladder (`test_gpu_scale_ladder`) [GpuScaleFeature] | **Off** (DEBUG launch extra only; Experimental; needs `test_auto_ladder=true`) | Capture once, scale on the GPU. With the measured Auto ladder on and a hardware surface-input encoder, the camera opens once at one capture mode (the smallest mode that covers the session's largest picture in its aspect; cropped on the GPU when the sensor has no such aspect) and every frame is drawn through an EGL crop/scale into the encoder's input surface at the rung size. A stream or call rung change then rebuilds only the MediaCodec (its first frame is a keyframe, marked as a discontinuity so viewers rebuild their decoder as before) and never restarts the camera; every rung keeps the session aspect (720/540/480/360/240/180/144p in 16:9, rounded to the encoder's alignment, a few pixels cropped rather than stretched), and the ladder's rungs are no longer limited by the camera's mode list. Software (YUV input) encoders such as the native libaom, raw fixtures, benchmark reference frames and Manual video settings keep the camera-mode path unchanged. Costs one GPU draw per frame; a YUV to RGB to YUV round trip in the encoder input (colour not yet compared). Logs `BENCHMARK gpu_scale: scope=stream|call ...`, `capture opened`, `rung=WxH | encoderResetMs=... | gpuMs/frame ...` and `firstFrameMs`. Untested on phones. |
| Call audio focus (`test_call_audio_focus`) [call_audio_focus] | On for user sessions; benchmark launches off unless the extra | `CallAudioFocus`. A call holds AUDIOFOCUS_GAIN_TRANSIENT for USAGE_VOICE_COMMUNICATION while its audio session is open, so music, podcasts and navigation pause instead of playing over the call. A transient loss (a cellular call ringing or active) holds the call on the existing mute path, so the peer gets Microphone muted (signal 9) and the screen says "On hold"; regaining focus restores the user's own mute choice. Permanent and duckable losses are only logged. Line `BENCHMARK call_audio_focus`. Untested on phones. |
| Call ringer policy (`test_call_ringer_policy`) [call_ringer_policy] | On for user sessions; benchmark launches off unless the extra | `CallRinger`/`CallRingerPolicy`. An incoming call follows the ringer mode (silent: nothing; vibrate: vibration only; normal: ringtone, and vibration when "vibrate when ringing" is on or unreadable) and Do Not Disturb (no ring under None/Alarms; Priority rings only when calls from anyone are allowed). Vibration uses USAGE_RINGTONE attributes. If the default ringtone cannot be read (some Samsung phones throw SecurityException) it falls back to the settings default, then a bundled generated tone (`res/raw/call_ring_fallback.wav`). Before, it always vibrated and rang, and a Samsung exception played nothing. Line `BENCHMARK call_ringer`. Untested on phones. |
| Outgoing ringback (formerly Settings > Features "Ringing sound while calling"; DEBUG `test_call_ringback` is the benchmark switch) [call_ringback] | On since 2026-10-17, no Settings switch (design decision; a constant with no stored choice). One constant, `CallFeatureDefaults.RINGBACK` (true) | The caller hears ToneGenerator TONE_SUP_RINGTONE while the callee rings, instead of silence; it stops on any other phase. A voice call plays it on the voice-call stream (the earpiece, where the phone is held); a video call on the music stream (the loudspeaker), because the call's audio session, which owns the route, does not exist yet. Not yet tested on phones. |
| CallStyle call notifications (formerly Settings > Features "Answer and decline from notifications"; DEBUG `test_call_style_notification` is the benchmark switch) [call_style_notification] | On since 2026-10-17, no Settings switch (design decision; a constant with no stored choice). One constant, `CallFeatureDefaults.CALL_NOTIFICATIONS` (true) | `CallNotificationActions`. The incoming alert becomes NotificationCompat.CallStyle with Decline/Answer and a full-screen intent (Android downgrades it to heads-up without the Android 14+ special access); the call's foreground-service notice becomes an ongoing CallStyle with Hang up while calling and in the call. Answer opens the call screen (the camera/microphone service still starts while visible); Decline and Hang up are not-exported broadcasts. Every action carries a per-process random token, because MainActivity is exported. Names follow `NotificationPrivacy` : the incoming person is the caller in the full notification, which a secure lock screen replaces with the name-free public version; the ongoing notice never names the peer. Showing the call screen over the keyguard is the separate lock-screen switch. On Android 13+ the notification permission is part of call readiness (see "Call readiness permissions"). Line `BENCHMARK call_notification_action`. Not yet tested on phones. |
| Camera pause at the ear (formerly Settings > Features "Pause video at the ear"; DEBUG `test_call_proximity_camera_pause` is the benchmark switch) [call_proximity_camera_pause] | On since 2026-10-17, no Settings switch (design decision; a constant with no stored choice). One constant, `CallFeatureDefaults.EAR_CAMERA_PAUSE` (true) | `CallProximityCameraPause`. In a video call with audio on the earpiece and the proximity sensor near, the outgoing camera stops and the peer gets Camera off; far again, or a route change away from the earpiece, restarts it with Camera on. The user's camera choice is unchanged; the screen-off lock above is separate and unchanged. Line `BENCHMARK call_proximity_camera`. Not yet tested on phones. Experimental hysteresis (`test_proximity_pause_hysteresis=true`, default off): near must hold 1.5 s before the camera pauses and two toggles are at least 3 s apart, so a flapping sensor cannot cause a camera session rebuild per flap; kept off until the 50-cycle ear check on the ASUS because it changes a shipped feature. |
| Call audio starvation concealment (`test_call_audio_conceal`) [call_audio_conceal] | Off (A/B later) | `AudioConcealment` (NetEq Expand/Merge/Accelerate ideas, WebRTC BSD-3, see THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES). When the call AudioTrack holds under 30 ms and no decoded audio is waiting, 10 ms chunks repeat the last pitch period under a 60 ms fade into comfort noise (at most about -50 dBFS) instead of underrunning; the next real audio is cross-faded in over 5 ms. Playout faster than 1.0x is then allowed only in quiet chunks unless the backlog exceeds 400 ms beyond the device baseline. Concealed samples are not traced as accepted media and do not move the sender timeline; they can add up to about 40 ms of queued audio after a gap. Line `BENCHMARK audio_conceal`. Untested on phones. |
| Caller names on the lock screen (Settings > Privacy "Show names on lock screen"; preference `notification_privacy`/`lock_screen_peer_names`; DEBUG `test_lock_screen_peer_names`) [lock_screen_peer_names] | Off (a privacy opt-in, not a promoted feature) | `NotificationPrivacy`, rule (2026-10-11): names while unlocked, hidden on the lock screen. Call/stream notifications are VISIBILITY_PRIVATE with a public version that says only "MeshLive / Active" or "Incoming call"; the incoming-call alert's full version (what an unlocked phone shows) names the caller, and a CallStyle alert uses the caller as its person. Opted in, the alert is VISIBILITY_PUBLIC, so the name shows on a secure lock screen too, and so does the call screen's. The ongoing call notification (CallStyle person) and a stream viewer's notification carry the peer's name in their full version only (2026-10-14, design decision Q16), with the same name-free public version, and become public only with this opt-in; audio-only live notifications never carry a name. Message notifications never carry text or sender: VISIBILITY_SECRET, or a generic "New message" when names are allowed. Before, neither set a visibility or public version, so a lock screen showing all content showed the activity and the caller's name. Android's own lock-screen setting "show all notification content" still overrides the public version, for every app. While a call lasts, the call screen follows the lock state: the real name after an unlock, "Encrypted call" again after the screen relocks. Not yet tested on phones. |
| Always reachable for calls (`test_always_reachable`) [always_reachable] | On for normal launches; Off for DEBUG benchmark launches unless the extra is `true` | Found by the first iPhone -> Android test: on Home `dumpsys bluetooth_manager` showed `Advertisers: []`, so no caller could find the phone. On: the private-call endpoint (the same availability advertisement and GATT/CoC listener as the Call screen, no scanning) also runs on Home and Settings-from-Home while the app is visible, and in the background while the session service holds the connectedDevice type (started while visible, Android 12+ rule; a refusal stops the endpoint and `BENCHMARK foreground_service` reports it). Going live or watching closes it so the stream offer, title pages and viewer forwarder keep their advertising sets; it never adds a scan start. Low battery and Battery Saver do not turn it off (design decision: relay helping pauses, calls stay core). A call that arrives through Home returns to Home. Home shows "Reachable for calls - Visible to nearby phones" only while the endpoint really advertises. Off: exactly the old Call-screen-only behaviour. Line `BENCHMARK call_reachability`. The Settings switch "Reachable for calls" (default on, preference `reachable_for_calls`) is the user off-switch for normal launches. Untested on phones. |
| Reachable while browsing (`test_reachable_while_browsing`) [reachable_while_browsing] | On for normal launches; Off for DEBUG benchmark launches unless the extra is `true` (`false` is the control arm) | The Live tab is the landing screen and the stream browser (auto-scan). Opening it used to apply the Watch rules and close the call endpoint, so a phone sitting on the Live tab could not receive calls. On: browsing (scanning only) keeps an idle call endpoint (call availability advertisement plus GATT/CoC listener, no scanning) running, exactly as on Home, because scanning uses no advertising set; it closes when the user joins a stream (`connectToStream`) or goes live and comes back when they return to browsing. The advertising-set budget argument (stream offer, viewer request, title set) applies only once one of those is on air. An incoming call while browsing stops the stream scan, shows the incoming-call screen, and returns to the Live tab (scan restarted, endpoint restored after the same GATT server pause) when it ends. Same gates as Home: the "Reachable for calls" setting and `test_always_reachable` still decide whether any endpoint runs, background needs the connectedDevice foreground service (Android 12+), low battery and Battery Saver do not turn it off, and it adds no scan start (ScanStartGate). Off: exactly the old behaviour (the Live tab closes the endpoint). Line `BENCHMARK call_reachability: reachable_while_browsing=...` and `role=... reason=live_browsing`. Phone check: a call to a phone resting on the Live tab rings, over BLE GATT and over BLE L2CAP (CoC). Untested on phones. |
| In-call link renewal (`test_call_link_renewal`, both phones; DEBUG hook `test_call_tx_stall_after_s=N`) [call_link_renewal] | On (design decision 2026-10-21; `false` is the control arm) | A direct GATT call whose one direction stalled (the phone that stopped hearing its peer still sends) replaces its hop link instead of ending: the silent side offers a renewal, the other side opens a one-time connectable advert, and the call moves to a new link with the same call ID and end-to-end keys. The old link is closed only after the new advert was found, and every failure ends the call within 12 s of the offer. Relayed, Classic, CoC and verification sessions are never renewed. Wire rules: PROTOCOL "Experimental in-call link renewal"; reasons and both reviews: DECISIONS "Design SR". The hook stops this phone's sending on the call's first link N s after the call became active, to induce the stall. CF-CALL-21 is amended (a renewal replaces only the hop link; the call ID and end-to-end keys continue, so it is not a redial; DECISIONS "Design SR") and the user guide says what the user sees. Still open: the A15s-as-peripheral connect failure behind the 3 lost calls. A renewal-negotiated direct GATT call also runs the 14 s stall rule whatever `test_call_stall_tolerance` says (see that row). **Requires / masked by:** a call whose peer lacks the renewal bit, and every relayed, Classic, CoC or verification call, is never renewed. Video fragments and renewal are both on by default for direct GATT user calls (benchmark launches get neither); the combination was not exercised by the 13/16 renewal A/B, which ran with fragments off. |
| Stranger call follow-up (`test_stranger_call_followup=false`) | On | DEBUG control disables the after-call Add prompt and restores old Recent taps. Default offers Add contact and explains how to call a stranger again. No stranger keys are saved. |
| Complete Recent calls (`test_complete_recent_calls=false`) | On | DEBUG control projects active-only, five-person history at load time without truncating the retained 50 entries. Default records ended calls with direction/outcome, retains 50 calls and reads the old store without inventing old directions. |
| Full-screen call guidance (`test_full_screen_call_guidance=false`) | On | DEBUG control skips the new onboarding step and status-sheet warning. Android 14+ opens the app's full-screen call setting; users can skip. |
| Call readiness permissions (`test_call_readiness_permissions=false`) | On | DEBUG control restores camera-at-onboarding and notification-independent readiness. Default defers camera until video use and requires notifications on Android 13+. |
| Honest call status (`test_honest_call_status=false`) | On | DEBUG control arm restores Ready during watching/broadcasting; default explains paused calls in the chip and sheet. |
| Bluetooth headset for call audio (Settings > Video and calls "Bluetooth headset for call audio"; DEBUG `test_call_bluetooth_output`) [call_bluetooth_output] | Off (until phone test T-OUT1) | Lists a connected Bluetooth audio device (SCO, LE Audio headset, hearing aid; Android 12 and newer) in the call screen's audio output sheet and lets the user pick it. Call audio never goes to a Bluetooth headset otherwise, because SCO and LE Audio share airtime with the Bluetooth link that carries the call itself (PLAN "Bluetooth headsets"); it is never chosen automatically even with the switch on. Off: the sheet lists the phone speaker, the earpiece and plugged-in wired/USB headsets only.  |
| Native libopus call audio (no Settings switch since 2026-11-04, a stored choice is ignored; DEBUG `test_call_native_opus` [call_native_opus] is the benchmark switch; sub-switches `test_call_native_opus_fec` [call_native_opus_fec] (default on), `test_call_native_opus_loss_pct` [call_native_opus_loss_pct] (default 10) and `test_call_native_opus_dtx` [call_native_opus_dtx] (default off) stay DEBUG-only: release builds always use FEC on, 10 % and no DTX) | Promoted 2026-10-11 (default OFF until its phone test passes; one constant, `ConnectivityFeatureDefaults.NATIVE_OPUS`; design decision) | Calls encode and decode Opus with the pinned libopus 1.5.2 (statically linked in `libbluetooth_video_native.so`) instead of MediaCodec: OPUS_APPLICATION_VOIP, in-band FEC with a fixed expected-loss hint, the same rate, 20 ms frames, complexity 10 and constrained VBR. The decoder conceals a gap of up to 100 ms with libopus PLC (the frame just before the arriving packet comes from its FEC when present) at the missing sender timestamps; a longer gap keeps its remainder for the existing sender-gap silence alignment. Concealed frames are counted separately, never as received packets. It is independent of `test_call_audio_conceal` [call_audio_conceal]: native PLC fills missing sender timestamps at decode, that switch fills a dry AudioTrack at playout; both may be on. Why: c2.android.opus hard-codes general-audio mode, no FEC, no DTX and no concealment (AOSP C2SoftOpusEnc.cpp/C2SoftOpusDec.cpp), while WebRTC and Signal use libopus with VOIP mode, FEC and PLC; on a gap the MediaCodec path plays silence. The packets are standard Opus with the Codec2 configuration block, so MediaCodec and iOS libopus peers decode them and older receivers need nothing new; each side switches independently. DTX (sub-switch, off) changes the packet cadence and needs its own A/B plus a PROTOCOL note before promotion. Library or encoder failure falls back to MediaCodec and logs `requested=native | impl=mediacodec | reason=native_unavailable`. Lines: `BENCHMARK audio_codec: role=encoder|decoder | impl=native|mediacodec | fec=... | plc=...`, `BENCHMARK audio_plc` and `BENCHMARK audio_codec_stats`. APK size (always built in, even with the switch off): arm64 `libbluetooth_video_native.so` 7,368,680 -> 7,785,168 bytes (+407 KB stored uncompressed for 16 KB alignment, about +244 KB deflated), 16 KB check OK. **Phone test (A/B, switch off versus on at both phones; M31 <-> ASUS and S24 <-> A15s calls, 3 repeats each, then one call against an iOS or MediaCodec-only peer), pass when:** audio drops and audio-queue age p95 are no worse with the switch on, concealed frames appear under real loss and never count as received packets, no `reason=native_unavailable` fallback occurs on the four phones, the call survives a switch change between calls, and the mixed-codec call plays both ways. DTX stays off: it needs its own A/B and a PROTOCOL note. Not yet tested on phones (speech quality, CPU, FEC overhead at 16/32 kbps and interop with MediaCodec and iOS peers are unmeasured). **Requires / masked by:** the sub-switches (`call_native_opus_fec`, `_loss_pct`, `_dtx`) do nothing unless `call_native_opus` is on. |
| Raw self-view rate (`test_call_raw_self_view_fps`) | 5 FPS in raw benchmark calls | Self-view picture-in-picture from the raw fixture. 0 turns it off. |
| Call head-of-line policy (`test_call_head_of_line=off\|gatt_default\|on`) [call_head_of_line] | User launches: fragments and speech-led playout on direct BLE GATT calls; benchmark launches: the separate extras (default off) | One resolver for "Call video fragments" and "Speech-led call playout" (it replaced the release-only `callGattSpeechDefaults` flag). `gatt_default` makes a benchmark launch measure exactly the user policy, `off` is the control arm, `on` turns both on for every transport; without the extra a benchmark launch reads the two separate extras. Speech-led playout of the user policy and of `gatt_default`/`on` applies only when the call negotiated fragments: only the pair was measured (DECISIONS "Known interactions" B5); the separate `test_call_speech_led_playout` arm stays ungated. Each call logs its source. |
| Dial a recent sighting at once (`test_dial_recent_sighting`) [dial_recent_sighting] | Off (Experimental) | A call to a row heard under 1.5 s ago connects to that sighting at once instead of first waiting for a sighting newer than the tap (`RecentSightingDial`). Not within 10 s of a local rotating-ID epoch boundary, where the old address stops advertising; relayed rows keep their reach-table path. No wire change: the callee sees the same connection to the same advert, and a failed connection takes the existing call failure and rediscovery path. Reason (2026-10-26, saved runs, 2238 dials): the wait for a fresh sighting took a median 160 ms (p90 591 ms) before anything else in tap-to-ring. A/B: tap-to-OUTGOING_RINGING per caller, plus the count of fast dials whose connection failed. Line `call_invite: peer_seen | ... | matchedBy=recent | ageMs=`. |
| Incoming hop-authentication timer (`test_call_preauth_deadline_short=true`; DEBUG only; Experimental) [call_preauth_deadline_short] | Off (release builds always off) | Design PP package A (2026-11-10), SEC-02. A second, shorter bound on an incoming BLE GATT or CoC connection: it starts at the transport accept and ends when the hop handshake and the required link opening are verified (not at the end-to-end session, so a relay waiting for its next hop or caller is never charged), 10 s (the caller's own hop bound) instead of waiting out the 20 s incoming watchdog, which stays exactly as it is. A BMH2 first frame (older builds, RV8 tarpit delay), Classic and Wi-Fi connections and the relay SETUP/READY budgets keep the conservative bound only. Line `incoming_phase: event=deadline \| timer=hop_auth \| gen= \| closed=true \| boundMs=10000`; with DEBUG the milestone lines `incoming_phase: milestone=transport_accept\|hop_verified\|endpoint_authenticated\|reader_started\|first_invite` are logged in both arms. Kept because it releases the call endpoint from a connector that never speaks sooner, and because the bound is a measured one (accept to hop verified, every saved run). No wire change. Pass criteria: a raw connection that sends nothing is closed at 10 s (+-1 s) with the line above and the phone is reachable again; every normal call (direct, stranger, contact, relayed target) is unaffected. Measured on one pair (2026-11-11): on a slow link the bound also ends a legitimate call. A15s calling ASUS, whose LE 2M link stalls or crawls, rang 6 of 6 with the switch off and 4 of 6 with it on: the 10 s bound closed two handshakes that were still progressing (hop verified in up to 6 s, the end-to-end session in up to 14 s on this pair). Keep off until the bound accounts for a slow but progressing link. |
| Incoming pre-INVITE timer (`test_call_pre_invite_deadline=true`; DEBUG only; Experimental) [call_pre_invite_deadline] | Off (release builds always off) | Design PP package A (2026-11-10), SEC-06. An authenticated incoming connection of an ordinary call that sends nothing but heartbeats held the call endpoint until its peer went away: no bound existed after the end-to-end handshake. With the switch on the connection is closed 10 s after the reader that consumes the INVITE started (20 s for a relayed, Classic or Wi-Fi endpoint, whose timing is unmeasured), and the first valid INVITE disarms the timer at once (the timer only observes an INVITE that already passed framing, sequence, call ID and identity checks; block and dial checks follow as before). A BVMID2 verification session (it never sends an INVITE), a message exchange, a relay duty, an outgoing call, a later carrier and a link renewal are never bounded by it. A timer and an INVITE at the same instant have exactly one outcome. Line `incoming_phase: event=deadline \| timer=pre_invite \| gen= \| closed=true \| boundMs=10000`. The recovery time to the call advert reappearing is the existing restart path (a requested GATT settle delay plus the asynchronous service start, and a 1-8 s rotating silence at an epoch boundary), measured separately from the close. No wire change. Pass criteria (T-PP2): an authenticated peer that sends only heartbeats is closed 10 s (+-1 s) after the reader started, the phone shows nothing, and the offer is back on air; a normal caller rings within its usual time in both arms; a QR code scan whose confirmation is read for over 20 s still completes. |

## Transports, PHY and Bluetooth-stack behaviour

| Switch | Default | What it does and why it is kept |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Classic without pairing (Settings > Advanced > Connection "Classic Bluetooth without pairing" (confirmation dialog "Connect without pairing"), confirmed by a dialog that explains visibility; DEBUG `test_classic_bond_free`, `test_classic_bounded_connect`, `test_classic_request_visible` are the benchmark switches) [classic_bond_free, classic_request_visible] | Promoted 2026-10-11 (default OFF until its phone test passes; one constant, `ConnectivityFeatureDefaults.CLASSIC_WITHOUT_PAIRING`; design decision) | Both phones need it. Calls, the call relay and the stream relay switch from Android's secure RFCOMM (which demands a numeric-comparison bond on both phones) to the insecure sockets streams already used; BMH2/BMS1/BME1 authentication and ordered encryption are unchanged and never depended on Android link security (LE CoC/GATT have none). Android then pairs Just Works without a dialog and stores no bond. Each phone remembers, in no-backup storage, the Classic address at which an authenticated identity was reached in either direction (64 entries, 30 days; a hint that BMH2 re-verifies, never trust), so a phone met once is paged directly. A first call to a never-met phone runs one Classic inquiry (about 13 s) after the normal candidates fail; the other phone must be visible (its Bluetooth settings screen, or the system visibility prompt it shows once per process when ready for Classic calls; benchmark launches only with `test_classic_request_visible`). Streams never run an inquiry (the broadcaster's 12 s budget) and reach only phones already met. Lines `Classic connect plan`, `Classic candidate failed`, `Classic connected`, `Classic address learned`, `BENCHMARK classic_features`. Turning the switch on asks first: a phone that is ready for Classic calls may ask Android to make it visible to nearby Bluetooth devices for two minutes (Android shows its own prompt), and anyone nearby can see its Bluetooth name meanwhile. It also turns on the bounded connect path (per-attempt deadlines, shared candidate order); that path alone stays a benchmark extra. Turning it on or off rebuilds an idle Classic call listener so the next call follows it, and turning it off deletes the remembered Classic addresses (they are used only while it is on). Failure texts name the setting: without it they say to pair, to turn on "Connect without pairing" on both phones, or to use BLE GATT; with it they say that both phones need it and that a phone not met before must be visible. **Phone test (S24, M31, ASUS: one pair with no Android bond, bonds removed first), pass when:** (1) with the switch on in both phones no Android pairing dialog appears and no bond exists afterwards; (2) a first Classic call to a visible phone connects within 30 s (inquiry), and the next call connects in under 12 s using the learned address (`Classic connected | kind=learned`); (3) with the other phone not visible the first call ends within 30 s with the "visible" text, never a hang; (4) with the switch on in one phone only the call ends with the text naming the setting; (5) switching off restores the secure path (`secure=true` in `Classic connect plan`); (6) a stream reaches a phone already met, and an unmet phone fails by the 12 s stream budget. Not yet tested on phones. |
| Bounded Classic connect (`test_classic_bounded_connect`; implied by Classic without pairing) [classic_bounded_connect] | Off | Every Classic role (stream, forwarder, call, call relay, stream relay) uses one connect path: a remembered or exactly bonded address first, then bonded phones and computers only (the call relay used to page earbuds and cars), each attempt bounded (exact address 12 s, bonded guess 3 s, BLE address 7 s; 11.5 s for streams inside the broadcaster's 12 s, 30 s otherwise), and a peer this phone cannot resolve fails within seconds with a pairing explanation instead of a bare timeout. A relayed call refuses a link that reaches the endpoint directly. Off: each role connects as before. Untested on phones. |
| Classic random-address skip off (`test_classic_page_random_address=true`) | Skip on Android 15+ | Control arm. Where Android reports a scanned BLE address's type (API 35+), Classic never pages an address reported random: BR/EDR paging can only reach a public address, and an unbonded peer's private address cost up to 12 s. Older APIs page it as a 7 s guess. Matcher `classicScanAddressTypeReported`, shaped for the policy registry. |
| Classic address overrides (`test_classic_addresses=prefix=AA:BB:..,...`, or `test_classic_address` + `test_classic_address_node`) [classic_addresses] | None | DEBUG: a run supplies a peer's Classic address read over adb for its 12-hex-digit announcement prefix; tried first, never stored. Samsung masks its own address in `dumpsys`, so this cannot always be filled automatically. |
| LE PHY (`test_ble_phy=1m`, `coded-s2` or `coded-s8`) [ble_phy], GATT request MTU (`test_gatt_request_mtu`, 23-517) [gatt_request_mtu] | 2M, 517 | 1M is a control for the ASUS <-> A15s ~65 kbps link. Coded is a long-range option for low-rate links such as voice calls (Core spec: 500 kb/s S2, 125 kb/s S8; measured GATT 154/47 kbps, CoC 81/40 kbps); both phones must support it (the M31 does not), connections still start on LE 1M, and learned link capacity is stored apart from 2M. Android 14+ clients always negotiate MTU 517 and ignore the MTU request. 2M stays the only selectable PHY. The `experimentalBle1mPhy` alias of this switch is used only by tests (**Not wired**). Untested on phones. |
| Aligned GATT values (`test_gatt_aligned_writes`) [gatt_aligned_writes] | Off | A 512-byte GATT value is a 519-byte L2CAP PDU, sent as three LE data PDUs of 251 + 251 + 17 octets; 495-byte values fill exactly two (about 245 instead of 170 payload bytes per PDU: +12% airtime efficiency on 2M, more where a controller limits PDUs per connection event). Writes and notifications. Untested on phones. |
| Stream source remembers a 2M link that went silent (`test_stream_adaptive_phy=false` is the control arm) [stream_adaptive_phy] | On | A direct GATT stream whose single 2M link ended with a link-layer connection timeout (GATT status 8) or the sender's own heartbeat timeout, with the peer's silence starting within 15 s of hop authentication, marks the authenticated hop peer bad on 2M (`BENCHMARK adaptive_phy: link=stream ... early_loss=true`); the next stream link to it requests 1M. A link closed unfinished (the broadcaster's 12 s connection timeout or a connection timeout during the hop handshake, 5 s or more after it opened) counts from the link opening, keyed by the advertised prefix when the peer never authenticated (not for a rotating pseudonym). A viewer leaving (status 19), a quick user cancel, a relay-capable peer, a fan-out source and a 1M link never count. Needs `test_adaptive_phy` and `test_adaptive_phy_memory`. Why: an A15s source never kept a 2M link to the ASUS (9 of 11 attempts ended in status 8 within 5-18 s, the rest by an app close after the link crawled), and the stream path was the only dial without the call and message protections. The first link to a new peer still pays the failure. Off redials on 2M every time; phone-measured, the off arm reproduces the failure (regression control, no routine A/B). |
| Call stall remembers 2M at the stall (`test_stall_remembers_2m=false` is the control arm) [stall_remembers_2m] | On | A direct call dial whose first 2M setup was closed by the stall guard records the callee's advertised node as bad on 2M immediately, instead of only after the 1M retry authenticated. A retry that cannot find the callee again (the callee still holds the dead link and is not advertising) used to leave no memory, so every later call repeated the 3 s stall. A relayed offer, a rotating (pseudonym) advertisement not recognised as one contact (see `phy_memory_contact_key`) and any other setup failure are not recorded. Needs `test_adaptive_phy_memory` and `test_phy_setup_stall`. |
| Call stall re-discovery budget (DEBUG `test_stall_rediscover_ms`; default 8000, A/B arm 20000) [stall_rediscover_ms] | Off (Experimental; 8000 = earlier behaviour) | After a call setup stall the 1M retry rescans for the callee's new advertisement. Today the scan is capped at 8 s and clamped by the 15 s early-loss window; when the A15s controller never delivers LL_TERMINATE, the ASUS only sees its supervision timeout 7-9 s later and re-advertises about 1.6 s after that (8.6-12.6 s after the close), so the scan ends with nothing and the retry dials the stale address (a 12 s connect timeout). With a value above 8000 the stalled path keeps scanning for the configured total (not clamped by the early-loss window; 60 s at most), dials a found fresh address at once and dials the stale address only if nothing was found. Hop-local, a direct offer only (a relayed offer keeps its relay), no wire change; the setup-stall guard, the bad-2M memory and the single 1M retry are unchanged. Tradeoff: a callee that never re-advertises costs the full total before the stale dial. Call dials only (the message-exchange retry keeps 8 s). Not yet run on phones. |
| Call retry release window (DEBUG `test_rediscover_release_window`) [rediscover_release_window] | On (promoted 2026-11-04, owner; `test_rediscover_release_window=false` is the DEBUG control arm) | After a failed 2M call setup the 1M retry scans for the callee's new advertisement for as long as the callee can take to release the old link, derived from the link's own timers: 3 x the 5 s LE supervision timeout after this phone closed a stalled link (the peer may never receive the terminate, and this phone's own controller can take up to 11.5 s to go quiet), 1 x after a loss this phone observed, plus the callee's 1.5 s GATT restart pause (0 for CoC) and 2 s scan slack, at most 20 s; a fresh advertisement is dialed as soon as it is seen. Root cause it addresses: on the A15s (MT6765) to ASUS (SD855) pair nothing the ASUS sends after the switch to 2M is acknowledged, so the A15s cannot end the link; the ASUS releases it one supervision timeout after the A15s goes quiet and advertises again 7-16.6 s after the close, after the fixed 8 s scan ended, and the retry dialed the stale address. Tradeoff: a callee that is really absent is reported up to about 9-10.5 s later. Hop-local, no wire change; healthy pairs never take this path. First phone A/B (2 x window): retry found the callee 10/12 on vs 6/13 off and calls reached ACTIVE 13/20 vs 9/20 (not significant); both on-arm misses re-advertised just after the scan ended, so the window was widened to 3 x. Second A/B on the 3 x window: calls reached ACTIVE 16/17 on vs 9/17 off (GATT 8/8 vs 4/8, CoC 8/9 vs 5/9) and the retry found the callee 14/14 vs 8/16; the one on-arm failure was the callee-found-too-early path. Preservation on this build: S22 -> ASUS and S22 -> A15s reached ACTIVE 48/48 with no retry in either arm; ASUS -> A15s has a separate fault in both arms (the caller stays in NEGOTIATING_MEDIA). Promoted 2026-11-04 (owner "yes turn on"). Two failure paths it does not touch: the callee found too early while both old links are still up (status 133, then a channel timeout), and no retry at all when the 2M link stays up but slow. |
| Fast 0x3E redials on a call's first dial (`test_fast_0x3e_dials`) [fast_0x3e_dials] | Off (Experimental) | A call's first GATT dial that fails fast with HCI 0x3E (GATT 133 or 62 within 1.5 s, no link) is redialed up to 4 dials in total, 0.3 s plus a random 0-0.5 s apart, while the next dial starts inside the call's early-loss window (`FastEstablishDials`); every other failure and every other connect path keep the ordinary rule (one redial after 0.5 s). Reason (2026-10-26, S22 and ASUS with HCI snoop on both): about 1 call in 10 failed when the caller got Connection Complete and then 0x3E 250-440 ms later twice, while the callee never received the CONNECT_IND, a per-event radio miss rather than an absent peer. The jitter keeps redials from repeating the same timing against the callee's scanning. No wire change. A/B: call success rate and tap-to-ring on the same pairs. Line `fast_0x3e_dial: failedAttempt= | delayMs=` or `action=give_up`. With `test_fast_fail_redial_all` too: that arm covers the BLE control client (CoC calls, streams, relay hops) with the same classifier and backoff, so both on = the rule on every LE connect. |
| Call 1M retry waits for the stalled link's ACL (`test_setup_retry_acl_wait=false` is the control arm) [setup_retry_acl_wait] | On | After the 2M setup-stall guard closes a call link, the 1M retry waits (at most 5 s) for that link's Bluetooth connection (ACL) to go down before it dials.
