The ACL can linger 1-4 s after the close while the callee already advertises again under a new address; a dial into the lingering ACL fails at once (HCI 0x3E, then 0x0B, GATT 133) and the call failed with most of the 15 s setup window unused. The watch is armed before the close and keyed by the old device, because the retry dials a new address; the stack's GATT connected list is not used (the A15s reports the peer disconnected while its ACL is up). Logs `BENCHMARK setup_retry_acl_wait: waitedMs=... \| dropped=true\|false`. Since the connection review (T1, 2026-10-27) the wait runs while the retry rescans (not before it) and is bounded at 1.5 s on Android 10/11, whose stacks never deliver the ACL broadcast for an unnamed random-address peer (9 of 9 A15s retries logged `waitedMs=5003 \| dropped=false`). |
| Fast-failure redial for the BLE control client - Experimental (`test_fast_fail_redial_all=true`; DEBUG only) [fast_fail_redial_all] | Off | Connection review T2/A11 (2026-10-27). A GATT 133/62 within 1.5 s (HCI 0x3E: the peer never answered the connect request) on the BLE control client (every CoC call, stream and relay-hop connect) is redialed after 300 ms + a random 0-500 ms while elapsed + delay + 600 ms fits an 11 s budget (and any outer setup budget), on any PHY. A slow failure (own deadline, the stack's 30 s timeout: out of range) still ends through the ordinary rule. How it relates to `test_fast_0x3e_dials`: that switch is the same rule for a call's first GATT video dial; this one covers only the BLE control client, so the two never act on the same connect and can be on together (the unified rule = both on). One classifier (`ConnectDurationClass.FAST_ESTABLISH_FAILURE`) and one backoff (`FastEstablishDials.delayMs`, used by `FastFailRedial`); only the window differs (11 s from this client's first connect here, 4 dials inside the call's early-loss window there). Line `BENCHMARK fast_fail_redial: link=ble-control \| ... \| delayMs= \| elapsedMs= \| budgetMs=11000`. Phone A/B: CoC calls and CoC relay hops on a pair with fast-0x3E failures (S22 <-> ASUS, A15s -> ASUS), ABBA, metric = setup success rate and dial-to-RINGING time; guard = no longer time-to-failure for an out-of-range peer. |
| Fast first redial after a quick 133/0x3E (DEBUG `test_fast_first_redial=false` is the control arm) [fast_first_redial] | On (promoted 2026-11-12 on mechanism; release builds too) | Join-latency P4a. The ordinary connect rule waits 500 ms before every redial; that spacing is for a peer whose old link is still going down. With the switch on, the FIRST attempt of a GATT video or BLE control connect that fails with GATT 133 or HCI 0x3E (62) within the shared fast-establishment class of 1.5 s (`ConnectDurationClass.isFastEstablishment`; the peer missed the connect request; 0.40-0.45 s in the evidence, 1.326 s in a JT2 first connect that the earlier 1.0 s window missed) is redialed after 200 ms. Later attempts, slower failures, other statuses, the attempt counts and the budget rules are unchanged; where `test_fast_0x3e_dials`, `test_fast_fail_redial_all`, `test_stall_retry_dials` or `test_unified_connect_policy` decide a failure, they still do. Expected saving: about 0.3 s per occurrence (8 of 28 CoC and 3 of 28 GATT cases had one). Risk: on crowded air a shorter spacing can repeat the same collision. Line `BENCHMARK fast_first_redial: link= \| failedAttempt=1 \| attemptMs= \| delayMs=200 \| ordinaryDelayMs=500`. Phone A/B: CoC and GATT streams and calls on pairs with status-133 redials (M31 -> ASUS, S22 <-> M31), ABBA, metric = join/setup time and setup success rate. Measured with the other two call-start switches (DECISIONS "Faster call start": S22 <-> ASUS, 12 calls per arm in random order, joins 0.9-1.2 s sooner from Accept; also ASUS <-> A15s and S22 <-> Realme) and promoted together on 2026-11-12 (design decision), before the idle-advert battery number (first-principles +0.1-0.3 mA; the advert switch goes back off if it measures above +0.5 mA). |
| Idle call endpoint keeps its fast advert (DEBUG `test_call_adv_idle_fast=false` is the control arm) [call_adv_idle_fast] | On (promoted 2026-11-12 on mechanism; release builds too) | Join-time JT2-P2. A call endpoint's GATT receiver advertising set starts at 100 ms / high power and steps to 250 ms / medium power 10 s later, so an idle callee answers a caller's connect request slowly (idle connects 0.27-1.08 s against 0.16-0.30 s inside the burst). With the switch on, only the call endpoint's own receiver set, while no link is up, keeps its fast interval: the balanced step is not asked for (no new advertising-set operation), the DEBUG line `adv_set_churn ... event=balanced_held` marks it, and a DEBUG line `adv_hold: role=call \| held_s=` repeats every 60 s while it holds. A link that is up, a shown verification code, streams, relays and the in-call renewal advert behave as before; the ASUS already gets the same outcome through the unconfirmed-in-place-step protection. Expected saving: 0.25-0.4 s median connect on an idle callee. Cost: a faster advertising interval and higher TX power while the endpoint is idle (first-principles estimate +0.1-0.3 mA; measure it). Phone A/B: callers dialling an idle callee after at least 30 s, ABBA, metric = connect time, plus a 30-minute idle battery arm on M31, A15s and Realme. Measured with the other two call-start switches (DECISIONS "Faster call start": S22 <-> ASUS, 12 calls per arm in random order, joins 0.9-1.2 s sooner from Accept; also ASUS <-> A15s and S22 <-> Realme) and promoted together on 2026-11-12 (design decision), before the idle-advert battery number (first-principles +0.1-0.3 mA; the advert switch goes back off if it measures above +0.5 mA). |
| Calls searching indicator settles (DEBUG `test_calls_indicator_settles=false` is the control arm) [calls_indicator_settles] | On (promoted on mechanism 2026-11-12) | The small searching indicator on Calls > Nearby spun for as long as discovery ran, and an indeterminate indicator redraws the window every frame: a phone left waiting on Nearby drew 60 frames a second the whole time (M31: 641 frames in 10 s on Nearby, 2 on Recent; a phone with system animations off is not affected). With the switch on it spins only for the first 12 s of a discovery round, the way Android's Bluetooth settings stops its indicator when a scan round ends; discovery keeps running, the list keeps updating and the empty-state hint is unchanged. Returning to Nearby starts a new round. Measured (B18, `dumpsys gfxinfo`, 10 s windows on an idle Nearby screen after the first 12 s): M31 608 frames off, 2 on; A15s 607 off, 2 on; during the first 12 s it still spins (M31 248 frames in 5 s). Release builds keep it on; the battery gain is not measured separately (the screen-on draw it removes is what hid the idle-advert cost). |
| Stall retry with its own budget - Experimental (`test_stall_retry_own_budget=true`; DEBUG only) [stall_retry_own_budget] | Off | Connection review A10 (2026-10-27). The 1M call retry after a 2M setup stall gets 10 s from the retry's own start for its stall-retry dials instead of what is left of the first attempt's 15 s window, and skips the ACL wait. Lines: the existing `stall_retry_dial`; no `setup_retry_acl_wait` line in this arm. Phone A/B: A15s -> ASUS calls (the stall pair), metric = retry success rate and stall-to-ACTIVE time. |
| Setup-stall guard arm - Experimental (`test_setup_stall_ms=N`, 0 = off; DEBUG only) [setup_stall_ms] | 0 (3000 ms, `AdaptivePhyMemory.SETUP_STALL_MS`) | Connection review A14 (2026-10-27). The first 2M call attempt's stall guard closes a link whose hop authentication has not finished N ms after it opened (healthy pairs authenticate in 71-269 ms after the link opens; the arm to test is 2000). Lines: the existing `adaptive_phy: ... reason=setup_stall \| guardMs=N`. Phone A/B: A15s -> ASUS and S22 -> ASUS calls, 3000 vs 2000, metric = dial-to-RINGING time and false stalls (a stall on a pair that authenticates on 2M otherwise). |
| Relayed SETUP budget from hop authentication - Experimental (`test_relayed_budget_from_hop_auth=true`; DEBUG only) [relayed_budget_from_hop_auth] | Off | Connection review A13 (2026-10-27). The caller's 20 s relayed SETUP budget starts when the first hop authenticates instead of at the dial, so a slow first hop (rescan, 1M retry) does not eat the helpers' share. Lines: the existing `relayed_setup: role=caller \| result=...`. Phone A/B: relayed calls through one helper with a slow first hop, metric = relayed setup success and `reason=` of failures. |
| Unified connect-retry policy - Experimental (`test_unified_connect_policy=true`; DEBUG only) [unified_connect_policy] | Off | Simplification pass A (2026-10-27), the single rule proposed by the connection review: every LE connect (GATT video and BLE control client: calls, messages, streams, relay hops, any PHY) redials a fast HCI 0x3E failure (GATT 133/62 within 1.5 s) after 300 ms + a random 0-500 ms while the dial still fits one absolute budget (the caller's setup deadline, at most 11 s after the first connect); any other failure keeps the ordinary class and attempt rule against the same budget; every per-attempt wait (GATT 12 s, control 6 s, the CoC socket bound, the MTU wait inside the attempt) is clipped to the budget (at least 2 s), and an attempt not connected after 5.5 s ends there. A relay helper's next hop gets one budgeted layer: no same-connect retry (`test_relayed_hop_retry`), the client loop runs inside the SETUP deadline minus 3 s, and the 2M -> 1M hop retry follows only a link that connected (stall or authentication failure). Replaces `test_fast_0x3e_dials`, `test_fast_fail_redial_all`, `test_stall_retry_dials` and the ordinary unbounded budget while on; `test_relay_establish_budget` still applies first where requested. Off: today's rules exactly, expressed as `ConnectRetryConfig.DEFAULT` and proven equal by JVM equivalence tests. Line `BENCHMARK unified_connect_retry: link= \| failedAttempt= \| class= \| action=dial/ordinary/give_up \| delayMs= \| elapsedMs= \| leftMs=` (GATT setup stage `connect_phase_timeout`). Phone A/B: direct CoC and GATT calls on pairs with fast-0x3E failures (S22 <-> ASUS, A15s -> ASUS), a relayed call through one helper, and an out-of-range callee; ABBA; metrics = setup success rate, dial-to-RINGING time, time to the failure notice for an absent peer (guard: not longer than off). |
| CoC control reattach makes one short attempt (`test_coc_attach_single=false` is the control arm; DEBUG only) [coc_attach_single] | On (release too) | Connection review T5 (2026-10-27). After a CoC socket connects, the GATT control client that adds priority and PHY control makes one attempt bounded at 3 s and returns at once; the PHY request follows 600 ms later, off the connect path. Control arm: up to 3 attempts of 6 s, and the connect waits the 600 ms. Phone check: CoC calls and streams on every pair, metric = connect-to-first-byte time and PHY reached (2M where requested). |
| Call 1M retry redials fast 0x3E failures (`test_stall_retry_dials=false` is the control arm) [stall_retry_dials] | On | Only for the 1M retry after a 2M setup stall, and only for a fast HCI 0x3E failure (GATT 133 or 62 within 1.5 s): up to 4 GATT dials in total, 1.5 s apart, each starting inside the call's 15 s early-loss window, instead of 2 dials 500 ms apart. Every other failure and every other connect path keep the ordinary rule (the stall retry's own `SetupDeadline`). GATT transport only. Logs `BENCHMARK stall_retry_dial: ... delayMs= \| remainingMs=` or `action=ordinary_rule` (review T3, 2026-10-27: out of stall-retry dials the ordinary rule still decides, so a stall retry never gets fewer dials than another connect; it was `action=give_up`). |
| Bad-2M memory keyed by a recognised contact (`test_phy_memory_contact_key=false` is the control arm) [phy_memory_contact_key] | On | A call offer with rotating IDs (pseudonyms) changes its advertised ID every epoch, so the bad-2M pair memory, looked up under that ID but written under the authenticated node after a 1M retry, never matched and every call repeated the 3 s stall. A rotating offer recognised as exactly one contact (local presence-lease recognition) is now looked up, and recorded at the stall, under that contact's node label; an unrecognised one has no memory and keeps the requested PHY. The memory stays in the phone's local preferences and is never sent. The connect log shows the move (`reason=remembered \| ... \| key=contact`). Phones that are not each other's contacts are unaffected. Since 2026-10-26 a relayed-call helper also looks up its next hop's rotating offer this way (it used the epoch's ID before, which no entry is written under); it still never records from a relaying hop. |
| Per-direction GATT PHY (`test_gatt_phy_split=tx2m-rx1m` or `tx1m-rx2m`) [gatt_phy_split] | Off (Experimental) | The GATT client asks for different PHYs per direction (`setPreferredPhy(tx, rx)`); applies to the 2M default only (a peer remembered bad on 2M and a `test_ble_phy` arm keep their PHY). `tx2m-rx1m` keeps the central's media direction on 2M and moves the peripheral-to-central direction, the one that went silent on the A15s-central/ASUS-peripheral pair, to 1M; `tx1m-rx2m` is the mirror, so a pair of runs names the failing direction. Logs `BENCHMARK gatt_phy_split` and the observed `PHY TX/RX` line. The controller may refuse an asymmetric pair and keep the old PHY; the observed PHY line is the result. Phone-measured: it helps the A15s <-> ASUS pair but costs or does not help healthy pairs, so it stays a non-default arm (at most a DeviceFamilyPolicy proposal for mt6765, which needs outside evidence). |
| 2M requested only after hop authentication (`test_phy_after_auth`) [phy_after_auth] | Off (Experimental) | The stream source's GATT link opens on 1M and asks for 2M only once the hop peer is authenticated, unless the authenticated node is remembered bad on 2M (`BENCHMARK phy_after_auth`). The hop handshake never runs on a 2M link, and the bad-2M memory is read by the authenticated node, which a rotating advertised prefix (the default in user builds) can never be matched to before connecting. Stream sender only; calls and messages keep the connect-time request. A link lost before its deferred 2M request is not counted as a 2M loss (fixed 2026-10-27; before, it could mark the peer bad on 2M). |
| Direct calls connect on LE 1M first (`test_call_1m_first`) [call_1m_first] | Off (Experimental, 2026-11-10) | A direct outgoing GATT call opens on LE 1M and makes no PHY request before the call is active (`BENCHMARK link_phy requested=1M effective=1M reason=call_1m_first`). After the first shown remote picture it asks the live link for 2M only when the call's planned load (video, audio and headers with the planner's 20% margin) exceeds what GATT carries on 1M (320 kbps, the measured 1M figure behind `LinkPacerSeed`) (`reason=rate_above_budget`, with `plannedKbps` and `budgetKbps`). When its sender reports two congested sample windows in a row the call stays on 1M and the rate controller handles the congestion (`reason=link_limit_held`, logged once); asking for 2M then is the Experimental `test_call_1m_first_link_limit`. A peer that goes silent for 2 s within 5 s of that upgrade is moved back to 1M on the same link and remembered bad on 2M by its authenticated node (`reason=call_upgrade_early_loss`). The 2M -> 1M setup retry and the 3 s setup-stall guard are inert for such a dial. Dialing GATT central only; relays, streams, CoC, Classic, Wi-Fi and the callee keep today's behaviour, and authentication and encryption are unchanged. |
| First-1M calls ask for 2M at their link limit (`test_call_1m_first_link_limit`) [call_1m_first_link_limit] | Off (Experimental, 2026-11-11) | The first version of `test_call_1m_first` / `test_call_1m_first_coc`: besides a planned load above the 1M budget, the sender's link limit (two congested sample windows in a row) also asks the live link for 2M after the first picture (`reason=link_limit`). Off, such a call stays on 1M. Kept as the control arm: congestion on 1M is no evidence that 2M works on that pair. |
| Direct CoC calls connect on LE 1M first (`test_call_1m_first_coc`) [call_1m_first_coc] | Off (Experimental, 2026-11-11) | The same start as `test_call_1m_first` for a direct outgoing BLE L2CAP CoC call: the CoC control link asks for LE 1M after the PSM read (the request a peer remembered bad on 2M already gets; `BENCHMARK link_phy requested=1M effective=1M reason=call_1m_first`), and after the first shown remote picture the call asks the same live control link for 2M only when its planned load exceeds the 1M budget or its sender reports a link limit. The budget is GATT's 1M figure (320 kbps); CoC carries more on 1M, so a CoC call may ask for 2M slightly earlier than it needs, never later. The early-loss fallback to 1M in place and the inert setup retry and setup-stall guard are as for GATT. Independent of `test_call_1m_first`, which covers GATT only. Dialing CoC central only; dual listening, relays, streams, Classic, Wi-Fi and the callee keep today's behaviour, and authentication and encryption are unchanged. |
| Own-controller 2M memory for unrecognised peers (`test_own_2m_memory`) [own_2m_memory] | Off (Experimental, 2026-11-11) | The bad-2M memory is kept per peer, so a peer with no memory key (an unrecognised rotating offer, the normal case for a stranger when IDs rotate) is never looked up and every call to one starts on symmetric 2M again. With this switch, a phone whose own symmetric 2M call setups as the dialing central stall twice in a row within 24 h (the 3 s setup-stall guard, with no clean symmetric 2M setup in between; `BENCHMARK adaptive_phy: link=memory \| own_controller_2m=marked \| stalls=2/2 \| windowH=24 \| ttlH=168`) writes a local mark about its own controller. While the mark is fresh (the bad-2M marks' expiry: 7 days, 24 h with `test_ceiling_probe_recovery`; a further stall refreshes it), links to peers with no memory key start on full LE 1M (`adaptive_phy: ... node=none \| from=2M to=1M \| reason=own_controller_2m \| at=connect`); two clean symmetric 2M setups as central in a row clear it (`own_controller_2m=cleared`). Peers with a memory key, relayed offers and the callee keep their per-peer rules unchanged. The mark is never sent and names no peer; the learned-state reset clears it. Only a symmetric 2M dial to an idle hop counts (a direct offer or a relayed route's idle helper; not a `test_ble_phy` arm, a per-direction split or a 1M-first dial). Full 1M, not a one-direction split: the clean split results come from streams with one media direction, a call carries media both ways, and the split is GATT-only. Expected effect: a phone with an unreliable controller stops repeating the 3 s stall, the 1M retry and the rediscovery on every call to a stranger. Risk: the measured fault is pair-specific (A15s central to ASUS peripheral; A15s to S22 rang 4 of 4 with no stall and reached symmetric 2M on CoC), but the mark applies to every unrecognised peer, so while it is fresh an A15s -> S22 call drops to 1M needlessly; a phone with a transient bad 2M also stays on 1M for strangers until the mark expires or is cleared; two stalls with the same bad peer count (a stranger cannot be told apart). Cost: the lower 1M throughput ceiling. A/B: A15s -> ASUS and A15s -> S22, user launch, idle callee 12 s, arm on vs off, 6 or more calls per arm; metric dial -> ringing and the FAILED_JOIN count (A15s -> S22 is the cost side: same ringing time and no 2M loss expected). |
| Slow 2M call setup moves to 1M in place (`test_slow_2m_in_place`) [slow_2m_in_place] | Off (Experimental, 2026-11-11) | When the caller's first symmetric LE 2M link to a direct peer with no memory key (an unrecognised rotating offer, the normal case for a stranger when IDs rotate) stalls or crawls during the handshake, the caller asks that same live link for LE 1M and keeps the handshake running instead of closing it at the 3 s guard. Crawl, from what the caller observes: no hop authentication 1.5 s after the link opened, the end-to-end handshake not done 2 s after hop authentication, or no byte from the peer for 1.5 s. GATT asks the call's own client; LE CoC asks the control GATT connection the socket rides on (one ACL per pair); both go through the link's LL procedure gate, and 1M counts as confirmed only when the stack reports it after the request (readPhy polled at 200 ms then every 400 ms, 2 s bound). Before hop authentication the link is closed into today's 2M -> 1M setup retry only after the 3 s guard, and only when 1M was not confirmed within 2 s, the handshake made no progress for 1.5 s after the confirmation, or hop authentication is still missing 6 s after the link opened; the switch never closes earlier than today. After hop authentication nothing is closed (today closes nothing there either): an unconfirmed or stuck move ends the watch and the existing deadlines apply. When that fallback close happens, the retry's rediscovery scan lasts until the callee's 20 s incoming-handshake deadline has released the dead link (20 s minus the time since the link opened, plus the callee's restart, 1.5 s on GATT and 0 on CoC, plus 2 s scan slack, at most 23 s, never shorter than today's 17 s CoC / 18.5 s GATT). Lines: `BENCHMARK adaptive_phy: link=call \| node=... \| phy=2M \| reason=slow_2m_in_place \| trigger=hop_crawl\|endpoint_crawl\|no_progress\|setup_stall \| outcome=moved\|confirmed\|fallback_close\|unresolved\|not_two_m \| sinceOpenMs=...` (`outcome=rediscover_window` with `budgetMs`/`baseMs` for the longer scan), and `BENCHMARK link_phy requested=2M effective=1M reason=slow_2m_in_place` when the moved link set up. Recognised peers, relayed offers, the remembered and early-loss paths, the callee's incoming-handshake code and deadlines, and the handshake content, authentication and encryption are unchanged: only the PHY of the caller's own link and its own timers change. Expected effect: a stalled or crawling A15s -> ASUS call rings on the first link a few seconds after the stall instead of after a 9.8-14.9 s rediscovery, and the calls that never rang (the callee held its dead handshake past the caller's scan) are found by the longer scan. Risk: a healthy but briefly quiet 2M setup is moved to 1M for the whole call (the lower 1M ceiling; healthy setups authenticate in 0.2-0.5 s and finish the end-to-end layer 0.3-1.1 s later, so the 1.5-2 s triggers leave a margin); a move that does not help delays today's close by 0.5 s (unconfirmed) to 3 s (confirmed but never authenticated); a callee that is really gone is reported up to 2-3 s later after a fallback close. A/B: A15s -> ASUS, user launch, idle callee 12 s, GATT and CoC, arm on vs off, 6 or more calls per arm in randomised order; metrics: calls that rang and dial -> ringing, plus the `slow_2m_in_place` outcomes (the arm took effect only where `outcome=moved` appears). Preservation: A15s -> S22 and S22 -> ASUS, same settings: same ringing time, no `outcome=moved` expected on healthy setups and symmetric 2M kept. |
| Adaptive per-direction GATT PHY (`test_adaptive_phy_split=true`, mirror `test_adaptive_phy_split_dir=tx1m-rx2m`) [adaptive_phy_split] | Off (Experimental) | Links start on the normal 2M preference. Where the adaptive PHY would move a GATT link from 2M to 1M (`one_pdu_per_event` or `stalled_one_way`), the central instead asks for the split that keeps its own transmit direction (the media writer it measures) on 2M and moves only the peripheral -> central direction to 1M (`setPreferredPhy` tx2M/rx1M). Why that side: a lock-step connection event ends when either direction fails, so the writer's slowness cannot name the failing direction; the only snoop evidence of a silent direction is peripheral -> central, the static split `tx2m-rx1m` removed both patterns, and it leaves the large media direction on 2M. The split is tried once per link, the decision is re-armed, and a link that still fails moves to full 1M with the old memory and rollback (`after_split=true`). Applies to GATT only, not to relay child links (`relay_one_pdu` and `relay_scheduling` keep their paths), a link that already started split (`test_gatt_phy_split`) or a `test_ble_phy` arm. A successful split is not remembered as bad on 2M (`memory=none`), so a later link to the same peer pays the detection again. Line `BENCHMARK adaptive_phy: link=gatt ... from=2M to=split-tx2m-rx1m (or split-tx1m-rx2m) \| reason=one_pdu_per_event\|stalled_one_way \| queued=true \| memory=none`; the old full-move lines gain `after_split=true` only after a split. Needs `test_adaptive_phy`. Off is the existing behaviour exactly. |
| Per-direction adaptive PHY split that names the failing direction (`test_phy_direction_split`) [phy_direction_split] | Off (Experimental, 2026-11-07) | Design decision 2026-11-06. A GATT central's 2M link moves only the failing direction to 1M (`setPreferredPhy(tx, rx)`) and keeps the other on 2M, and the split that worked is remembered per peer beside the bad-2M marks (the same 7-day expiry and learned-state reset), so the next call or stream link to that peer asks for it at setup. Direction: when the peer, heard at least every 1.5 s for 4 s, goes silent for 3 s while this phone's own writes still complete (`rx_silent`), the receive direction moves (TX 2M / RX 1M); it counts as proven when the peer is heard again within 3 s, otherwise the line says `proven=false` and nothing is remembered or moved further (a peer that left is no evidence about 2M). A backlogged writer (`one_pdu_per_event`, or `stalled_one_way` while the peer is heard) cannot name the direction, because a connection event ends when either direction fails: step 1 moves the receive direction and keeps this phone's media direction on 2M, step 2 tries the mirror (TX 1M / RX 2M), step 3 is the old full move to 1M with the bad-2M memory (`after_split=true`). The split that the next decision keeps is recorded. Lines: `BENCHMARK adaptive_phy: link=gatt ... to=split-tx2m-rx1m \| reason=rx_silent\|one_pdu_per_event\|stalled_one_way \| direction=rx\|tx \| step=1\|2 \| queued=true \| memory=pending`; `adaptive_phy: link=memory ... split=tx2m-rx1m \| recorded=true`; on the next link `adaptive_phy: link=call\|stream ... reason=remembered_split \| at=connect` and `gatt_phy_split: ... source=memory`; the observed PHY is the `LE link: ... observed=tx2M/rx1M` line. Hop-local, no wire change; off is the existing behaviour exactly. Untested on phones. **Requires / masked by:** `test_adaptive_phy` (default on; off makes it inert), and `test_adaptive_phy_memory` for the memory. GATT central links only: not CoC, not a relay's own child link, not with `test_gatt_phy_split` or `test_ble_phy`; a `test_phy_after_auth` link does not start split. With it on, `test_adaptive_phy_split` is not consulted (inert). |
| Stalled 2M writer moves to 1M (`test_stalled_2m_move=false` is the control arm) [stalled_2m_move] | On | A GATT link whose backlogged writer carried under a quarter of one PDU per event (under 33 kbps at 15 ms) for the whole 3-window decision while the peer's data is still arriving (any record within 4 s) moves to 1M and the peer is remembered bad on 2M (`BENCHMARK adaptive_phy: link=gatt ... reason=stalled_one_way`). Before, such a link was `kept` on 2M for the rest of the session, because the one-PDU rule exempts stalls (a relay's downstream link, mesh4-7a6c401c). A relay's own links and a link the peer is not heard on keep 2M and remember nothing. Needs `test_adaptive_phy`. |
| Skip the backlog at a PHY move (`test_phy_move_skip_backlog=false` is the control arm, `=true` forces it on) [phy_move_skip_backlog] | On for live streams with a fixed (manual) bitrate; off with Auto quality, calls and audio-only live (default-on since 2026-10-14, design decision Q-SKIP1) | When a live stream sender moves its own link from 2M to 1M (`one_pdu_per_event` or `stalled_one_way`), queued video older than twice the viewer's playout (at least 4 s) is dropped once, codec-safely: it resumes at a queued keyframe still inside the playout, else drops arriving inter frames until a requested keyframe arrives (at most 15 s). Audio, codec configuration, rotation and lifecycle records are never dropped; calls (their bounded-age experiment is separate), audio-only live and relay forwarders never skip. Logs `BENCHMARK phy_move_skip: ... skipped= | skippedRecords= | skippedAgeMs= | thresholdMs=`. Why: the records queued while the link crawled on the bad PHY cannot be absorbed by the viewer's playout; the 6.7 s queue of an ASUS source drained to only 4.5 s in 60 s on 1M, and a fixed-rate stream above the new link's rate grows it again (the skip is not rate control). Evidence: a fixed 300 kbps ASUS-to-A15s stream, three runs each, froze for 64.5 / 59.8 / 25.8 percent of the time without the skip and 9.6 / 9.1 / 8.8 percent with it (about 100 records, 6.7 s of video, skipped once per run; the longest single stall did not improve). With Auto quality on the skip gave no benefit (7.3 / 8.6 / 5.8 percent without, 5.2 / 7.2 / 7.4 with), so it stays off there. It trades one visible jump for much less freezing; the app's default of delay over frame loss still holds elsewhere (no-loss stays the default, bounded age stays Experimental). |
| PHY-move flood guard (`test_phy_move_flood_guard=false` is the control arm) [phy_move_flood_guard] | On | At a PHY move (`stalled_one_way` or `one_pdu_per_event`) a live stream sender drops queued media older than 8 s once instead of writing it into the viewer at link speed: video codec-safely (it resumes at a queued keyframe still inside the viewer's playout, else after a requested one) and audio older than the viewer's playout (the viewer sees a sender gap and realigns). Codec configuration and lifecycle records are never dropped; calls and audio-only live never. `BENCHMARK phy_move_skip: ... kind=flood_guard | skippedRecords= | skippedAudio=`. Correctness, not preference: the viewer's receive path cannot take a multi-ten-second burst. The backlog skip (`test_phy_move_skip_backlog`, default on for fixed-rate streams) keeps its lower 4 s video threshold and keeps audio. |
| GATT input failure ends the link (`test_gatt_input_failure_ends_link=false` is the control arm) [gatt_input_failure_ends_link] | On | When a GATT video receiver's input pipe fails (overflow, bad chunk header, reordering) it cancels the connection (this applies to the pipe of the GATT server side, the phone that accepted the link; a client-side pipe is unchanged), so the sender sees the loss and the existing redial and re-request recover. Before, the pipe was closed but the link stayed up and the viewer's heartbeats kept the sender satisfied. The input pipe is now bounded by bytes (1 MiB) before chunks (4,096; it was 512 chunks, about 40 KB of small audio records). |
| A delivered recovery control write counts as the stage-2 first write (`test_gatt_first_write_counts_control`) [gatt_first_write_counts_control] | On (promoted 2026-11-10; false is the control arm) | When the Android 10-11 call listener has admitted a client's subscribe, its stage-2 watch expects the client's first data write within 1.2 s; a well-formed recovery control write (`GR1` hello or high) that the server actually received now counts as that write too, so the watch no longer cancels the connection and re-registers the server on a link whose writes did reach the app. A data write, an empty value, a malformed `GR1` and a nack/gone do not count. The held advertising set is released by the watch's own end (about 1.2 s after the subscribe) rather than at the control write. Reason: the A15s -> ASUS pair (Android 10 central, Android 11 peripheral) had the ASUS receive and log the recovery hello and then `gatt_server_first_write_missing` at +1.2 s; the cancel does not drop the dead ACL and the re-registered set can be deaf, so the retry succeeded 4 of 8 times after the watch versus 4 of 4 without it (small n). Off is today's data-only rule. Hop-local, no wire change. Measured A15s (Android 10) dialing ASUS (Android 11 server), GATT, n=6 per arm: calls active 6/6 vs 4/6 and 5/6 vs 4/6 in two runs, GATT 133 failures 10 vs 83 and 5 vs 19, the false "first write missing" re-registration gone; ASUS dialing A15s unchanged (5/6 vs 6/6, the miss was missing log evidence). **Requires / masked by:** the legacy-stack entry `android11-gatt-server-connection-untracked` (Android 10-11; `test_device_policy_off` removes both stages). |
| GATT re-registration keeps the advertising address (`test_gatt_reregister_keep_address=false` is the control arm) [gatt_reregister_keep_address] | On (Android 10-11 legacy stack, part of `android11-gatt-server-connection-untracked`) | When the call listener registers its GATT server again (no PHY answer within 400 ms, or no first data write within 1.2 s of the subscribe), only the server is replaced: the advertising set is a separate stack object and keeps running with the same address, so the caller's retry finds it at once instead of waiting for a new set's advert. To keep it for the second stage, the set stays on after a subscribe until the client's first data write (normally 70-140 ms, at most 1.2 s) instead of stopping at the subscribe. A rotating-ID epoch change always stops the running set, also one held through an admission, so an address never carries into a new epoch; a re-registration during the new ID's silence leaves the start to the silence. The control arm stops the set at the subscribe and starts a new set after a re-registration. Both watch lines also log what the app can see of the registration table (its own registrations, server age and idle time); other apps' registrations are not visible to an app. |
| Lost GATT chunk ends the stream at once (`test_gatt_gap_fast_fail=false` is the control arm) [gatt_gap_fast_fail] | On | A GATT receiver's input pipe used to treat a missing chunk as reordering and park up to 4,096 later chunks, delivering nothing until the 8 s call liveness check ended the call. The sender never reorders, so a gap held 1 s or behind 128 later chunks is now a loss: the pipe fails with "GATT chunk N was lost (M later chunks waited T ms)" and a call ends with that reason. It does not recover the chunk (DECISIONS "Design GR"). Cause seen: a host that drops a short ACL start fragment (Android 11 legacy fragmenter). Release builds keep it on. |
| Lost GATT chunk recovery `GR1` (`test_gatt_chunk_recovery`) [gatt_chunk_recovery] | On (design decision 2026-10-21; `false` is the control arm) | The reader asks for a GATT value lost in its host (a NACK in a type-2 value that older readers ignore) and the sender resends the stored value byte for byte. The sender keeps a history of 2 s, 1,024 values or 256 KiB; resends are capped at 25% of the link's measured budget. The reader waits only after the peer's HELLO, with a 1 s deadline after 3 requests, and keeps the fail-fast above otherwise. Callbacks only queue; a per-link writer thread sends. Off: no history, no thread, nothing sent. Wire rules: PROTOCOL "GATT byte stream and draft lost-chunk recovery"; reasons and review 1: DECISIONS "Design GR". Windows not built. |
| DEBUG: drop received GATT chunks (`test_gatt_drop_received_every=N`, 0 = off) [gatt_drop_received_every] | 0 | Every GATT reader drops the first copy of each received data value whose sequence is N-1 mod N, as if its host had lost it, and logs `gatt_debug_drop`. It forces the fail-fast or the recovery above on any pair. Release builds ignore it. |
| Hop-integrity resend diff (`test_hop_integrity_resend_diff`) [hop_integrity_resend_diff] | Off (Experimental, diagnostic) | After a `BMS1` record fails its tag on a GATT link whose peer announced `GR1`, the reader asks the peer once for the chunks that carried the record (one NACK; the sender resends the values it still holds, unchanged), waits at most 300 ms, compares each resent value byte for byte with what it received and logs `hop_integrity_diff: chunk= | firstOffset= | bytes= | pattern=bitflip|run|fragment251|whole_value|none` plus one `hop_integrity_diff_summary` line (no payload bytes), then ends the link exactly as before. Nothing from a resent copy is decrypted, delivered or used to repair the record; authentication, exact-next ordering and the fail-closed end are unchanged. Reason: a tag failure with header and length intact and no chunk gap points to a same-length change of bytes below the app on one phone; this shows which bytes and in what shape. DEBUG builds only (release ignores it). |
| Nearby list: "Bluetooth needs a restart" (`test_scan_restart_hint=false` is the control arm) [scan_restart_hint] | On | When the Nearby scanner is refused a registration three back-off steps in a row (about 7 s; scan error 2, the phone-wide pool of 32 GATT registrations is full), the empty list says "Bluetooth needs a restart to find nearby people" with an "Open Bluetooth settings" button instead of "No one found yet". It goes away at the scanner's first result. An app cannot free registrations leaked by other processes or switch Bluetooth off and on (API 33+), so this is guidance only; the scanner's own back-off is unchanged. |
| Copied-ID warning ignores a PC's legacy and extended twin (`test_copied_id_pc_pair=false` is the control arm) [copied_id_pc_pair] | On | Calling a Windows PC no longer shows "More than one phone is using this ID / Call anyway": Windows sends its compact call offer as one legacy and one extended advertising set from two addresses. Exactly two recent addresses, one received as legacy and one as extended (with a secondary PHY), whose Flags and UUID-list ADs are byte-identical and carry `0x180A`, count as one sender. An address that a newer one of the same form replaced is not counted, so one set restarting with a new address is not a copy. A third address, two of the same kind or any difference still warns. Turned off if V1 finds `0x180A` in an iPhone or Mac advert. Design decision Q-PC-COPIED-ID option A; DECISIONS "Design CI" (review 1). |
| Copied-ID warning needs overlapping addresses (`test_copied_id_overlap=false` is the control arm) [copied_id_overlap] | On | "More than one phone is using this ID" now needs two addresses whose sightings interleave (the other address heard again after this one appeared), on two occasions. Before, a phone that replaced its advertising set (a new random address 31 ms after the old one stopped, as Android 10 does when the Meet code boosts the advert and again when the boost ends) was heard on two addresses within 2 s, twice, and every call to it asked "Call anyway". Two senders on the air at once (a copy, or a PC's legacy and extended twin) still interleave and still warn. |
| A dial follows its person across a rotating-ID epoch (`test_dial_follows_rotation=false` is the control arm) [dial_follows_rotation] | On | A user's dial waits for the next sighting of the row they tapped. Across a 15-minute rotating-ID boundary that person announces a new ID, so the dial waited for a key that no longer existed and failed after 10 s. Now a sighting continues the dial when its ID is recognised as the same contact, or when it comes from the same advertising address. The call's key check still decides who answers. A rotating row whose address now announces another rotating ID is the same phone's previous epoch and leaves the list at once instead of staying tappable until it expires. |
| Crash reports ("Don't ask again" in the prompt; DEBUG `test_crash_reports=false`) [crash_reports] | On | An uncaught exception writes a short scrubbed report (error types, code locations, app and Android version, phone model; names, quoted text, addresses, IDs, keys, numbers, links and paths replaced) to private storage. The next plain launch asks "MeshLive closed unexpectedly. Share a crash report?" before any radio opens, and Export diagnostics includes the report for 7 days. Nothing is sent by the app. Benchmark and debug launches are not interrupted. |
| Relay stall hold-off (`test_relay_stall_holdoff=false` is the control arm) [relay_stall_holdoff] | On | After the relay child stall guard closed a child link, sightings of that child for the next 10 s are not dialed and not counted as establishment failures (`BENCHMARK relay_child_stall: ... action=holdoff`). The child is still timing out its dead link and tearing its receiver down; the advertisement it shows meanwhile belongs to the receiver that is going away, and two 5 s status-133 dials to it used to add the second establishment failure that withdraws the relay's offer for 5 minutes. Needs `test_relay_child_stall_guard`. Refusal escalation keeps its own hold-off, so this control arm changes only the stall hold-off (fixed 2026-10-27; before, both shared one map that only this switch read). |
| Relay stalls counted apart (`test_relay_stall_separate=false` is the control arm) [relay_stall_separate] | On | A hop-stage stall caught by the relay child stall guard (the dead-anchor signature: the link is silent after the connection update) no longer counts as an establishment refusal. Stalls need 3 within 2 minutes and mark the child for 30 s, then 60, 120 and 300 s on consecutive marks without a success (`relay_parent_fallback ... kind=stall`, `action=count_stall` for a counted stall that does not mark); refusals (status 133/62) keep 2 failures and the 5-minute mark and are not added to by stalls. Off counts a stall as a refusal, as before. Needs `test_relay_establish_fallback` and `test_relay_child_stall_guard`. **Requires / masked by:** `relay_establish_fallback` and `relay_child_stall_guard`, as written above. |
| Relay refusal escalation (`test_relay_refusal_escalation=false` is the control arm) [relay_refusal_escalation] | On | A relay's refusal marks (GATT status 133/62 toward a child, the stack's HCI 0x3E) escalate 30, 60, 120, then 300 s on consecutive marks without a success instead of a flat 5 minutes, and a refusal round holds the relay's next dial to that child off for 10 s (`relay_child_stall: ... action=holdoff`), so the two failures are no longer spent in 3 s while the child is rediscovered every second. A successful link clears the level. Needs `test_relay_establish_fallback`. |
| Connection-priority retry off (`test_le_priority_retry=false`) [le_priority_retry] | Retry on | Control arm. A central repeats its own priority request up to three times (0.5/1.5/4 s) after the stack reports the LE connection update failed (0x23 collision, 0x3B unacceptable parameters: ASUS 16, S24 39 in saved Bluetooth logs), which otherwise leaves the link at its 45-49 ms creation interval. Needs the hidden `onConnectionUpdated` report; no report means no retry. Line `BENCHMARK le_priority_retry`. Untested on phones. |
| Setup PHY after the priority update (`test_phy_after_priority`; DEBUG only) [phy_after_priority] | Off | Experimental. An LE central's setup PHY request (the LE CoC control client, including the reattach after a fresh ACL, and the GATT video client) waits while the link's last reported connection interval is outside the requested priority window: below it (the 7.5 ms interval Android uses during service discovery) or still at the pre-request interval. It is sent 100 ms after the update that brings the link into the window, or after 1.5 s at the latest; a link closed meanwhile never sends it. With no report yet, or a link already in the window, the PHY goes out at once as before. Why kept: it was built because relay child links of an Android 12 helper died after a PHY update that landed between the discovery 7.5 ms update and the requested 15 ms update; the phone A/B did not confirm that cause (child-link deaths with it in 2 of 12 relayed calls, without it in 1 of 11; every death followed the link's update to 15 ms with or without a PHY update, the dual-role pattern in DECISIONS "Relay dual-role scheduling"). No harm was shown, so it stays as an arm. Lines: `BENCHMARK phy_after_priority: link= | event=hold | label= | lastMs= | windowMs= | boundMs=` and `event=release | reason=settled/bound/inactive/superseded | waitedMs=`; the switch is logged in `BENCHMARK le_link_policy` as `phyAfterPriority`. **Requires / masked by:** `coc_sequenced_phy` (CoC only; off requests the PHY at connection, outside this hold), `coc_phy_after_connect` and the GATT PHY-after-auth arm (they defer the PHY past setup), `ll_gate` (when on, the held request still enters the gate), `psm_read_link_quiet` (a quiet PSM-read link sends no PHY at all). |
| Quiet PSM-read link (`test_psm_read_link_quiet`; DEBUG only) [psm_read_link_quiet] | Off | Experimental A/B arm. On a phone that connects LE CoC on a fresh ACL (Android 12 and 13, `coc_fresh_acl`), the GATT link that only reads the PSM and is then closed asks for no connection priority, PHY or MTU. Android's service discovery still moves it to 7.5 ms and back to its creation interval, but it no longer reaches the requested 15 ms. Why: relay helper links to the callee that died one supervision timeout after their update to 15 ms left that ACL up past the 2 s fresh-ACL wait, so the CoC connect waited about 4 s more for the dead link's timeout before opening a new link. The arm separates two readings: if the late ACL drop is the fault, calls pass with it; if the standing dual-role anchor phase is (DECISIONS "Relay dual-role scheduling"), the call's next link still dies at its own 15 ms update and the call fails about 5 s sooner. Line: `BENCHMARK psm_read_link_quiet: link=ble-control | event=skip | peer= | priority=skipped | phy=skipped | mtu=skipped`; logged in `BENCHMARK le_link_policy` as `psmReadLinkQuiet`. **Requires / masked by:** `coc_fresh_acl` (without a fresh ACL the PSM-read link is the call's control link and keeps its requests). |
| CoC control PHY at connection (`test_coc_sequenced_phy=false`) [coc_sequenced_phy] | Sequenced | Control arm. The CoC control client requests the PHY after its priority update and MTU exchange, and the control server only reads it, as the GATT video path does. The old order (both sides at connection, beside the priority update) logged 339 `BLE PHY unavailable (status 42)` on M31, ASUS and A15s. Untested on phones. |
| CoC PHY after connect (`test_coc_phy_after_connect`) [coc_phy_after_connect] | Off | Experimental A/B arm. The CoC central requests no PHY during setup (the control server never does); after the CoC socket is connected and hop authentication finished, it requests the preferred PHY and waits for `onPhyUpdate`, with a `readPhy` fallback after 1 s as Nordic's Android-BLE-Library does (BleManagerHandler L2958-2985/L3838-3860, 46d17813), before the end-to-end handshake and media. Applies to stream sender, forwarder child, call (caller), call relay and video relay CoC links. Line `BENCHMARK link_phy: link=... \| requested=2M \| effective=2M\|1M\|failed(status N)\|unknown\|not_queued \| reason=after_connect \| source=update\|read\|none`. Why: a CoC connect overlapped the 2M PHY update (A15s -> ASUS CoC connect failed 6 ms after the request); Nordic treats a link failing right after an early 2M switch as a controller interop fault (Kotlin-BLE-Library Peripheral.kt L1056-1074). Cost: hop authentication runs on 1M and up to 1.5 s more setup. Off: the sequenced request after the PSM read. Untested on phones. |
| Peer address book (`test_peer_address_book`) [peer_address_book] | Off | Experimental. Every accepted scan result refreshes its node-ID prefix's freshest address. A direct LE call to an address older than 10 s, replaced by a newer address of the same node, or never seen rescans for the node first (same prefix and route cost, through the scan-start gate; the handshake verifies identity). The first failed attempt is classified by elapsed time as Nordic does (retry only failures under 20 s, at most twice, 300 ms apart; a 133 after 25 s or more is a stale address: rescan, not a blind retry); the class is logged only. Why: Android rotates its resolvable private address every 7-15 min and at every new advertising set (android11-release `btm_ble_addr.cc` 96-102, `btm_ble_multi_adv.cc` 240-267); a connect to a dead address ends only at the ~30 s direct-connect timeout as status 133 (`connection_manager.cc` 33). Not done: streams and relays still connect to the scanned address. Untested on phones. |
| Android 11 and older CoC SDU coalescing (`test_coc_coalesce_sdus`) [coc_coalesce_sdus] | Off | Experimental, scoped by the inert registry proposal `android-11-coc-small-sdu-coalescing` (API 30 and older). Where it matches, CoC media SDUs are up to 8,000 bytes instead of 245-byte single-PDU SDUs (a 50 KB keyframe: 7 SDUs instead of 205), without pacing or drops; elsewhere the switch logs `reason=stack_not_matched`. Line `BENCHMARK coc_sdu: requested=coalesce\|single_pdu\|socket_limit \| effective=... \| sduBytes=...`. Why: Android 11 btif reads one message per SDU into its own buffer, L2CAP counts congestion in queued SDUs and a congested write is freed (`bta_jv_act.cc` 1182, `l2c_main.cc` 997-1007; upstream 471249f4, 46bd51ed); a candidate for the ASUS Bluetooth crash, which has no backtrace yet. It replaces `test_coc_single_pdu_sdus` on matching stacks, so the 251+4-octet keyframe split returns for MPS-251 peers; the app cannot see or cap the stack's own SDU queue. Untested on phones. |
| Forwarding CoC SDU coalescing on Android 12 and older (`test_relay_coc_coalesce`) [relay_coc_coalesce] | Off | Experimental, scoped by the inert registry proposal `pre-android-13-coc-congested-write-loss` (API 32 and older). Where it matches, forwarding CoC writers (the private-call relay's own sockets, stream relays and forwarders, and a helper's parent link from the moment it becomes a relay hop) write SDUs of up to 8,000 bytes, never more than the peer's receive limit, instead of 245-byte single-PDU SDUs. Endpoint links are unchanged; nothing is paced or dropped and the peer reads the same bytes. Lines `BENCHMARK coc_sdu: ... \| scope=forwarding \| relayCoalesce=requested\|off \| relayEffective=...` and `coc_sdu: scope=forwarding_handoff \| link=call-relay-parent \| sduBytesBefore=... \| sduBytes=...`. Why: before Android 13 the Bluetooth socket thread could read stale poll entries while several sockets were busy and drop data it read for a congested channel (AOSP 46bd51ed, b/223974328); congestion is counted in queued SDUs, so fewer, larger SDUs reach it less often. Every LE CoC hop-integrity failure in the relayed-call runs had a forwarding phone on Android 10-12 as the transmitter, and none was seen on a direct link. Diagnostics that are always on (no switch): a failed hop record logs `hop_integrity: kind=tag\|sequence\|header \| ... \| next_header=valid_n1\|valid_other\|invalid\|unavailable` (a changed byte leaves the next header intact, a lost SDU shifts it; the peek never waits for bytes), each private-call relay direction logs `relay_forwarded: direction=... \| read=... \| forwarded=... \| destinationRecordsWritten=...` when it stops, and the user-visible error no longer carries the record type, sequence or size. Untested on phones. |
| Shared LE CoC listener (`test_coc_shared_listener=true` or `false` forces it) | Android 10 only (registry) | Android 10 never stops a closed LE CoC server (`btsock_l2cap_free_l` lacks `BTA_JvL2capStopServer`; AOSP d3c996662453 added it in Android 11), so every closed listener, graceful or killed, kept a BTA JV security id (18 shared with RFCOMM/L2CAP servers) until Bluetooth restarted; the A15s then refused all LE CoC listens and connects. On that stack family one long-lived listener per role (private call, receiver, relay, call relay, raw benchmark) is leased to sessions; a connection accepted for an earlier or no session is closed. Its idle PSM stays connectable (closing it would leak). Bound: one registration per role per app process, plus one per concurrent same-role session or listener failure. Fixed stacks keep one listener per session. A debug receiver (`DebugBluetoothRestartReceiver`, adb shell only, DUMP-protected, refused during any session) cycles Bluetooth on Android 12 and older. Untested on phones. |
| Advertising restart at the balanced step (`test_stable_adv_address=false`) | Same set, parameters changed in place | Control arm. The 10 s low-latency -> balanced step stopped and restarted the advertising set; with LE privacy on (every reference phone) a new set gets a new resolvable private address, and all four A15s -> S24 connect timeouts dialled the old address within about 0.3 s of that restart. This is the app's own lifecycle race, not a stack regression. The default disables the set, changes interval/TX power and enables it again; the address stays until the stack's privacy timer rotates it. A failed or stalled step falls back to a restart whose start is bounded (8 s); if that fails the owner clears its advertising state and starts once more. Untested on phones. |
| Deferred GATT client close (`test_deferred_gatt_close=true` or `false`) | Android 11 and older only (registry) | Android 10/11 `ContextMap.removeConnectionsByAppId` is unsynchronized (AOSP 1a2c1e97d203 locked it in Android 12); `close()` 22 ms after `disconnect()` threw ConcurrentModificationException in the A15s Bluetooth process, which skips the native unregister. Its GATT discovery later failed until Bluetooth restarted, but the exception is not proven to have caused that. Deferred: disconnect, then close on the disconnect callback or after 2 s; an expired control attempt is cancelled first so its late callbacks do nothing. Line `GATT client closed after disconnect`. Untested on phones. |
| LE CoC client on a fresh ACL (`test_coc_fresh_acl=true` or `false` forces it) | Android 12 and 13 only (registry) | As LE central these stacks start EATT on every link; a peer that refuses it (the S24 on an unencrypted link) fails only the first of the five channels (android12/13 `l2c_ble.cc` 654-663; android14 `l2cble_handle_connect_rsp_neg` fixes it). A later CoC response can match a stranded channel whose EATT registration has no connect callback: the M31 Bluetooth process crashed (SIGSEGV pc 0 in `l2c_csm_indicate_connection_open`) on every saved CoC connect after a refused EATT. The client reads the PSM over GATT, closes that client and waits up to 2 s for the ACL to drop, connects the CoC on a fresh ACL (its channel queued before EATT's) and reattaches a GATT client for priority/PHY. Line `BENCHMARK coc_fresh_acl`. Untested on phones. |
| Automatic BLE GATT when LE CoC fails (no Settings switch since 2026-11-04, a stored choice is ignored; DEBUG `test_coc_auto_gatt`, `test_coc_gatt_threshold` are the benchmark switches) | Promoted 2026-10-11 (default OFF until its phone test passes; one constant, `ConnectivityFeatureDefaults.COC_AUTO_GATT`) | Design decision (2026-10-09). The A15s stack refuses LE CoC listens and connects after about 15 registrations per Bluetooth process. With the flag on, a session that requests BLE L2CAP is moved to BLE GATT, with a visible notice ("Using BLE GATT: L2CAP unavailable"), when call availability (the CoC listen) fails, an outgoing CoC connect fails (that call is retried once over GATT), or the CoC registration count of this Bluetooth-adapter session reaches the threshold (default 12; reset by an adapter off/on broadcast). While on, the call list also shows GATT-advertising phones and a GATT peer is called over GATT for that call. A GATT or Classic request is never changed. Lines `LE CoC registration: kind=listen|connect | count=N`, `BENCHMARK call transport: event=... | requested=... | effective=... | automatic=... | reason=... | cocRegistrations=N | threshold=T`. The registration threshold (12 in user launches, with no setting; DEBUG `test_coc_gatt_threshold` in benchmark launches) moves a session only on a stack that leaks registrations (`DeviceFamilyPolicy.leCocServerLeaks`, Android 10); a benchmark launch always counts it, as measured. On a healthy stack only a real listen or connect failure moves a session, so ordinary use is never downgraded. The notice reads "Using BLE GATT: ..." and says that turning Bluetooth off and on restores L2CAP (the adapter broadcast resets the count). A peer that only listens on L2CAP cannot be reached after a caller-side fallback; both phones need GATT reachability (the experimental dual listening row below, designed in PLAN "Design D", lets a CoC phone also answer over GATT). An untouched switch follows the constant; an explicit choice sticks; it matters only when the transport is BLE L2CAP (Advanced). **Phone test (A15s CoC exhaustion, S24/M31/ASUS as peers), pass when:** (1) the A15s with the switch on completes 20 consecutive call cycles against a GATT-scanning peer, the endpoint moving to GATT with the visible notice no later than the 12th registration or the first failed listen/connect, and `call transport: ... requested=BLE_L2CAP | effective=BLE_GATT` logged; (2) the same cycle with the switch off reproduces the failure after about 15 registrations; (3) Bluetooth off/on returns the next session to L2CAP; (4) an S24 with the switch on keeps L2CAP for 20 cycles (`thresholdActive=false`); (5) a GATT-advertising peer appears in the call list and a call to it completes. Not yet tested on phones. |
| Automatic GATT fallback for streams (`test_coc_auto_gatt_streams`; sub-switch of `coc_auto_gatt`, shipped constant `ConnectivityFeatureDefaults.COC_AUTO_GATT_STREAMS`) | Off (Experimental; does nothing unless `coc_auto_gatt` is on too; a release build ignores the extra) | Design SG (design decision Q50). Stage 0 (always on, text and log only): a stream receiver whose LE CoC listen fails shows "This phone's Bluetooth could not listen for L2CAP streams" and logs `stream_listen_failed` (transport, token, cause, CoC registration count). Stage 1: with both switches on, that receiver restarts on BLE GATT once per adapter session (the same ledger as calls; `requested=BLE_L2CAP effective=BLE_GATT reason=availability_failed`, line `stream_gatt_fallback`) and joins later L2CAP offers over GATT until Bluetooth is turned off and on. It reacts only to its own failed listen, never to a received advertisement, and an L2CAP stream that was already active never restarts. Stage 2: an LE CoC source also accepts GATT viewer requests (same stream-token rule) into a separate GATT child pool (size 1, `StreamGattFallback.GATT_POOL_CAPACITY`) with its own admission: the child must be able to carry the stream's planned rate (else refused, never given another stream), and its airtime is charged against the source's aggregate fan-out budget, which later L2CAP admissions see. L2CAP children are never moved, counted into the pool or evicted; a GATT-pool child does not feed the shared rate controller or the L2CAP ceiling learning, and the starvation guard still applies to it. GATT and Classic sources never accept another transport's request. Hop authentication, ordered encryption and rotating IDs are the same on a GATT child; a direct fallback from a requested relay chain still counts as a failed chain. No new wire byte (PROTOCOL "Source filter"). Lines `stream_gatt_pool: event=admit|refuse`. Untested on phones; tests need the injection below. |
| Stream listen-failure injection (DEBUG `test_coc_listen_inject=stream`) | Off (DEBUG only) | Makes the LE CoC listen of a stream receiver throw, so a healthy phone can exercise stage 0 and stage 1 of the automatic GATT fallback for streams (the real trigger is the Android 10 A15s registration leak). Exact value `stream`; a release build ignores it. The failure is logged `stream_listen_failed ... injected=true`. |
| Partial-open cleanup (DEBUG `test_listen_partial_cleanup=true`) | Off (Experimental, DEBUG only) | A call listener whose open fails after the LE CoC listen succeeded (the control server, say) closes the lease, control server and any other part it opened; a failed rotating-ID or Bluetooth-on reopen then stops availability and reports the endpoint unavailable (with Automatic GATT on, it retries over GATT). Off, the orphaned lease kept the role busy until the endpoint closed, so on Android 10 the next session opened a dedicated listener and leaked one more registration. Logged `call_availability: state=partial_open_released`. Untested on phones. |
| Persisted Android 10 CoC budget (DEBUG `test_coc_ledger_persist=true`) | Off (Experimental, DEBUG only) | On stacks that leak LE CoC servers (Android 10, the registry rule) the registration count of Automatic GATT survives app processes. The Bluetooth process is told apart by its LE PSM allocator, which hands out 0x80 to 0xFF round-robin and starts again at 0x80 after a restart: a listen PSM at or below the stored one, a new boot or an adapter off/on resets the count. Without it the count restarted at 0 in every app process, so the threshold never fired when runs force-stopped the app or Android relaunched it, and the 13th process failed. Logged `coc_budget: event=load/listen/clear`. Untested on phones. |
| Call listen watchdog (DEBUG `test_call_listen_watchdog_ms=<ms>`, 0 turns it off) | **On, 5000 ms** (owner 2026-11-05) | Runs every LE CoC listen (call, receiver, relay) on a worker with a deadline. A listen the stack has not completed in time is an availability failure (`ListenStalledException`), and a socket that returns later is closed. Healthy listens take about 1 ms. Before this, a stack whose main thread stopped kept the listen blocked forever and the phone silently uncallable; now the failure is visible and Automatic GATT can retry (which probably fails too on a fully wedged stack). Logged `call_availability: state=failed | stage=coc_listen | reason=stalled`. Untested on phones. |
| Calls unavailable status (DEBUG `test_calls_unavailable_status=false` turns it off) | **On** (owner 2026-11-05) | When calls are wanted but the endpoint failed, or has been starting for longer than the watchdog deadline (5 s when the watchdog is off), the status indicator says "Calls unavailable" (white, no red) instead of a green Ready or a "Hidden" that reads like a choice. The status sheet's Calls row and Home say why (Bluetooth stopped responding, no free connections, or calls couldn't start) and offer Bluetooth settings, since apps cannot turn Bluetooth off and on. A stalled listen right after a "peer stopped responding" end is logged as this phone's own stack (`call_end_cause=local_stack`). Logged `calls_status: kind=CALLS_UNAVAILABLE`. Untested on phones. |
| Dual listening: a CoC call endpoint also answers over BLE GATT (no Settings switch since 2026-11-04, a stored choice is ignored; DEBUG `test_dual_listening` is the benchmark switch, DEBUG `test_dual_listening_inject=coc_connect|coc_hang` fails or stalls the caller's CoC attempt so the GATT fallback can be tested) | Experimental, designed and coded 2026-10-11 (default OFF until its phone test passes; one constant, `ConnectivityFeatureDefaults.DUAL_LISTENING`) | Follow-up to the CoC-to-GATT safety net. Transport is per endpoint, so a phone that falls back to GATT was reachable only by phones that scan GATT and, being a GATT endpoint, could not list or call a CoC phone at all. With the switch on, an LE CoC endpoint keeps its CoC listener and offer, also starts the GATT call service in a second `BluetoothGattServer` that does not advertise (same advertising set, same address, no extra scan filter), and sets `capabilitySummary` bit 3 (`0x08`) of its call offer once that service runs. Every existing build ignores the bit and connects over CoC as before. A GATT endpoint lists such an offer and calls it over GATT; a CoC caller whose CoC attempt fails (PSM read, connect, or the 9 s CoC phase) opens the GATT service of the same device inside the same attempt, with no rescan, visibly ("Using BLE GATT for this call: L2CAP did not connect") and with `call transport: event=dual_fallback | requested=BLE_L2CAP | effective=BLE_GATT` in the log. The endpoint's link hello, codec and bitrate limits, buffers and policies are those of the link it runs (a per-link transport override); BMH2, BMS1, BME1, the prefix/RDI checks and the rotating-ID openings are unchanged and run on whichever transport answers. Not designed here: GATT-primary dual, streams, relay hops, Classic, iPhones, store-and-forward dialing. Cost: one more GATT server and service while available, one more stable capability bit on air (a quasi-identifier like the relay consent bit). Lines `dual_listening: event=offer | hint= | summary= | secondary=`, `event=secondary | state= | gattServers= | adSets=`, `event=link | accepted=`, `event=attempt_failed`. PROTOCOL "Experimental dual listening", PLAN "Design D". Known limits of the first version (PROTOCOL): the activity's call settings, codec choice, buffer and rate memory follow the primary transport while the hello, limits and link policies follow the link in use; a late CoC accept can make the GATT attempt fail once; exchanges are not carried on the second transport; an accepted link must finish its handshakes within 20 s. **Phone test (the phones available, now S24, M31, ASUS and A15s; S22, Xiaomi and A55 as soon as they are available; the A15s is the CoC-exhaustion phone, not a required scope phone), pass when:** (1) switch off: every phone's announcement `summary` byte (read on the scanners from `dual_listening: event=seen`) and the call matrix equal the previous build; (2) switch on everywhere: every ordered pair of the four phones, both setup roles, completes a call over its primary transport, no pair that connects today fails, and the median connect time (5 calls per pair) is within 1 s of the off matrix; (3) the A15s forced to GATT (`test_coc_auto_gatt` with threshold 1) lists and calls each dual phone over GATT and connects, while a phone with the switch off is not listed; (4) with `test_dual_listening_inject=coc_connect` and with `coc_hang` on the caller, the call connects over GATT inside the same attempt with no `BLE scan start` between the attempts, the fallback line states requested and effective, and the prefix/opening check passes; with both transports refused the failure notice comes within 30 s of the tap and the safety net does not fire; (5) while idle and available there is exactly one advertising set and (`dumpsys bluetooth_manager`) two GATT servers per dual phone, CoC registrations per role are not higher than in the off run, 20 consecutive call cycles on the A15s end without an availability failure, and the first 30 s of a call over the second transport are compared with a native GATT call; (6) with `test_rotating_ids` on, the version 5 offer carries the bit, a call over the second transport passes `rotid_open`, and no fixed prefix appears in any line of the second service; (7) a scripted central that enables the second service's notifications and then stalls ends in the 20 s incoming deadline and a rebuilt endpoint, not a stuck phone; (8) Bluetooth off/on and an app restart with the switch on return to a single hint on a single set. Not yet tested on phones. |
| Caller answer deadline (`test_caller_answer_deadline=false` is the control arm; DEBUG only) [caller_answer_deadline] | On (release too) | Connection review A3 (2026-10-27). From hop authentication to the callee's RINGING (or Accept) nothing bounded the caller: the end-to-end handshake read and the wait for RINGING had no timeout, so a callee or helper that stopped answering held "Calling" until the user gave up. A link still waiting 15 s after hop authentication (relayed: the 20 s SETUP budget + 10 s) is closed and the call fails with "The other phone connected but did not answer the call". With relayed calls on, the callee's 20 s incoming deadline is armed again after the first record (the relay pre-setup watchdog ended there). Line `call_setup: event=answer_deadline \| relayed= \| deadlineMs= \| action=close`. Phone check: a callee whose app is frozen after hop authentication (DEBUG), and normal direct and relayed calls (no line). |
| Incoming call handshake deadline on every endpoint off (`test_call_handshake_deadline_all=false`; DEBUG only) [call_handshake_deadline_all] | Deadline on every CoC and GATT call endpoint (design decision Q29, 2026-10-14) | Control arm. An accepted call link that has not completed both handshakes within 20 s is closed, so a silent stranger that connects and sends nothing cannot hold the endpoint; before this the deadline covered dual endpoints only. With the extra false only dual endpoints keep it, as before. Relayed-call endpoints keep their own pre-setup watchdog in both arms. Release builds always have the deadline. Line `BENCHMARK incoming_handshake: event=deadline | closed=true | deadlineMs=20000 | scope=single|dual`. Phone test T-HS20 (untested on phones). Pass criteria: with a raw connection that sends nothing, the endpoint closes it at 20 s (+-1 s) with the line above; a normal call from a second phone still connects and rings; a call whose handshakes take under 20 s (a verified contact, a stranger delayed by the tarpit for under 20 s) is not closed. **Requires / masked by:** a no-op while relayed calls are on: the deadline is then not applied to any endpoint (the relayed-call endpoint keeps its own pre-setup watchdog). |
| GATT MTU wait probe off (`test_gatt_mtu_probe=false`) [gatt_mtu_probe] | Probe on | Control arm (2026-10-11, `GattMtuWait`). A GATT client setup that has no `onMtuChanged` after 0.75 s sends one `readPhy`; a PHY callback proves the platform still owns the connection and the MTU request is only queued behind the stack's own service discovery (A15s median 1.3 s, S24 relay with 4 children 3.2 s+), so the wait runs to 8 s instead of cancelling that discovery at 3 s. No PHY callback keeps the old 3 s verdict (`configureMTU() - No connection`: ASUS 6 of 6, A15s 5 of 7 saved stalls), so the probe never shortens a wait. Off: fixed 3 s. Lines `GattVideoSetup ... stage=mtu_probe_request|mtu_timeout reason=` (DEBUG) and `BENCHMARK le_link_policy ... gattMtuProbe=`. Control arm, not yet tested on phones: GATT call and stream joins on M31, ASUS, A15s and S24 as client (both call roles); pass = no new `mtu_timeout`, setup time not above the control, and every `mtu_timeout reason=FAIL_QUEUED_TOO_LONG` accompanied by `platformAlive=true`. |
| Source/forwarder GATT priority (`test_source_gatt_priority`, `test_forwarder_gatt_priority`) | Policy by rate | Connection-priority experiments. Production: HIGH below 500 kbps and DCK from 500 kbps, except at the source, where a relay-capable child (`maxChildren > 0`) gets HIGH after its handshake whatever the rate (a forwarder keeps the rate rule). |
| Deferred call GATT priority (`test_gatt_defer_connection_priority`) | Off (DEBUG) | The private-call GATT client does not request its connection priority when the link connects; it requests it after the subscribe. DEBUG builds only. |
| Direct GATT heartbeat off (`test_direct_gatt_heartbeat=false`) | Heartbeat on (DEBUG control arm) | A direct GATT stream receiver sends periodic heartbeats and its source expects them (a mesh link or a non-GATT transport always does). Off removes them on a direct GATT link in a DEBUG build; a release build always sends them. |
| Scan on LE 1M only off (`test_scan_le_1m_only=false`) | On | Control arm (2026-10-19, F11). Every app scan asks for the LE 1M PHY (`ScanSettings.setLegacy(true)`, `setPhy(PHY_LE_1M)`): all of the app's adverts are legacy adverts, always on LE 1M, while the default `PHY_LE_ALL_SUPPORTED` makes Android 13+ stacks scan LE 1M and LE Coded in turn (`le_scanning_manager.cc SetScanParameters scan_phy 255 phy 5` on a Redmi 12). Line `scan_duty ... | phy=1M|all`. While the Find PCs filter is active that scan asks for extended advertising instead (a PC advertises extended), so this arm does not apply to it. |
| Call list software filter (`test_call_list_software_filter`) | Off (Experimental) | F11 follow-up (2026-10-20): the Nearby call list's scanner, too, gives the stack an empty filter and matches the service UUID in software, for an A/B on a phone whose offloaded filter misses another phone's advert (the Redmi 12 and the A15s). The list scans only while visible (screen on). Line `scan_duty`/`scan_stats` of that scanner; compare sightings of the missed phone with and without. |
| Call stall tolerance (`test_call_stall_tolerance`) | Off for a plain call; effectively ON for every renewal-negotiated direct GATT call (Experimental) | F17 Stage A (2026-10-20): a call whose peer went quiet is kept while this phone's own writes still complete, for up to 14 s instead of 8 s, with "Poor connection" from 3 s; if the own writes stop too, the 8 s rule ends it. Reason: an S24 whose sending stalled for 10 s (with a paired laptop connecting to it) recovered by itself after the M31 had ended the call at 8 s. It changes only this phone's timer (CF-CALL-10 says 8 s; promotion needs that rule amended). A call that negotiated in-call link renewal runs this rule whatever the switch says (renewal needs more than 8 s), so CF-CALL-10 is already not true for those calls: an open decision (DECISIONS "Known interactions", B6). Stale audio is not dropped after the stall (late audio is played, user direction 2026-10-07) unless `test_call_stale_audio_drop` is on (design decision 2026-11-06). Line `call_stall: phase=soft|resumed|ended`. |
| Skip redundant priority request (`test_skip_redundant_priority`) | Off (Experimental) | A connection-priority request for the priority already requested is not sent when the link's last update already put the interval inside that priority's window. Reason (2026-10-20, one instance): an Android 10 radio source re-requested HIGH right after the stream handshake on a link already at 15 ms; no update followed on either phone and the link dropped with a supervision timeout 5.07 s later. A/B on that source's radio joins. Line `prio_request: skipped | intervalMs= | window=`. |
| Drop leading blank camera frames (`test_drop_leading_blank_frames`) | Off (Experimental) | An Image-input encoder (software VP9, native AV1) drops the camera's leading blank buffers (luma below 1, chroma within 1 of 128) until the first real frame, at most 15 frames or 1 s after the first; nothing is dropped after the first real frame, and surface-input encoders are not affected. Reason (2026-10-20): the S22 as a source delivered 4 exactly black buffers before its first real frame in all 8 prewarm A/B runs (decoded: luma 0.008 for frames 1-4, then 50.7 rising), so a viewer's first picture was black; the ASUS never did. On, the first frame sent is a real picture, about 0.27 s later on the S22. Line `camera_leading_blank: dropped=`. |
| Source media pre-warm (`test_source_media_prewarm`) | On (design decision 2026-10-21; `false` is the control arm) | A broadcast source starts its camera and encoder when it begins connecting to its first viewer, not after that viewer authenticated; the microphone still waits for an authenticated viewer, and no viewer means the existing all-disconnected path closes the media. Not for radio, the Auto ladder or replay. Reason (2026-10-20, 8 S22/ASUS runs, GATT and CoC, both directions): selection to first surface 2.9-3.8 s against the 3 s gate; the first key frame came a median 995 ms after authentication because media started then (and its first frame was black in 8 of 8), while link setup before it took 1.2-2 s. Expected: about 0.9 s off selection to first surface. Lines `source_prewarm: event=start|first_viewer`. |
| Viewer admission bounds off (`test_viewer_admission_bounds=false`) | On | Control arm (2026-10-20, Design QV review 1, R3). A source starts at most max(4, fan-out) new pre-authentication viewer attempts per 10 s, oldest request key first; a key whose attempt failed before authentication sorts after untried ones for 10 s (never excluded); requests over the limit wait for the next scan without the 30 s capacity cooldown; recovery requests are not counted. Reason: fresh RDI/address pairs were always new keys, skipped every per-key cooldown and held fan-out slots for the 12 s connection timeout. ViewerAdmissionBoundsTest simulates a 50-per-second flood and requires the real viewer admitted within 15 s with at most 4 attempts per 10 s, and the old admission (control arm) never admitting it in 120 s. The bounded refusal tables (512 entries, swept) are not switchable. Line `viewer_admission: deferred= | chosen= | tracked=`. |
| Join retry and message off (`test_stream_join_retry=false`) | On | Control arm (2026-10-20). A live join (radio or video) that fails before it becomes active shows "Couldn't join <title>." with Try again, and joins once more by itself when the same stream is listed again after the route cooldown (one automatic retry per stream until a join succeeds; a pending join waits at most 30 s). Off: only the browser status line, as before. Lines `stream_join: failed|try_again|retry`. |
| Callee name sent at Accept (`test_callee_name_after_reveal=false` is the DEBUG control arm) [callee_name_after_reveal] | On since 2026-11-10 (design decision, audit CALL-06; promoted on mechanism: no outcome claim; release builds always on) | A callee behind a throwaway key sends its name to a stranger caller only after that caller's reveal verified and the person is not on the block list, as its camera and microphone already do; a blocked caller never learns it. Control arm: the name goes with Accept, before the block check, as before. Applies to audience "Everyone" in a stranger-to-stranger call; a verified contact still gets the name at Ringing, a real-key caller was checked before ringing, "Only people I verified" already waited for the reveal and "Nobody" sends nothing. No wire change. Line `call_label_decision: role=callee | name=after_reveal`. |
| Unbounded stream search (`test_stream_search_bound=false`) [stream_search_bound] | On (control arm; owner answer 2026-11-10, "small defaults", audit CALL-04; promoted on mechanism: no outcome claim) | A viewer whose stream was lost (source out of range, app killed, battery dead) searches for another route for at most 60 s, then returns to the stream list with "The stream was lost" and Try again, which also stops the scan and reopens the phone to calls. A failed reconnect attempt inside the search does not restart the 60 s; the stream playing again ends the search. Off: the search lasts until the user leaves, as before. Line `stream_search: gave_up`. |
| Ring timeout off (`test_call_ring_timeout=false`) | On | Control arm (2026-10-20, F19): direct calls ring without a limit, as before. On: the caller cancels after 60 s of ringing ("No answer"), the callee declines after 65 s ("Missed call"; 75 s until the 2026-10-21 decision), existing signals only (PROTOCOL "Ring timeout"). Reason: an unanswered call rang for 6 minutes, and both phones were unreachable meanwhile. Line `call_ring_timeout: role= | rangMs=`. |
| Remote video fit off (`test_remote_video_fit=false`) | On | Control arm (2026-10-28). On: in a private call, the other person's video is shown whole and centred with plain black bars when its displayed shape (after the sender's quarter-turn rotation) differs strongly from the view's (longer/shorter aspect at least 1.8, or landscape against portrait), and fills when the shapes are close (portrait to portrait); the app-wide "Show full frame" setting still always fits; a double-tap on the large remote video toggles Fit/Fill for the rest of that call; the activity is never rotated and the self-view picture-in-picture is unchanged. Off: the app-wide framing setting only (Fill by default), no double-tap. Reason: a landscape Windows laptop camera on a portrait S24 was cropped to a narrow middle strip. A blurred backdrop was rejected by the user; bars stay black. |
