| Liveness hang-up off (`test_call_liveness_hang_up=false`) | On | Control arm (2026-10-20, F17). A phone ending a call on 8 s of silence first writes Hang-up/Error (best effort, 300 ms), so a peer that can still hear it ends at once instead of after its own 8 s (PROTOCOL "Hang-up before a liveness close"). Line `call_liveness_hang_up: written= | waitedMs=`. |
| Meet scan software filter off (`test_meet_software_filter=false`) | On | Control arm (2026-10-20, F11). The Meet scan (Add contact, scanning a code: screen on, at most the Meet scan limit) gives the stack an empty filter and matches the service UUID in software; every other scanner keeps the offloaded filter (the process-wide S-06 stage is unchanged). Reason: a Redmi 12's offloaded filter never reported an Android 10 phone's code advert (no report from it at all in the HCI log), while the same phone's unfiltered observer heard it; that advert differs only in its flags (0x02, which an Android 10 app cannot change). Line `meet_scan: start ... | filter=software|hardware`. |
| Weak-pair reach off (`test_weak_pair_reach=false`) | On | Control arm (2026-10-19, F11). While a verification code is on screen the call listener advertises at low latency and high power (100 ms, `TX_POWER_HIGH`; a set that already took its balanced step is restarted once). The Meet scan looks for 30 s with one fresh scanner after 15 s. A Contacts call waits for room for five sightings at the scanner's measured median gap (10-30 s). Off: the balanced step after 10 s, a 10 s Meet search, a 10 s wait. Lines `meet_code: shown`, `meet_scan: start ... searchMs | restart`, `call_invite: waiting ... typicalGapMs`, `adv_payload ... mode | intervalMs | tx`. |
| Reachable phones' list scans yield (control arm `test_reachable_scan_yield=false`) | On (promoted 2026-10-27) | Better or equal on every measured pair, never worse: S22/ASUS 10/10 vs 8/10 calls (status 133 2 vs 8), M31/A15s 4/5 vs 2/5, S24/M31 6/6 vs 3/6, ASUS/A15s 3/6 vs 3/6; median tap->ring 1.9 vs 2.5 s (profiling-20261026 predial10, relay-showcase direct A/Bs on e6a7ff06). Xiaomi and A55 still to measure. F11 item 5 A/B (2026-10-19). The Nearby call list and the Live list scan at low latency for 8 s, then balanced, instead of low latency without end, so that a reachable phone's radio is free to accept a caller's connection. Setup-latency use (2026-10-26): the LL connect of a direct GATT call (connect request to connected) is a median 497 ms, p90 1124 ms over 1074 saved calls although the caller had just heard the callee; compare that stage with the switch on and off. Hypothesis: status 133 with HCI 0x3E ("failed to be established") toward phones that scan without pause (counts of 2026-10-19 by target: Xiaomi 42, A15s 26, ASUS 20). Measure establishment per pair with it on and off before any default change. |
| Experimental: call link sharing (`test_call_link_share=true`) | Off | F16 fix 1 A/B (2026-10-20). An Automatic call caps its own video rate when the video it RECEIVES keeps arriving late (playout lateness >= 250 ms in 2 consecutive 1 s windows): 75 % of what it sends, never below 60 kbps; 5 calm windows raise the cap by 15 % until it reaches the request and is lifted. Windows without received video do not count. No wire change. A fixed rate the user chose is untouched. Evidence: the M31 sent 200 kbps over a ~254 kbps GATT link (`blocked` 0.52-0.62) while the A15s's and the Xiaomi's video waited up to 2063 / 1218 ms in their send queues (19 and 9 freezes). Line `call_link_share: requested | target | effective | cap | reason`. |
| Experimental: follow the camera's frame rate (`test_call_fps_follow=true`) | Off | F16 fix 2 A/B (2026-10-20). An Automatic call whose camera keeps a sensor frame above 1.2x the requested interval for 2 encoder windows (5 s each) asks the camera and the encoder for the camera's real rate (an even 10 fps instead of 10/15 bursts); an exposure below 0.8x the requested interval for 2 windows returns to the request. A fixed frame rate the user chose is untouched. Evidence: the A15s in low light, sensor frame 100.05 ms with AE range 15-15. Line `call_fps_follow: requested | effective | sensorFrameMs | exposureMs`. |
| Scan-failure recovery off (`test_scan_recovery=false`) [scan_recovery] | Recovery on | Control arm. Every production BLE scanner goes through `BleL2capDiscovery` (call list, stream discovery, receiver/relay recovery, forwarder, benchmark); any `onScanFailed` used to end discovery and hand the error to the caller, which showed it and restarted after its own delay (30 s for viewer requests). Now ALREADY_STARTED stops and restarts the scan; registration failure, internal error, out of resources, scanning too frequently and unknown codes back off 3 s x n (capped at 30 s, reset by any result); FEATURE_UNSUPPORTED stops and reports. Each restart takes a slot from the scan-start gate. A continuous scan hands the failure to its caller's visible error path once the back-off reaches 30 s (about 2 minutes of failures); a timed scan that never recovered still reports its failure. Classic inquiry is not a BLE scan and is unchanged. Line `BENCHMARK scan_recovery`. Untested on phones. The same switch also turns off scan-stall recovery (2026-10-19, `ScanStallWatch`): the Nearby call list and the Live list restart a scan that heard nothing at all for 10 s, only when the scan-start gate has a free slot (never past 5 starts in 30 s), doubling the wait after each restart that changed nothing (up to 80 s) and returning to 10 s on any result; line `scan_stall: scanner | silentMs | restart=yes/quota_full/failed | startsIn30s`. It answers scans that the stack accepted but never fed (a Redmi 12 on HyperOS: raw=0 for 28-90 s with five phones advertising). |
| Bluetooth ON recovery off (`test_bluetooth_on_recovery=false`; DEBUG only) [bluetooth_on_recovery] | Recovery on | Control arm. Switching Bluetooth off and on destroys every native object: scans, advertising sets, GATT servers and their services, CoC listeners. The epoch callback already resumed the scanners it had paused; the rest stayed broken. Now, when Bluetooth is back: (1) every scanner that was started while the adapter was going down or in BLE-only mode, so it is still marked running but holds a dead stack registration, is stopped and restarted through its gated start path (a stream viewer, call list or request-discovery scan that stayed at zero results); (2) a live broadcast no longer disarms when its offer cannot start while Bluetooth is down: the stream, its encoder session and its status stay, the status line says Bluetooth is off, and the offer, title pages and viewer-request discovery start again through the normal start path when Bluetooth is back; (3) a stream viewer (including a forwarding viewer's upstream link) whose adapter goes off closes its parent link at once, so the read fails like any link loss and the normal route recovery runs ("Connection lost. Finding another route...") instead of showing a frozen picture on a dead link; (4) an idle private-call listener (GATT server with its video service, CoC control server and listener, Classic socket, advertising set) is closed and reopened through the same path as the rotating-ID restart, so a caller no longer finds the phone and then fails service discovery. A call or message exchange in progress is left alone. Relay and receiver GATT servers are not touched. Hop-local, no wire change. Control arm: only the scanners the off callback paused are resumed, a failed offer disarms the broadcast, and the listener keeps its dead server. One line per component `BENCHMARK bluetooth_on_recovery: component=scanner|stream_advertise|call_listener|viewer_link | action=restarted|skipped | reason=`. Untested on phones. |
| LE scan start quota (`test_device_policy_off=le-scan-start-quota`) | On (registry, every phone) | AOSP drops a 6th BLE scan start inside 30 s per app UID without telling the app (a "scanning too frequently" line in the Bluetooth log, seen on the A15s and S24 in multi-phone runs; no onScanFailed callback arrives on any release, so only this budget protects). Starts wait for a free slot instead of failing, the user sees "searching again in N s", and the low-latency -> balanced switch is skipped when no slot is free. Starts are persisted per boot (elapsedRealtime keyed by Settings.Global.BOOT_COUNT) because the stack's count survives app process restarts but not a reboot. Untested on phones. |
| LE connect retry beyond 133 (`test_device_policy_off=le-connect-retry-link-statuses`) | On (registry, every phone) | GATT video and CoC control setup also retry status 8, 0x22, 0x3E and 147 (Nordic's Android-BLE-Library retries any connect-stage failure), at most once, and never past a private call's early-loss budget so the 2M -> 1M setup retry stays possible. Upstream-documented, not locally tested. |
| GATT write busy retry (`test_device_policy_off=gatt-write-busy-retry`) | On (API 33+) | A write the stack reports busy (one permit per peer address for every client and app) is repeated every 2 ms for at most 500 ms instead of failing the link. Upstream-documented, not locally tested. |
| Android 14 MTU note (`test_device_policy_off=gatt-mtu-fixed-517`) | On (API 34+, diagnostics) | The `LE link:` line marks a `test_gatt_request_mtu` below 517 as ignored: Android 14+ always requests 517. |
| Transport proposals (`android16-bond-loss-dialog`, `samsung-gatt-direct-connect-5s`, `android15-qcom-coc-connect-stall`, `android12-gatt-disconnect-status-zero`, `exynos9611-dual-role-equal-interval-child-starvation`) | Inert (reported only) | Sourced in `BluetoothTransportPolicy.kt`; promotion needs a phone A/B. The Exynos 9611 entry is a diagnostic record only (matcher: hardware `exynos9611`): a relay that is peripheral to its parent and central to its child, both at 15 ms, loses its child link in about half of the attempts; the generic mitigations (`relay_child_stall_guard`, `relay_child_reanchor`, `relay_establish_fallback`) apply on every phone. |
| Decoder init fallback (`test_device_policy_off=decoder-init-fallback` to disable) | On for user sessions only | Media3 `maybeInitCodecWithFallback`. A video decoder that fails to create, configure or start is released and the next decoder for the format is tried: hardware first, aliases skipped, at most 3 attempts. A policy-forced software decoder (`qcom-legacy-call-vp9-software-decoder`) falls back only to another software decoder, or the call fails. Never in a DEBUG launch with `test_*` extras, and never for a `test_decoder` request: those keep the former single choice, and `BENCHMARK decoder requested=/effective=` lets the scorer fail a substitution. Upstream-documented, untested on phones. |
| Device policy entry off (`test_device_policy_off=<id>[,<id>]` or `all`) | Every entry on | Turns individual registry entries off for an A/B; release builds ignore it. Codec and YUV entries that apply on every phone: `codec-exception-recoverable-reinit` (a call decoder whose CodecException reports `isRecoverable()` is re-created at the next keyframe, at most twice) and `yuv-image-crop-rect` (camera YUV copies and the reference recorder start from `Image.getCropRect()`). Both are upstream-documented and untested on phones. |
| GATT dial probe - DEBUG (`test_gatt_dial_address=<MAC>`, `test_gatt_dial_service`, `test_gatt_dial_address_type=random\|public`) | Off | Design QV gate W2: dials one exact BLE address through the production GATT client (LE 1M, connect, MTU, service discovery, subscribe), writes one test chunk and closes, logging `debug_gatt_dial` per stage. DEBUG builds only; a release build never reads the extras. |

## Background and lifecycle

| Switch | Default | What it does and why it is kept |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Foreground-service refusal fallback (`test_device_policy_off=bg-fgs-start-fallback`) | On (API 31+) | Only Android's two refusals are handled: a background start (ForegroundServiceStartNotAllowedException) or a while-in-use camera/microphone type refusal. The service keeps connectedDevice, shows "Camera and microphone are unavailable while the app is in the background", and logs `BENCHMARK foreground_service` (requested, effective and refused types); the scorer fails a session whose refusal is not `none`. Off: the exception propagates (crash) as before. Untested on phones. |
| Background reliability checks (`test_device_policy_off=bg-battery-optimization`, `bg-background-restricted`, `bg-standby-bucket`, `bg-unused-app-restrictions`, `bg-power-save-mode`, `bg-sony-stamina`) | On (diagnostics) | `BackgroundReliability.status(context)` reports Android's documented restriction states with settings links; line `Background reliability:` at start. No keep-alive hacks, no exemption prompt. |
| Phone-maker background guidance (`test_device_policy_off=bg-oem-<maker>`, 20 makers) | On (by `Build.MANUFACTURER`) | Don't Kill My App (3616bc8b) steps per maker (Samsung, Xiaomi, Huawei, OnePlus, OPPO, vivo, realme, Motorola, Sony, Nokia/HMD, Google, ASUS, Tecno and others) plus documented killer apps (manifest `<queries>`, detection only). Makers without a page get generic advice. Upstream-documented, not locally tested. |
| Play battery explanation (`test_play_battery_guidance=false`) | On (Play only) | DEBUG control restores the previous explanation and suppresses the added guidance toast. Both arms open the optimisation list, never the direct exemption prompt. GitHub/F-Droid keep their direct prompt. JVM regressions added; no build or phone test run. |

## Build and benchmark harness

Build options and DEBUG tools of the benchmark harness. None changes a user session.

| Switch | Default | What it does and why it is kept |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Optional armeabi-v7a library (Gradle `-PenableArmeabiV7a=true`) | Off | Adds the 32-bit ARM native library so phones without arm64-v8a can install. Release APK 10.7 -> 17.6 MB (+6.9 MB stored, about +2.6 MB compressed); software AV1 speed on 32-bit cores is unmeasured. Registry proposal `native-armeabi-v7a-optional-build`. Release builds set it through `make_release.py`. |
| Automated-run speaker gain (`test_playback_gain`) | Unset (the device runners pass 0.01; the iOS interop helper 0.05) | Quiet speakers in automated runs. It does not affect measured PCM. |
| Legacy stage-trace lines (DEBUG `test_stage_trace_v1=true`; with `test_media_trace`) | Off (batched v2) | The per-stage pipeline trace logs one batched `BENCHMARK stage v2` line per stream per 250 ms or 3500 bytes (per-stream constants once, records as compact arrays, same records and fields, direction derived by the reader). The original one-line-per-record `BENCHMARK stage v1` format logged 150 to 200 lines a second and overflowed logd, which dropped unrelated lines (call-end acknowledgements, join stage lines). This switch keeps v1 available so older evidence stays reproducible; the scorer and the log-loss detector read both. |
| Encoder bench, radio-free (DEBUG `test_encoder_bench`; settings extras `test_bench_codec`, `test_bench_bitrate_mode` auto/cq/vbr/cbr/cbr_fd/encoder_default (Design HC: no bitrate mode key, only for an encoder that declares none), `test_bench_keyframe_s`, `test_bench_duration_s`, `test_bench_libaom_cpu_used`, `test_bench_libaom_threads`, plus the suite's `test_width/height/fps/bitrate_kbps`, `test_intra_refresh_frames` and the raw fixture `test_raw_yuv/width/height`) | Off (Experimental, DEBUG builds only) | Encodes the declared native raw-motion fixture through the phone's real selected encoder (the app's own `CameraVideoEncoder` raw-input path: MediaCodec configuration, rate control and libaom setup are the product's) for a fixed time and logs the result, without starting Bluetooth, advertising or scanning, so a phone that is outside a radio A/B can still do per-chip-family encoder work: hardware intra refresh, bitrate mode CBR/VBR/CQ/CBR-FD, libaom real-time speed and threads, hardware AV1, bench-only HEVC and AVC hardware encoders (`test_bench_codec` `hevc`/`avc`, `video/hevc`/`video/avc` or an exact encoder name; these never enter a product path, the stream protocol or negotiation, and their bitrate mode is always set explicitly, CBR unless another mode is requested), the bundled libvpx VP9 encoder (Design VX: `test_bench_codec=libvpx-vp9` or `libvpx` only; its cpu-used pin must be 5..9), and later an SVT-AV1 comparison. `MainActivity` hands the launch to `EncoderBenchActivity` before it creates any view, service or radio object; a bench launch into an already running app is refused. A guard (`EncoderBenchGuard`) is active in the bench process and every Bluetooth start path (scan, advertise, GATT, L2CAP, RFCOMM, discovery) calls it first: a start during the bench is refused, counted and reported as `ble_violations` in the summary, which then fails. A setting an encoder cannot apply is never swapped silently: statically known impossibilities (CQ on an encoder that does not report it, a libaom knob on a MediaCodec encoder, intra refresh on libaom, a size the encoder reports unsupported, a fixture of another size) refuse the run with the reason, and a setting the encoder may ignore (intra refresh) is passed through and its `effective` value is read back from the encoder's output format. The launch does not change any default; with the extra absent nothing differs. Untested on phones. |

## Device-family research batches (integrated 2026-10-14)

Switches and registry arms from the device-family batches R, T, U, S, B1, G, D, C, V, B2, A and P (research files and decisions: [decisions](decisions/0050-device-family-research-2026-10-14.md)). Every non-default arm is DEBUG-only: a release build ignores the launch extras, and each launch starts from the defaults. Entries that are on by default are turned off with `test_device_policy_off=<id>` (or `all`). Nothing here has a phone result yet unless a row says so; "untested on phones" means unit tests only.

### Registry matchers (batch R)

| Switch | Default | What it does and why it is kept |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Device-policy matchers: security-patch window, five Bluetooth-stack eras, runtime traits (`DeviceTraitsProbe`) | On (read-only) | One off-switch rule for every batch (the launch extra and a traits view's own off list). An entry whose effect waits for an Experimental arm is reported under `armOff`, not `active`, until the arm is on; a proposal superseded by an implemented entry is never reported as matching (2026-10-27). Entries can match a security-patch window, a stack era (AOSP base, vendor stack) and runtime facts read once at start (ROM version properties, ABIs, memory, Bluetooth package versions, audio features). No switch of its own: an entry using them is turned off with `test_device_policy_off=<id>`. ROM property names are unverified (`rom=unverified{...}` in the device_policy line). The policy report tool ignores patch and runtime fields until the first entry uses them. |

### Thermal, memory and native (batch T)

All Experimental and default off except the always-on memory-tier log.

| Switch | Default | What it does and why it is kept |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Thermal headroom forecast (`test_thermal_forecast=true`) | Off | Treats the ADPF forecast `getThermalHeadroom(10)` above 0.85/0.95/1.0 as LIGHT/MODERATE/SEVERE for the FPS/bitrate ladder only (never a stream stop), and only while automatic cooling is on (benchmark profiles turn it off). Guide thresholds, not locally tested; registry proposal `thermal-headroom-forecast`. Since 2026-10-14 the forecast is sampled every 2 s with a 3 s warm-up and a decision every 10 s (the old one call per 10 s could never produce a forecast, see the thermal rows below). |
| Thermal libaom speed floor (`test_thermal_speed_floor=true`) | Off | While automatic cooling is on and the effective status is MODERATE/SEVERE, the software AV1 encoder's cpu-used floor is 8/10 and slower trial presets are blocked (the raised preset is not remembered). Not measured on a hot phone; registry proposal `thermal-native-speed-floor`. |
| Legacy forecast sampling (`test_thermal_forecast_legacy_10s`) | Off (Experimental) | Keeps the old arm of the forecast path (one call per 10 s, no warm-up discard) so the corrected 2 s sampler can be compared with it. Only meaningful with `test_thermal_forecast=true`. Untested on phones. Planned A/B: S24 and ASUS. |
| Battery-temperature fallback (`test_thermal_battery_fallback`; registry `thermal-battery-temperature-fallback`) | Off (Experimental) | When the sampler latches "no skin thresholds" (three NaN headroom answers, or an empty threshold map on API 35+), the battery temperature (40/43/46 C for light/moderate/severe) can raise the status the FPS/bitrate ladder follows; it never reaches critical, so it never stops a stream. Replaces the former SoC-family matcher with runtime detection. Untested on phones. **Requires / masked by:** `test_device_policy_off=thermal-battery-temperature-fallback` does nothing; the reader ignores the registry id (use `test_thermal_battery_fallback`). |
| Memory-tier sizing (`test_memory_tier`; registry `memory-tier-sizing`) | Off (Experimental); the tier log is always on | Sender queue and lag-frame caps by memory tier (from MemoryInfo, not a model name). The `memory_tier` log line is written on every launch (off with `test_device_policy_off=memory-tier-log`). Planned check: A55 and M31. |
| Native SIMD safe mode (`test_native_simd_safe_mode`; registry `native-simd-safe-mode`) | Off (Experimental) | After a native crash at encoder start on arm64 the app relaunches once with the libaom SIMD capability mask reduced (`AOM_SIMD_CAPS_MASK=0x3`). Marker files live in the app files directory. |
| 32-bit userspace tier (`test_userspace32_tier`; registry `userspace32-tier`) | Off (Experimental) | On a 64-bit CPU running 32-bit userspace the software AV1 thread count is capped (a MediaCodec preference over libaom is a PLAN idea, not a switch). |

### Audio capture, routing and focus (batch U)

Active entries (off with `test_device_policy_off=<id>`): `android15-audio-focus-requires-top-or-fgs`, `audio-effect-default-state-logging`, `audio-capture-silence-watchdog`, `audio-echo-risk-hint`, `call-route-set-result-and-reassert`, `opus-encoder-native-fallback`. Inert proposals: `audio-capture-source-fallback-chain`, `audio-off-main-thread-commands`, `call-audio-mode-change-wait`, `call-usb-headset-mode-normal`, `opus-complexity-governor`.

| Switch | Default | What it does and why it is kept |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Capture source fallback (`test_capture_source_fallback`) | Off (Experimental) | After confirmed digital silence the capture restarts once per step on the next source (voice communication, mic, camcorder). It loses the platform echo canceller, so it is never used in benchmarks (requested != effective is logged). |
| Blank-capture evidence off (`test_capture_silence_evidence=false`) | On | Control arm (2026-10-20, F18): the old notice after any 2 s of exact digital zeros. With the evidence rule the "microphone is not picking up any sound" notice for a blank capture needs no signal since the recorder started (8 s) or one 30 s zero run; the platform's own silencing (isClientSilenced) is still told at once. Reason: Samsung voice-communication capture outputs exact zeros in a quiet room and recovers by itself (16-34 episodes and 6-8 false notices per 4-minute call on the S24 and M31). Lines `audio_capture_silence ... | notice=` and `audio_capture_blank: evidence=never_signal|persistent`. |
| Audio work off the main thread (`test_audio_off_main`) | On (promoted 2026-11-09; false is the control arm) | AudioRecord/AudioTrack/AudioManager teardown, and the call microphone start, run on one serialised command thread with a watchdog. Aimed at the M31 audio ANR class (Oboe #1216/#1474). Planned check: 50 call cycles on the M31 with the switch off and on. Setup-latency use (2026-10-26): the call microphone starts on the UI thread, so the peer's stream header waits there a median 584 ms (p90 988 ms, 4475 saved calls) before the call turns ACTIVE and the first remote picture can show, on both phones; A/B with `call_ui_lag event=remote_header`, accept to ACTIVE and ACTIVE to first surface. Measured M31<->ASUS BLE CoC calls, n=6 per arm and direction: accept to ACTIVE -0.08 s and -0.23 s, remote-header lag down, total setup unchanged within the scatter, 12/12 calls active in both arms, no freezes (DECISIONS "Audio work off the main thread promoted"). |
| Mode-change wait (`test_audio_mode_change_wait`) | Off (Experimental, API 31+) | After setMode(IN_COMMUNICATION) waits up to 500 ms for the mode-changed listener before choosing the communication device (Signal sequence). |
| USB headset mode (`test_audio_usb_headset_mode_normal`) | Off (Experimental, low confidence) | One UI with Qualcomm and a USB headset uses MODE_NORMAL (Twilio workaround). Needs a USB-headset hardware test before any promotion. |
| Opus complexity governor (`test_audio_complexity_governor`) | Off (Experimental) | The native call encoder steps complexity 10 to 8 to 5 when the measured encode time stays high; the requested bitrate never changes. |
| Route re-assert off (`test_audio_route_reassert=false`) | Re-assert on (control arm) | The call re-asserts its communication device when the platform or another app changes it (rate limited). false restores setting the route once. Risk: OEM route moves. |

### BLE scanning and adapter state (batch S)

Shipped on (off with `test_device_policy_off=<id>`): `android11-gatt-server-connection-untracked` (Android 10-11 legacy stack: a client connection whose PHY read gets no answer within 400 ms is missing from the stack's server map; the call listener cancels it and registers again so the client's retry reaches a fresh server; second stage: a subscribed client whose first data write does not reach the app within 1.2 s is ended the same way, never a link that has received data; a re-registration keeps the running advertising set and its address, see `test_gatt_reregister_keep_address`), `android10-11-location-switch-scan-gate` (Location guidance on Android 10-11), `scan-silence-watchdog` (log only), `bluetooth-process-restart-recovery` (its off-switch stops only the scanner rebuild; the Bluetooth-epoch receiver that calls, viewers and advertising use stays attached, fixed 2026-10-27), `android10-scanner-leak-on-process-death` (scope SDK 29-30 since the lane11 ASUS dumpsys showed 25 leaked scanner registrations of force-killed Android 11 processes; the DEBUG `test_control=shutdown` also stops all scans before a runner's force-stop). Default change: the relay-helper scan restarts after 8 min on Android 14+ and 25 min on 10-13 (was 20 min).

| Switch | Default | What it does and why it is kept |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Restart interval control (`test_scan_renewal_legacy`) | Off (control arm) | The relay-helper scan restarts every 20 min as before. |
| App-wide scan renewal (`test_scan_renewal_all`; registry `le-scan-timeout-renewal`) | Off (Experimental) | Every scanner of the app stops together and restarts through the scan-start gate. Planned check: S24, 25 minutes, results per minute. |
| Stale-scan restart (`test_scan_stale_restart`) | Off (Experimental); the `scan_stale` log is always on | A stale-scan verdict requests one gated restart. Planned check: Xiaomi, force-idle. |
| Software filter fallback (`test_scan_sw_filter_fallback`; registry `scan-hardware-filter-fallback`) | Off (Experimental) | A filtered scan that yields nothing is probed once; if unfiltered scans see adverts the app switches to an unfiltered scan with a software filter while the screen is on. |
| Probe scans ungated (`test_scan_probe_ungated`) | Off (control arm) | The DEBUG probes start scans without a scan-start gate slot, as before 2026-10-14. The shipped behaviour gates them: the stack drops a 6th start in 30 s silently (no onScanFailed on any release). |

### Foreground service, onboarding and boot (batch B1)

The Google Play flavour never uses the Android 10-11 `location` foreground-service type (`BuildConfig.LOCATION_FGS_AVAILABLE=false`; its manifest declares neither the type nor `FOREGROUND_SERVICE_LOCATION`), whatever the family-policy entry says; ARCHITECTURE "Distribution and releases" gives the reason.


| Switch | Default | What it does and why it is kept |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Session wake lock (`test_session_wake_lock=true`; registry `fgs-session-wake-lock`; promotion constant `SwitchDefaults.SESSION_WAKE_LOCK`, Q44) | Off (Experimental, DEBUG) | A partial wake lock while a screen-off session forwards media. A held lock does not prove the CPU stays awake: measure with dumpsys power before any default. Planned check: S24 and ASUS, 20 min screen-off relay with the lock off and on. |
| Wake-lock daily cap (`test_session_wake_lock_cap_min=N`) | 100 minutes (0 = uncapped, A/B only) | Budget of the lock per day; Play vitals call two hours of screen-off partial wake lock in 24 h excessive. |
| Stop scans on exit off (`test_stop_scans_on_exit=false`) | On (control arm) | The app's BLE scanners are stopped together when the session service ends or the task is removed (the reason is the Android 10 scanner leak, but the stop applies on every Android version). false keeps them. |
| Boot resume (`test_boot_resume=true`; registry `boot-resume-after-restart`, Q41) | Off (Experimental, DEBUG only) | Starts the connectedDevice service after a restart or app update. Only the DEBUG extra switches it on; a release opt-in setting is a PLAN item (Q-N2). The boot receiver is disabled in the manifest until the switch is on. Known gap: the relay controllers live in the activity, so a service started at boot holds the notification but does not relay until a headless host exists. Reboot checks only after Q41. |

### GATT links and capacity (batch G)

On by default (off with `test_device_policy_off=<id>`): GATT client close after registration for SDK 34 and lower (G-02), stale CONNECTED callback cleanup, one client per remote, link-silence classification, pool-exhaustion guard, link-slot limit, Bluetooth-audio diagnostics. The link-budget admission, the wedge arm and every sizing arm below are Experimental. Release builds ignore all non-default arms.

| Switch | Default | What it does and why it is kept |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Busy-write wedge (`test_gatt_busy_wedge=legacy` or `recover`) | legacy (review 2026-10-14) | `recover` (Experimental) ends a link on one busy answer while MeshLive has nothing in flight on that client (Android 13-14) and retries every 25 ms; `legacy` is the old 2 ms spin up to 500 ms. Kept at legacy because the wedge check would end links on one answer on three of four lane phones without a phone A/B under load (A15s, ASUS, M31). |
| Close deferral off (`test_gatt_close_deferral=false`) | On for SDK 34 and lower (control arm) | A GATT client is not closed before its first callback (bounded 2 s) so the stack registration finishes first. |
| Pool back-off off (`test_gatt_pool_backoff=false`) | On (control arm) | After the phone-wide GATT registration pool refuses a client registration (status 257/128; scanner code 2 and a null GATT server are recorded the same way), automatic stream connects from viewer requests wait an exponential hold (1 s doubling to 30 s, reset by the first success) instead of re-dialling at once (lane11 ASUS Android 11: about 1,500 `MAX client reached: 32` stack errors per 3-minute run). One `BENCHMARK gatt_pool_exhausted: source=server\|client\|scanner` line per step; three steps of one source raise the Bluetooth off/on guidance on the reliability card. Off removes only the hold; the evidence lines and the card feed stay. User dials and DebugDialWait are not held. |
| Native GATT client wedge guard (`test_gatt_native_wedge_guard=false` is the DEBUG control arm) [gatt_native_wedge_guard] | On for the matched family since 2026-11-10 (owner, promoted on mechanism: no outcome claim; release builds always on; every other phone is untouched because the entry does not match; DeviceFamilyPolicy `mediatek-coloros-native-gatt-client-wedge`, low confidence, `test_device_policy_off=mediatek-coloros-native-gatt-client-wedge`) | On a MediaTek phone with ColorOS or Realme UI on Android 16 or newer, two central connects in a row (GATT or LE CoC) that fail within 500 ms of the dial with status 128 (GATT_NO_RESOURCES), inside 60 s, while MeshLive holds no GATT client, show the reliability card's Bluetooth off/on guidance at once and log one `BENCHMARK gatt_native_client_wedge` line; a successful central connect clears it. Nothing is toggled: apps cannot restart Bluetooth on Android 13 and newer. Reason: after hours of use a Realme (MediaTek, Android 16) stack refused every new central connection ("No resources to open a new connection") with no client registered until Bluetooth was restarted, while its peripheral role kept working; the generic guidance needs three back-off steps 1-4 s apart and showed in 4 of 23 affected app sessions, the guard would have shown it in all 23 (offline replay). One phone, trigger unknown. |
| Call-relay acknowledged GATT writes (`test_relay_gatt_acknowledged_writes=true`) [relay_gatt_acknowledged_writes] | Off (Experimental, DEBUG builds only; DeviceFamilyPolicy `qcom-legacy-call-relay-gatt-acknowledged-writes`, low confidence, `test_device_policy_off=qcom-legacy-call-relay-gatt-acknowledged-writes`) | On a legacy-Qualcomm phone (API 32 or lower, Qualcomm hardware) acting as the hidden call relay, the arm opens its next GATT hop with acknowledged writes (write with response) paced at 24 KB/s; by default the relay writes without response, as every other relay does. Reason it is off: the rule was shipped as the legacy-Qualcomm default without any retained evidence, and on a relayed call S22 -> ASUS (legacy Qualcomm relay) -> A15s it measured harmful: with the rule on 0 of 3 calls held 60 s (the A15s dropped write responses, the GATT write timed out, the relay closed at 18-26 s), with it off 2 of 3 held the full 60 s (the third failed on the other hop). Kept as an arm so an A/B can compare it. |
| Link budget admission (`test_link_budget_admission`) | Off (Experimental) | Refuse a child past the phone-wide LE link budget with the distinct reason "relay full" (never "low memory"). The decision is computed and logged either way (`gatt_link_budget`). Planned check: S24 fan-out of five and more. |
| Server hold (`test_gatt_server_hold`), ACL wait on redial (`test_gatt_acl_wait`), remote-full fail-fast (`test_gatt_remote_full_failfast`) | Off (Experimental) | Make-before-break pieces of G-04/G-05: `BluetoothGattServer.connect(device,false)` on an admitted link, wait for the ACL to drop before a redial, fail a link at once when the peer's GATT pool is full. |
| Settle delay (`test_gatt_settle_ms=<0..3000>`) and connectGatt on the main looper (`test_connect_gatt_main`) | Off (Experimental) | A wait after CONNECTED before the first GATT operation (G-05), and `connectGatt` posted to the main looper (G-06, Huawei/Samsung reports). A/B on the A15s. |
| Notification sizing (`test_gatt_payload=244`, `495` or `default`; `test_gatt_byte_window=<bytes>`, `test_gatt_window_kbps=<kbps>`) | default, no window (Experimental) | ATT value size per write or notification (one or two data PDUs), and a byte window with an assumed drain rate. Planned check: 244 and 495 between M31 and S24. |
| Bluetooth audio coexistence (`test_bt_audio_capacity_key`, `test_bt_audio_load_cut`) | Off (Experimental) | Learned capacity is kept apart with an active Bluetooth audio output (".btaudio" key), and the planned load is cut to 0.6x. Planned check: earbuds. |
| GATT cache refresh (`test_gatt_refresh`) | Off (Experimental) | Refresh the GATT cache before service discovery. |

### Decoder robustness (batch D)

On by default (off with `test_device_policy_off=<id>`): `c2-mtk-release-instead-of-flush` (a c2.mtk.* decoder is rebuilt instead of flushed on recovery), `qcom-decoder-vendor-low-latency` (a Qualcomm decoder that does not offer the standard low-latency key gets Qualcomm's own, `vendor.qti-ext-dec-low-latency.enable=1`, retried without it if configure refuses it; logged `decoder_low_latency` requested/effective), `decoder-no-output-watchdog` (logging), `decoder-bounded-release` (5 s), slow and hung configure logging, and a transient 0x0 output format is ignored. No owned phone has a c2.mtk decoder.

| Switch | Default | What it does and why it is kept |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Stall recovery (`test_decoder_stall_recovery`; registry arm `decoder-stall-recovery-arm`) [decoder_stall_recovery] | On (promoted 2026-11-10; randomised user-launch A/B 12 vs 12: survived 12 of 12 vs 8 of 12, p = 0.047) | A decoder stall (input queued, no output) rebuilds the decoder once at the next key frame, then excludes the component for the session and tries the next candidate. The switch is read by the shared video decoder, so calls and streams both get it. `false` (DEBUG launch extra only) is the control arm: a stall is only logged. In a call with `test_call_video_restart` on, that arm handles the stall instead. Release builds keep it on. |
| Low-latency ladder (`test_decoder_low_latency_ladder`; registry arm `decoder-low-latency-ladder-arm`) | Off (Experimental) | Decoders that declare FEATURE_LowLatency are tried first and the format carries the vendor low-latency keys the component lists (MediaTek vdec-lowlatency, vdec-no-record, use-clearmotion-mode=0), dropping the newest key first when configure fails. Vendor key evidence is AVC/HEVC. Planned check: S24 and M31 via `decoder_behaviour`. |
| Real-time priority (`test_decoder_priority`; registry arm `decoder-realtime-priority-arm`) | Off (Experimental) | KEY_PRIORITY=0 on the decoder format, dropped first when configure fails; never an operating rate. |

### CoC and Classic (batch C)

On by default (off with `test_device_policy_off=<id>`): `coc-connect-bounded` (every LE CoC connect bounded at 12 s, because the stack timer is 60 s; failure goes to the existing retry and automatic GATT path), `coc-socket-error-codes`, `coc-android10-address-type-precondition`, `classic-connect-runtime-guard`, `classic-pairing-request-logging`, `coc-credit-stall-classification` (classifier only; the writer does not feed it yet).

| Switch | Default | What it does and why it is kept |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Connect bound (`test_coc_connect_bound_ms=<ms>`) | 12000 (0 = unbounded as before) | Value of the CoC connect bound. |
| MTU exchange before CoC traffic (`test_coc_mtu_prime=false` is the DEBUG control arm; registry `coc-mtu-exchange-before-traffic`, off-switch `test_device_policy_off=coc-mtu-exchange-before-traffic`) | On for fresh-ACL CoC centrals (Android 12-13 by registry, Q49); other stacks log `coc_mtu_prime ... outcome=skipped reason=control-link-alive` | One GATT MTU exchange (517) on the fresh CoC ACL, because LE Data Length rises to 251 only after an ATT MTU exchange (MTU-02); a CoC-only link keeps the controller default (27-byte payloads). Only the fresh-ACL client path runs it (Android 12-13, for example the M31); stacks whose control GATT client stays open already exchanged the MTU, so the central logs a skipped line and does nothing else. It never fails the CoC connect. Control arm: DEBUG `test_coc_mtu_prime=false` restores the old no-prime connect exactly (a release build cannot change it by extra). Phones: M31 central, ASUS peer, 632 kbps on 5/5 runs with the prime against about 325 kbps on 5/5 without; S24 peer neutral. |
| Echo verification (`test_coc_echo_verify`) | Off (Experimental, both ends) | A 1 KB echo over the new CoC socket must round-trip before the socket is used; the listener answers it before anything else, so both phones need the switch. |
| MTU prime and control reattach off the authentication path (`test_coc_prime_async`) [coc_prime_async] | Off (Experimental, 2026-11-07) | Join-latency P1. A fresh-ACL LE CoC client (Android 12-13) used to run the MTU prime (0.54-0.58 s) and the GATT control reattach (about 0.17 s) before it handed over the connected socket, so hop authentication started only after both. With the switch on, the socket is handed over right after the CoC connect and the prime and reattach run on a background thread in the same order; the prime still always runs (it doubled throughput on M31 -> ASUS). The hop handshake's own records (up to 8 KB) go out at once; the first larger write on the socket waits until the prime and reattach are done, at most 1.5 s, then goes on as without the prime. Authentication and encryption are unchanged and no media byte is written before the hop handshake completes. A reattach that finishes after the socket was closed is closed, not handed over. Expected saving: about 0.5-0.7 s per CoC join where the prime runs. Lines `BENCHMARK coc_prime_async: phase=primed \| primeOutcome= \| primeMs= \| attachMs= \| writerWaited=true\|false\|waiting` and `phase=writer_wait \| waitedMs= \| released=`. Known interaction: the deferred-PHY arm (`test_coc_phy_after_connect`) and a relay's upstream priority request act through the control connection, which may not be attached yet when they run. Phone A/B: M31 -> ASUS and M31 -> S24 CoC streams and calls, ABBA, metric = connect-to-authenticated and selection/setup-to-first-surface; guard = CoC throughput and no stall in the first seconds. Untested on phones. |
| CoC connect retry with the PSM already read (`test_coc_psm_reuse_retry`) [coc_psm_reuse_retry] | Off (Experimental, 2026-11-07) | Join-latency P4b. On the fresh-ACL CoC path (Android 12-13 clients) a CoC connect that fails within 3 s after the PSM was read is retried once with the same PSM, instead of the caller repeating the GATT connect, the PSM read, the ACL-down wait and the connect (+2.9 s in 3 of 14 M31-client joins). The retry keeps the ACL-down wait (bounded 2 s) that the stranded-EATT stack class needs before every CoC connect; the watch is armed before the first connect, so a drop during it counts. Not retried: the app-side connect bound expiring, a caller deadline with less than 3 s left, the main thread. A second failure goes to the caller's own retry as before. If the failed attempt never brought an ACL up, the wait runs its full 2 s (still shorter than the redo). A connect cancelled by its caller (socket closed) can be retried once and fail again, delaying the cancel by up to the wait. Line `BENCHMARK coc_psm_reuse_retry: owner= \| failedAfterMs= \| error= \| aclDown= \| aclWaitMs=`. Phone A/B: M31 -> ASUS CoC streams and calls, ABBA, metric = connect-to-authenticated after a failed first connect; guard = no Bluetooth process crash (the EATT class). Untested on phones. |
| Classic RFCOMM peer cap (`test_classic_rfcomm_cap=<n>`; registry `classic-rfcomm-peer-cap`) | Log only (refusal off; root review 2026-10-14) | A Classic connect to a seventh distinct peer writes a log-only `classic_rfcomm_cap` line; with `=6` (Experimental) it is refused as capacity. Refusal is off because it refuses connections without phone evidence and incoming peers are undercounted until the accept paths call `ClassicRfcommCap.note`. |

### Camera and encoder configuration (batch V)

On by default (off with `test_device_policy_off=<id>`): `camera-session-wait-closed` (create the next session only after onClosed; Qualcomm, SDK 31 and lower, so the ASUS and the Xiaomi when its API is 31 or lower), `camera-stream-combination-validation` (log), `camera-open-error-retry`, `camera-frame-stall-watchdog` (log), `camera-privacy-toggle-notice`, `encoder-init-fallback` (after a failure only, user sessions only, never in benchmark launches). The Experimental arms never apply in a release build.

| Switch | Default | What it does and why it is kept |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Camera device reopen (`test_camera_reopen`) | Off (Experimental) | Close and reopen the CameraDevice when the session is rebuilt, on families that need it (V-01). Costs 300-800 ms and flickers the privacy indicator. Planned A/B: S24. |
| SessionConfiguration path (`test_camera_session_configuration`) | Off (Experimental) | SessionConfiguration plus session parameters (V-02). |
| LEGACY AE range (`test_camera_legacy_ae_range`), starvation fallback (`test_camera_starvation_fallback`), capability probe (`test_capability_probe_configure`) | Off (Experimental) | LEGACY hardware level picks the smallest lower bound of the AE range; a frame stall rebuilds the session with TEMPLATE_PREVIEW; a size within 8 px of the reported limit is probe-configured. |
| Encoder colour keys (`test_encoder_colour_keys`), Exynos QP range (`test_exynos_qp_range=<min>,<max>`), encoder KEY_LATENCY (`test_encoder_key_latency`) | Off (Experimental) | BT.709 limited SDR colour keys on the encoder format; vendor QP range of OMX.Exynos/c2.exynos encoders when the component lists the keys; KEY_LATENCY=1 retried without the key. One key at a time, newest key dropped first. Planned checks: M31 alignment and Exynos QP range against the VP9 overshoot. |
| Release timing, rotate and crop, YUV tail clamp (`test_camera_release_timing`, `test_camera_rotate_and_crop_none`, `test_yuv_tail_clamp`) | Off (Experimental) | Capture-interval monitor outside DEBUG logging; SCALER_ROTATE_AND_CROP=NONE where supported; a YUV plane short by its last samples is copied with the tail clamped. |
| Proximity pause hysteresis (`test_proximity_pause_hysteresis`) | Off (Experimental; root review 2026-10-14) | Near held 1.5 s before the camera pauses and two toggles at least 3 s apart. Shipped behaviour (pause and resume follow the sensor at once) stays until the 50-cycle ear check on the ASUS. |

### Reliability banner and phone-maker guidance (batch B2)

On by default as text and diagnostics (off with `test_device_policy_off=<id>`): `bg-oem-honor`, `bg-oem-infinix-itel`, `bg-system-bluetooth-app`, `bg-phone-updated` (a phone-update stamp: build fingerprint, patch and boot count, kept locally), `bg-incoming-call-popup`, `bg-killer-package-scan`, `bg-process-exit` (the last unexpected kill reason), `bg-scan-paused-by-maker`.

| Switch | Default | What it does and why it is kept |
| --- | --- | --- |
| MIUI/HyperOS app-ops probe (`test_miui_appops_probe`) | Off (Experimental) | Reads the background pop-up permission through the hidden app-ops service on Xiaomi-family phones to refine the incoming-call advice. Planned check: Xiaomi HyperOS card. |

### Advertising (batch A)

On by default as after-failure behaviour (each has a control arm): watchdog restart of an unexpectedly stopped set, title/reach preemption after TOO_MANY, stop-settle wait up to 0.7 s, non-throwing start, learned budget, stuck report, 28-byte payload test.

| Switch | Default | What it does and why it is kept |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Keep the set when an in-place disable is never confirmed off (`test_adv_inplace_keep_set=false`) | On | Control arm (2026-10-20). The call listener's in-place balanced step (disable, set parameters, enable on the same set) normally keeps the address. On an Android 11 ASUS the stack disabled the set but its confirmation never reached the app (DeadObjectException on the stack's callback), so after the 2 s step timeout the set was replaced with a new address 12 s after every low-latency start, and a PC's connect in flight failed. On: a step still waiting for that disable enables the same set again (same address, low latency kept), and the process skips later balanced steps; a timeout in the later steps restarts as before. Lines `adv_set_churn: event=reenable_same_set|balanced_skipped`. **Requires / masked by:** `stable_adv_address` (the in-place step). |
| Boost and its end on the same set off (`test_adv_inplace_boost=false`) | On | Control arm (2026-10-21). Showing the Meet code boosts the call listener to low latency and its end returns it to balanced; both are now in-place steps on the same advertising set (disable, set parameters, enable), so the address stays and a caller that just saw it still reaches it. A step on a set already in that mode does nothing (before, the second balanced step on a boosted set replaced it: one more address). A phone whose in-place disable is never confirmed keeps the set as it is. A failed step replaces the set in the target mode. The control arm replaces the set for the boost and its end, as before. **Requires / masked by:** `stable_adv_address` (the in-place step). |
| Watchdog, preemption and stop-settle control arms (`test_adv_watchdog=false`, `test_adv_preempt=false`, `test_adv_stop_settle=false`) | All on | false restores the earlier behaviour for an A/B in either direction. |
| Advertising budget simulation (`test_adv_budget_cap=<1..8>`) | Off (Experimental) | Act as if the controller offered only N sets, to exercise the one-instance paths (no owned 1-slot phone). Planned check: N=1. |
| Quiesce before stop (`test_adv_quiesce_stop`) | Off (Experimental) | Disable a set and let that confirm before stopping it, so a stop cannot meet the stack's address-rotation command (the Android 15 slot-leak hypothesis). |

### PHY and link-layer procedures (batch P)

On by default (off with `test_device_policy_off=<id>`): `android13-phy-update-callback-backstop` (poll readPhy when the PHY callback may be missing), `le-priority-request-supervision-log`, `dual-role-probe-log`.

| Switch | Default | What it does and why it is kept |
| --- | --- | --- |
| LL procedure gate (`test_ll_gate`) | Off (Experimental) | One MTU, discovery, PHY or connection-update request in flight per link with settle gaps, a peripheral waiting for the central's first connection update, and one 1M retry on 0x23/0x2A. Delays the 2M start by seconds. Off: every request goes straight to the stack as before (the gate is a pass-through). On, it also switches on an in-session rollback memory (a link lost after a gated change remembers that change as bad for the peer until the app process ends); the persisted memory is the next row. |
| Link change rollback (`test_link_change_rollback`) | Off (Experimental) | A link lost with a fault status within 10 s of an MTU, PHY or priority change remembers that change as bad for the peer (persisted; cleared by a Bluetooth restart, a security-patch change or 7 days). The MTU and PHY rollbacks are applied; a lost link after a priority change is recorded but nothing rolls the priority back (a PLAN idea, not a switch). |
| Priority re-assert (`test_priority_reassert`) | Off (Experimental) | When the stack reports an interval above the requested priority's maximum (CONN-02) the same priority is requested again, at most twice. Off: the drift is only logged. |

## Discovery privacy, identity and messages

| Switch | Default | What it does and why it is kept |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Windows: hardware identity key (`MESHLIVE_TPM_IDENTITY=1`) | Off (Experimental) | A new identity's signing key is created in the PC's TPM (Platform Crypto Provider, non-exportable); the software key store is used only on a PC without a usable chip, and any other chip failure is an error; an existing identity never moves (its ID would change), and a recorded chip identity is never silently replaced; while a record names the chip or the switch is on, Delete everything stays incomplete until the chip key is confirmed gone (with no record and the switch off, a chip that fails twice is logged and the wipe completes); Settings > Privacy says where the key is. Off because a chip signature or key open costs about 47 ms (median; 62-91 ms p95) on the development laptop against under 1 ms in software, and call setup does both; it is promoted after the identity stays open for the whole process and call setup is re-measured. |
| Rotating discovery IDs, phases 1, 1b and 2 (`test_rotating_ids`; no Settings switch since 2026-11-04; DEBUG `test_rotating_visible_window`, `test_rotating_neutral_name`, `test_relay_callee_rotating`) | Promoted 2026-10-12 for 0.2.0-beta: default ON in user launches (one constant, `RotatingDiscoveryFeature.DEFAULT_ENABLED`). Since 2026-11-04 a constant with no user switch: an "off" stored by 0.4.4 or older is ignored, because a phone with it off could not find phones that had it on. A DEBUG benchmark launch reads only `test_rotating_ids` | LE (CoC/GATT) call-ready presence, stream offers, viewer requests and forwarder offers broadcast a 15-minute rotating ID (announcement version 5; compact UUID v2 decodes too) instead of the fixed node prefix, so a passive scanner cannot follow a phone between places. Each boundary restarts every app advertising set (call listener, stream offer, waiting viewer request, forwarder offer, title pages) after a random 1-8 s silence on a new address; a connection in progress keeps its link. Whoever connects proves the ID it advertised with a link opening `BDO1` (hop type 13, link hello bit 3; stream connector role 1, viewer role 2, call listener role 0) checked against the advert it selected, with a two-epoch window against precomputed forgery; call endpoints exchange the callee's `BDX1` opening (endpoint hello bit 3) before its first answer, which also verifies relayed calls end to end (the DEBUG call relay mirrors version 5 offers). Stream tokens follow the ID and a source accepts the previous token for 60 s; a viewer that lost a forwarder sends a recovery hint bound to its own ID. A rotating viewer that joins an older (version 2) source shows its fixed ID while it joins (status line and `rotid_downgrade`). Contacts: mutual verified contacts that both offer exchange daily presence leases in an answered call (per-contact switch "Can see when I'm nearby" on the verify screen, default on; sealed store; Forget, Reset and the switch start a new key, "Reset presence key" in Settings) and then see "name . seen nearby . unconfirmed" in the people and stream lists (never a check mark, never an automatic connection). Strangers' repeated silent handshakes wait 2^(n-6) s (max 30 s). Hardening (2026-10-11): on API 34+ every connectable app advertising set is non-discoverable (control: DEBUG `test_adv_non_discoverable=false`), the phone is no longer renamed automatically (the explicit neutral-name option has its own row), contact keys are independent random daily keys in a rolling window with the earlier HMAC derivation kept only to migrate an old store, version 5 viewer requests carry no codec summary and stream bitrates are rounded to 25 kbps, and a legacy-only controller is detected by capability and reported (`rotid_address_reuse`). The Settings button "Visible by handle for 10-25 minutes" advertises version 2 (calls and streams) so old builds can find the phone. Classic roles, the DEBUG direct-receiver mode and `BGK1` group hops stay version 2; old builds cannot see the phone outside a window. Promoted after the scanner test (P5 PASS, zero carry-over at 3 of 3 boundaries) and the phone cases P1 to P4 and P7 of the combined privacy session of 2026-10-11 (build 3996bfb2); P6 (battery) was not measured. PROTOCOL "Experimental rotating discovery IDs". Lines `rotating_ids: enabled=`, `rotid_open`, `rotid_tarpit`, `rotid_name`, `rotid_adv`, `rotid_address_reuse`, `rotid_visible_window`, `rotid_presence` (including `event=window`), `rotid_downgrade`, DEBUG `rotid_boundary`, `rotid_restart`, `adv_set_churn`, `adv_address_seen` (`tools/rotid_phase0.py`, `tools/rotid_tracker.py`). Fast retest: DEBUG `test_rotid_epoch_s=60` shortens the epoch to one minute. |
| Debug contact verification and day-roll clock (`test_verify_contact=<token>`, `test_forget_contact=<token>`, `test_presence_day_offset=<n>`; DEBUG builds of a debuggable package only) | No trigger; clock unshifted | Lets the rotating-ID contact-verification test run without two humans scanning QR codes. Same gating as the debug emergency wipe: adb writes a one-shot file into app-private storage with `run-as` (`files/debug-verify-contact` or `files/debug-forget-contact`: a 32-128 hex token, the peer's full 64-hex fingerprint, an optional name) and the launch extra must carry the same token. At launch or on a new intent the app deletes the file when the token matches, then marks that fingerprint verified in the same Keystore-sealed verified-contact store the QR flow writes (method QR_SCAN) and logs `BENCHMARK contact_verified source=debug fingerprint_prefix=...`; forget calls `IdentityVerification.forget` (including presence-lease revocation). A mismatch leaves the file and does nothing; its own fingerprint is refused. `BENCHMARK identity fingerprint=<64 hex> node=<32 hex>` is logged at every debug launch; `tools/debug_verify_contacts.py --phones A,B [--forget]` reads it from both phones and writes the files. `test_presence_day_offset` (cold start, 0-28, tunable `presence_day_offset`) adds whole days to the discovery wall clock only (`DiscoveryWallClock`: epoch ticks, the RD4 tolerance, recognition rebuilds, the Visible window and lease received-times); use the same value on both phones of a pair, and clear app data afterwards (RV4 recovery otherwise needs an hour). Release builds ignore all three extras. Not yet tested on phones. |
| DEBUG short rotating-ID epoch (`test_rotid_epoch_s=<60..900>`; tunable `rotid_epoch_s`; DEBUG builds of a debuggable package only) | Off: 900 s epochs | Shortens the epoch of the rotating discovery IDs so a functional phone test sees three boundaries in 3 minutes (60 s) instead of 45. Read once at cold start before rotating discovery starts (a later intent in a running process is ignored); release builds, non-debuggable packages, values outside 60-900 and launches without the extra keep 900 s. The wire format is unchanged (`e = floor(unix_seconds / S)`); every epoch-counted rule keeps its meaning in epochs, presence-lease days stay real days (`floor(e * S / 86400)`, use `test_presence_day_offset` to roll days). **Give every phone of a test the same value**: unequal lengths fail the two-epoch opening check as stale, by design. The stored RV4 counter carries its length, so the next normal launch restarts at the wall epoch (no app-data clearing needed; contacts' stored epoch skews correct themselves at the next lease exchange). Line `BENCHMARK rotid_epoch_s: requested= | epochSeconds= | applied=`; `tools/rotid_tracker.py --epoch-s <S>` places boundaries and scales the grace and early offsets. Tests: `ShortEpochHarnessTest`, `tools/tests/test_rotid_tracker.py`. PROTOCOL "DEBUG short epoch". Not yet run on phones. |
| Hidden identities: the BMH3 handshake (on, no Settings switch since 2026-11-04, a stored choice is ignored; DEBUG `test_bmh3` is the benchmark switch, DEBUG sub-options `test_bmh3_only`, `test_bmh3_contacts_only` and `test_bmh3_test_lease=<64 hex>`) | Promoted 2026-10-12 for 0.2.0-beta: default ON in user launches (one constant, `ConnectivityFeatureDefaults.BMH3`; the Settings switch is the off-switch; a DEBUG benchmark launch still reads only `test_bmh3`) after the BMH3 test steps run in the combined privacy session of 2026-10-11 (build 3996bfb2) | Rotating discovery IDs hide the advertisement only; BMH2 sends both identity keys in the clear on every hop and shows the responder's key to anyone who connects. BMH3 (SIGMA, PROTOCOL "Experimental BMH3 identity-hiding link handshake") sends only a one-time key and a nonce first, then each identity, its capabilities, a signature over the whole transcript and a key-confirmation MAC encrypted under keys derived from that exchange: a passive sniffer learns neither identity, and a caller reveals itself only after the answerer's signature, the discovery binding (advert prefix or rotating-ID opening) and, for a recognised contact, its full node ID passed. Both phones need it. With the switch on a call to a rotating-ID advert is always BMH3, a call to a fixed-prefix advert uses BMH3 when the callee's announcement carries one more capability bit (`capabilitySummary` bit 5) or the prefix is remembered from an earlier BMH3 call (30 days, kept in no-backup storage), and otherwise stays BMH2 so older phones keep working; toward a relay the caller presents a throwaway key. The updater's build tag, when that switch is on, travels inside the encrypted block instead of the cleartext hello. Costs: 887 handshake bytes against about 638, one more bit on air, and phones with rotating IDs on but BMH3 off cannot be called by phones with both on. **What it does not hide:** a phone that accepts calls from anyone still shows its identity to someone who connects to it on purpose (the public tracking warning stays true for that case; a Contacts-only mode keyed by the presence leases is Phase 2, a DEBUG fixed-lease version exists for the test), message sizes and timing, the order (3 or 4 messages), the advertisement itself, and any caller that speaks BMH2 to a fixed-prefix advert whose hint was stripped. Phase 1 covers calls and message exchanges, not streams, viewers, forwarders or Design C helper routes. Lines `bmh3: event=link|endpoint | role= | order= | result= | ms=` (no identifiers), `event=choice | version= | reason=`. PLAN "Design F" holds both security reviews (SR1-SR14, BR1-BR11) and the Phase 2 list. **Phone test (PLAN Design F has the full list), pass when:** (1) switch off: calls unchanged and an HCI snoop of a call shows the BMH2 hello (the control); (2) switch on, every ordered pair and transport, both roles: ringing time within +300 ms of BMH2 on CoC and Classic and +600 ms on GATT; (3) `tools/bmh3_snoop_check.py --expect hidden` finds no SPKI, node ID, prefix or capability block of either phone in any L2CAP or ATT stream of a BMH3 call; (4) a Contacts-only responder answers a stranger with at most the 105-byte msg2 and a lease holder with the full handshake; (5) a copied rotating advert, also at route cost 1, collects nothing from the caller; (6) the negotiation matrix fails closed as specified; (7) relays, message exchange, replay and flood checks and the combined rotating-ID passive-tracker run pass. **BMH3 test result:** steps 1, 3 and 6 passed on the S24 and ASUS (control call with the cleartext hello visible; snoop check finds none of the four identity needles in the stranger calls; negotiation: BMH3 caller to BMH3-off callee works over BMH2, the reverse works, `test_bmh3_only` to a BMH3-off callee is refused); step 2 was measured on one pair and transport only (GATT S24 to ASUS, 3 calls per arm: mean invite to ringing 3.8 s with BMH3 against 3.3 s without, link handshake 101 to 137 ms, end-to-end 115 to 838 ms, variance dominated by GATT connection time), so the +600 ms GATT gate is not decided and CoC and Classic were not run; steps 4 (active probe with a lease key), 5 (fake adverts), 7 (relays), 8 (message exchange), 10 (abuse) and 11 (battery) were not run. |
| Pseudonymous toward strangers on top of BMH3 (on, no Settings switch since 2026-11-04, a stored choice is ignored, a child of "Hide my ID from strangers"; DEBUG `test_bmh3_pseudonyms` next to `test_bmh3`) | Promoted 2026-10-12 for 0.2.0-beta: default ON in user launches (one constant, `ConnectivityFeatureDefaults.BMH3_PSEUDONYMS`; the Settings switch is the off-switch; a DEBUG benchmark launch reads only `test_bmh3_pseudonyms`); needs BMH3 and rotating discovery IDs, and is off at run time while either is off | Design decision: a phone that accepts calls from anyone should not hand its long-term identity to whoever connects. With the switch on, every connection that is not a recognised contact meets a throwaway signing key (a fresh one per connection for the callee, per attempt for the caller; software keys, never the Keystore, nothing derived from your identity); a recognised contact (the presence-lease tag, now wired to the real lease keys) still gets the real identity at once. The callee rings as an "unverified stranger"; if the user accepts, the callee sends a signed `REVEAL` (bound to that session, with the opening of the rotating ID that it withheld until then), the caller checks that it opens the offer it selected and the verified-contact store, and only then reveals itself; declining reveals nothing. The salt of the rotating ID, which would let someone who knows your public key confirm that you are here, is withheld from strangers; BMH2 is refused (it would answer with the real key), the replay cache and signing budget are kept per 15-minute epoch, the caller name is held until the callee is bound. Message exchanges and relay requests are refused with a stranger (their cards and bundle IDs are stable identities); relayed calls to such a phone, Design C helpers and phones with BMH3 off cannot reach it, and a BMH3-on caller without this switch cannot call it. Streams still answer BMH2 and show the source (streams on BMH3 with a per-stream pseudonym are specified in PROTOCOL, not built). **What it does not hide, and what stays true for the public warning:** accepting a call from a stranger shows them who you are and calling a stranger shows whoever answers who you are; someone who can see you can ring phones and watch which reacts, and a connection held across the 15-minute change can link the old and new ID; within one epoch the advert and every connection are the same phone; anyone who connects learns phone class, app build and feature bits; Bluetooth leaks outside the protocol (GAP name on older Android, Classic, chip fingerprints, bonded addresses); streams you send; contacts' leases for up to four weeks. Lines `bmh3: event=present | layer= | role= | key=real|pseudonym | reason=`, `event=reveal | role= | result=`, `event=lease_misuse`. PLAN "Design F" holds the review (PR1-PR15). **Phone test (inside the one combined privacy session, PLAN "Phone test"), pass when:** a stranger call shows no node ID or name before Accept and the prober phone's log shows a different throwaway per attempt; the real identity appears on both phones only after Accept; a verified-contact call reveals nothing (real keys at once, no `REVEAL`); a BMH2 hello is refused; `bmh3_snoop_check.py --expect hidden` finds no real SPKI in any stranger call. **Pseudonym test result:** a stranger call showed throwaways on both layers and both phones and a different one per attempt (also across a boundary and after a restart inside the epoch); the callee rang as an unverified stranger; the reveal followed Accept in order (caller verified and sent, callee sent and verified); a declined call had no reveal; the snoop check found none of the four needles in the stranger calls; verified contacts called with real keys and no reveal after their lease exchange. Not run: wrong contact (step 5), a prober across a boundary (step 6; the BMH2 prober call hung 170 s in OUTGOING, now bounded to 10 s of hop handshake with a reason on the caller and a log line on the answerer), message exchange and relayed call refusal for a stranger (step 8). |
| Non-discoverable connectable sets (part of rotating discovery IDs; DEBUG `test_adv_non_discoverable`) | On while rotating IDs are on, API 34+ only; `=false` is the control arm | Pre-handshake exposure of the GAP Device Name, hardening 2026-10-11 (PROTOCOL "Pre-handshake exposure"). Every connectable app set starts with `AdvertisingSetParameters.Builder.setDiscoverable(false)`: AOSP then drops the Flags AD and refuses the Device Name (0x2A00) to an unbonded, unencrypted central that connected through the set (`gap_ble.cc`, Android 14 and newer; verified in the sources, to be measured per phone). Not covered: links this phone opens as central, Android 13 and older, legacy-only controllers, OEM stacks that differ, Classic. A stack that refuses the parameters gets the set started discoverable again, visibly and logged. Phone test P7 measured the advertising side (S24 non-discoverable; ASUS and M31 not); its unpaired-central probe is the DEBUG extra `test_gap_name_probe=read|pair` (any type of extra works since 2026-10-12; it starts from onResume as well as after the permission flow, and logs `gap_name_probe: event=skipped | reason=` when it cannot run). |
| Discoverable call offers while the name is neutral (B2, 2026-10-27; DEBUG `test_discoverable_when_neutral=true` or `=false`) | Default on exactly when the neutral-name option ("Hide phone name", on by default since 0.4.1 after the user's answer) is on; otherwise off (sets stay non-discoverable as before). Release builds ignore the extra | A Mac (CoreBluetooth) reads the GAP Device Name (0x2A00) right after connecting; through a non-discoverable set on Android 14+ that read fails with ATT error 0x05 and macOS starts LE pairing, with a prompt on both devices. The call-offer and viewer-request sets (`DiscoverableWhenNeutral`, `StableAdvertisingSet(neutralRole=)`) are started discoverable only when the switch is on and the adapter name read just before the start is exactly "Phone"; a rename away from "Phone" (`ACTION_LOCAL_NAME_CHANGED`) stops each such running set and its owner restarts it non-discoverable. Stream offers, forwarder offers, the in-call renewal advert and the name-window offer are unchanged. No wire change. Log (DEBUG) `adv_discoverable: set= ; discoverable= ; reason=` (pipe-separated in the log; set `call_offer` or `viewer_request`; reason `neutral_name`, `switch_off`, `name_not_neutral`, `name_unreadable` or `name_changed`). Phone evidence before the switch existed: m5-v1 (non-discoverable: 0x2A00 refused, SMP prompt) vs m5-v2 (`test_adv_non_discoverable=false` + neutral name: name read, no SMP, link open 1.7-2.3 s). Not yet measured with this switch (DECISIONS "Mac name read: B2"). |
| Use a neutral Bluetooth name ("Phone") (Settings > Privacy "Hide phone name"; preference `neutral_bluetooth_name`; onboarding page "Hide your phone's name"; DEBUG `test_neutral_bluetooth_name`, alias `test_rotating_neutral_name`) | On by default since 0.4.1 (design decision 2026-10-27, DECISIONS "Hide phone name on by default"): one constant, `NeutralNameOption.spec` `defaultEnabled`; a stored choice always wins, so someone who turned it off keeps it off. The phone is renamed only after the user answers the question (onboarding page for a new install, a one-time dialog after an update for someone who never chose: "Hide name" / "Keep my name"). A DEBUG benchmark launch reads only its extra, default off | Turned on by default so a Mac or PC can call without a pairing prompt (with B2 below) and the real name is not broadcast. Cost: the phone shows as "Phone" to headphones, cars and other devices. Without the Nearby devices permission, or when Android refuses the rename, the switch shows off with a short reason. Replaces the automatic rename that came with the rotating switch. Asks first, shows the saved original name, restores it on switch-off, never writes the name per session, pauses itself when the user renames the phone, and keeps the name of an earlier automatic rename with a one-time notice (a benchmark launch restores it). Recommended on Android 13 and older, on legacy-only controllers and with Classic roles. Uninstalling while it is on leaves `Phone` until the phone is renamed in Android settings. |
| Relayed calls and multi-hop discovery (`test_relayed_calls`; Settings > Experiments "Relayed calls (beta)"; DEBUG `test_relayed_calls_help`, `test_relayed_calls_consent`, `test_relayed_calls_max_relays` 1..4, `test_relayed_calls_require=<n>`, `test_relayed_calls_no_direct`) | Off. The switch is always shown under Experiments and its stored choice counts with or without Manual video settings (design decision 2026-11-06; until then it counted only while Manual settings was on, spec option `advancedOnly`) | Naming phase 2 (design decision2026-10-10; PLAN "Design C", independently reviewed 2026-10-11). Ordinary phones relay other people's private calls in the background and a caller can find and call a phone one or two relays away. On: a call-ready phone sets the consent bit (call-offer capability bit 4, "Let phones nearby relay calls to me") and, as a helper ("Help calls nearby", both preselected when the experiment is switched on), keeps a reach table from the adverts it scans itself, advertises its best entries in a second legacy set (reach pages, version 4, needs a free advertising set) and accepts one relay request at a time: the caller sends `SETUP` (hop type 12) after the BMH2 link handshake, each helper regenerates it for the next hop, `READY` comes back, and the existing opaque call relay copies the end-to-end records. Relays cannot read or impersonate (the caller still checks the target's prefix and a verified contact's full fingerprint), but each relay on the path sees the signed endpoint hello and the target prefix. Helping pauses below 20 % battery when not charging, in Battery Saver, when hot, or while the phone has its own call or stream; the phone's own activity ends a relay at once and a relayed call already running is not cut by a pause; the notification reads "Helping a call nearby". A chain is abandoned unless the end-to-end handshake crosses within 10 s and the callee answers within 20 s of READY, and a relayed incoming call that rings 60 s is declined. LE CoC/GATT only, one bearer end to end. Targets are fixed-prefix (version 2), or role-0 rotating IDs when rotating discovery IDs are also on (reach pages version 6, `SETUP` message 4, every hop checked with `BDO1`/`BDX1`; tables flush at each boundary; DEBUG `test_relayed_rotating=false` restores the earlier exclusion). Direct fallback fails a benchmark that requires a chain. Off: every byte on air and on the link is unchanged. PROTOCOL "Experimental relayed calls and multi-hop discovery". Lines `relayed_calls: enabled=`, `relayed_setup: role=`, `relayed_helper: state=`, `relayed_reach_table` (`entries` counts the fixed-prefix table, `rotating_entries` the role-0 one). First phone probe 2026-10-11 (ASUS helper, GATT, one relay, rotating IDs on and off): the helper app crashed 1.2 s after it accepted the SETUP (`InputStream.nullInputStream()` is API 33, minSdk is 29; fixed on `fix/relayed-call-gatt`, with `tools/dex_api_scan.py` and a source guard against the class); one-helper calls completed on GATT and CoC in build 3996bfb2 (case ). Two helpers (`require=2`) never formed a route there because the four phones were in one room: every phone hears the target, the table prefers the shortest path, so nobody advertised a two-relay entry and a first helper with budget 2 would have dialled the target directly. Experimental DEBUG topology mask `test_relayed_calls_no_direct=true` (default off) makes a phone ignore every target's own offer (offers of helpers are kept) as if out of radio range: mark the caller and the first helper, leave the second helper normal (PLAN "Design C" phone verification queue). Production behaviour (shortest path wins) is unchanged. The lab route controls (`test_relayed_calls_require`, `test_relayed_calls_no_direct` and the helper and next-hop pins) take effect in every DEBUG launch, a person's launch (`test_user_launch=true`) included; release builds never read them. The `relayed_reach_table` line now also prints `costs=`/`rotating_costs=` (entries by cost heard), and a refused required-chain call logs `relayed_call: refused`. The setting takes effect only while Advanced settings are visible; hiding them turns relayed calls off. Under the BMH3 pseudonym defaults relayed calls need DECISIONS Design C "Amendment CP" (the rows below). |
| Pseudonymous relay edges (`test_relayed_pseudonymous_edges=false` is the control arm) [relayed_pseudonymous_edges] | On (2026-10-27, reviews CP-1) | Every relay edge runs BMH3 with a throwaway that declares PSEUDONYM, fresh per connection, never BMH2 while pseudonym mode is on; a hop answering with a real key is bound to the selected offer, a declared throwaway is not; a helper accepts one `SETUP` on such a link; per-parent limit keyed on the remote address; own-ID requests answer FAIL 1; closures before READY are FAIL 4; a route is marked used only after the target's identity checked out. Off: relayed calls fail at the first link under the BMH3 pseudonym defaults (`pseudonymous_answerer_to_a_real_caller`). DECISIONS Design C "Amendment CP". Not yet run on phones. |
| Relayed stranger calls (`test_relayed_stranger_calls=false` is the control arm, contacts only) [relayed_stranger_calls] | On within the relayed-calls experiment (design decision 2026-10-27) | A relayed call whose end-to-end caller is a throwaway rings, with the status "via a phone nearby" and the wording that a reveal goes to whoever holds the call, which may be a relaying phone; a caller may place a relayed call to a target it has no contact tag for. Off: the target declines such calls quietly before ringing and the caller dials relayed routes only to tagged contacts. A relay can sit in the middle of a stranger call (the callee's reveal is the exposure); contact calls are protected by the full node-ID check. Independently of this switch, a target declines any relayed call unless its relayed calls and relay consent are both on, and caps every relayed ring at 60 s. |
| Contact-helper relay edge - Experimental (`test_relayed_contact_helper=true`) [relayed_contact_helper] | Off | A caller that recognises the selected helper's offer as exactly one contact tags that helper's own offer and runs real/real with the opening check, instead of a throwaway: the first hop is authenticated, at the price that the helper learns who is calling. A throwaway answer fails that candidate (no anonymous retry). |
| Routed strangers in the Nearby list (DEBUG `test_relayed_list_strangers=true`; part of relayed calls) | Off (contacts only) | Design decision 2026-10-26: with relayed calls on, the Nearby list shows a person reached only through other phones when they are a saved contact (fixed prefix or a rotating ID recognised from their presence lease); strangers several phones away are not listed. Tapping Call on a contact searches direct and routed sightings and waits up to 45 s (two relays) before saying the contact cannot be found. On: the earlier arm that lists everyone reachable through helpers ("via 1 phone"). The background reach tables are unchanged either way; an on-demand route query (no background tables) is a separate design. **Requires / masked by:** relayed calls (see the row above); the whole relayed family is inert without it. |
| Experimental: on-demand relayed route search, Design OR revision 3 (DEBUG launch extras only, no release or Advanced setting: `test_relayed_on_demand` arm O, `test_relayed_on_demand_keep_tables` arm B, tunables `test_relayed_seek_scan_ms` 1000..6000, `test_relayed_seek_first_hops` 1..3, `test_relayed_seek_fanout` 1..2, `test_relayed_seek_order=strong|far`, `test_relayed_seek_connect_ms`, `test_relayed_seek_budget_scale` 1..20, `test_relayed_seek_no_backoff`; all need `test_relayed_calls`) | Off | A/B arm of the proactive reach tables (DECISIONS "Design OR", PROTOCOL "Draft: on-demand route seek"). Arm O: helpers run no reach-table scan and no reach pages; a contact call not heard directly searches through up to three first hops in expanding rings (`SEEK`, hop type 12 message 5, a blinded 6-byte token; budget 1 to every first hop, then budget 2 with a skip list) under one 45 s give-up time; helpers sign BMH3 extension type 5 `RELAY_SEEK`, take a phone-wide relay duty and charge phone-wide budgets (60 s duty, 30 s scanning, 12 dials and 12 handshakes per 10 minutes); the target declines a relayed call unless its current consent is on. Scope OR-1: rotating IDs and BMH3 on, pseudonyms off on helpers and targets (Amendment CP not built), version 5 targets only. Kept off: prototype for the A/B; a user-facing build needs OR-T and a design decision (the presence-oracle question).
