With Amendment CP (`test_relayed_pseudonymous_edges`, on by default): the declared-throwaway onward edge applies to a helper's SETUP duty only; a search keeps its own BMH3 onward link and identity checks, and an incoming relayed call must pass both the CP decision (`RelayIdentityPolicy.incoming`) and this prototype's consent check. |
| iPhone compact discovery (Settings > Features "Find iPhones"; DEBUG `test_ios_compact_discovery` is the benchmark switch) | On since 2026-10-17 (first physical iPhone: calls and streams both ways passed; design decision; key renamed `_v2`; one constant, `ConnectivityFeatureDefaults.IPHONE_DISCOVERY`) | Discovery also accepts the one-UUID announcement a foreground iPhone can advertise (iOS apps cannot send manufacturer data): one more masked scan filter (top 16 bits `0xC8D6`) in the same scan start, decoded into the ordinary announcement, so iPhone call offers, viewer requests and stream offers reach the existing lists, matching and handshakes. The 22-byte announcement wins when both are present; this phone's advertising and all authentication are unchanged. On by default; the masked-filter behaviour of the six reference phones is still to be checked (phone test below). PROTOCOL "Experimental iPhone compact discovery". Line `compact_discovery: enabled=`, DEBUG `scan_stats ... compact= | compactInvalid=`. The masked service-UUID filter is the unknown: a phone whose scanner refuses it (`SCAN_FAILED_FEATURE_UNSUPPORTED` or `OUT_OF_HARDWARE_RESOURCES` while the filter is active) drops only that filter for the rest of the process and restarts the scan with the app filter alone, logging `compact_filter: event=dropped | code=`, so Android-to-Android discovery never depends on it. **Phone test (all six Android phones, plus an iPhone with the app in the foreground when available), pass when:** (1) no scan failure with the filter active on any phone, or a failure ends in the logged drop with discovery still working; (2) Android-to-Android discovery counts over three 60 s scans equal the switch-off counts (`scan_stats ... accepted=`); (3) with an iPhone announcing, `compact=` above 0 with `compactInvalid=0`, and the iPhone appears in the call and stream lists within 10 s. Not yet tested on phones. |
| Find PCs (Settings > Experiments "Find PCs (experimental)"; DEBUG `test_find_pcs` is the benchmark switch; preference `experimental_find_pcs`; one constant, `ConnectivityFeatureDefaults.FIND_PCS`) | Off. Experimental (Design QW, DECISIONS) | A Windows PC advertises one connectable EXTENDED advert whose 128-bit service data under `c8d5a10a-9f88-4f65-a03d-7f5a2fb921fa` is a strict 9-byte rotating call offer (version/kind, RDI, flags GATT/relay/consent, codec and cost; shared vectors `app/src/test/resources/pc_announcement/pc_announcement_v1.json`). On, a call-list scanner (one that accepts rotating offers) adds one `setServiceData(uuid, empty)` filter next to the existing ones and, only where `BluetoothAdapter.isLeExtendedAdvertisingSupported`, `setLegacy(false)` with `PHY_LE_1M` (the PC's secondary PHY is LE 1M per gate 1, and Coded scanning hurt reception on the Xiaomi in F11; for that scan only, the F11 LE-1M-only settings otherwise stay) and logs `pc_scan: ... extended_supported=`; without extended support it logs `pc_scan: unsupported reason=no_extended` and does nothing else. A valid payload maps to the ordinary rotating call offer (version 5, ACCEPTING|CALL|GATT, +RELAY when relay-capable, summary = codecs, +0x10 on consent, cost from the payload) and goes through `acceptAnnouncement` like any rotating offer, so rotating IDs must also be on. Precedence in one record: 22-byte manufacturer announcement, then compact UUID, then PC service data. Own counters `scan_stats ... pc= | pcInvalid=` (shown only while on), one rate-limited log line per decoded announcement (`pc_announcement:`) and per rejection with its error (`pc_announcement_rejected:`). Filter-slot guard: on `SCAN_FAILED_FEATURE_UNSUPPORTED` or `OUT_OF_HARDWARE_RESOURCES` the PC filter (and the extended settings) is dropped FIRST for the rest of the process (`pc_filter: event=dropped`), then the compact filter, never the app filter, so normal discovery cannot be taken down. Off: filters and settings are exactly as before (`DiscoveryScanPlan`, JVM-tested). Android never advertises this form. **Phone test (QW, not yet run):** screen-on and screen-off PC appearance time, filter offload status and slot exhaustion on every required phone, and a phone calling the PC. |
| PC live streams (Settings > Experiments "PC live streams (experimental)"; DEBUG `test_pc_streams` is the benchmark switch; preference `experimental_pc_streams`; Design QV revision 4) | Off. Experimental (DECISIONS "Design QV") | Needs Find PCs. With both on, a Windows PC's kind 1 viewer requests and kind 2 stream offers map to ordinary version 5 announcements. With this switch off they are dropped and counted as `pcDisabled`. The existing PC-filter guard, precedence, GATT transport, admission, openings and encryption are unchanged. Extended-capable phones only; physical interoperability remains unmeasured for this implementation. |
| Names and titles, called "Names and subjects" until the call subject was retired on 2026-11-05 (no Settings switch since 2026-10-17, design decision: an empty name is anonymous; a constant with no stored choice; DEBUG `test_peer_labels` is the benchmark switch) [peer_labels] | On since 2026-10-17 (`CallFeatureDefaults.NAMES_AND_TITLES`; design decision) | Naming design phase 1 (review R1-R10, R13; design decision2026-10-10). Settings > Your ID gets an optional name (empty = anonymous: handle only); Go live asks for an optional title (the call subject is retired since 2026-11-05: never sent or shown, and one from an older build is dropped silently; PROTOCOL "Peer labels"). Names are identity-signed `BNL1` labels bound to the call ID, sent only inside the encrypted endpoint session when both endpoint hellos carry bit 5 (the callee's name only with Accept, or at Ringing to a verified contact); stream titles are bound to the stream ID, sent to direct viewers before the header and passed on unchanged by forwarders after `GROUP_KEY`. The title is also advertised publicly in a second non-connectable legacy set (title pages, one-time free-set probe `BENCHMARK adv_set_probe`; no free set = signed title only) and shown "unconfirmed" until the signed copy arrives after joining. That set is the registry entry `peer-label-title-advertising-set` (DEBUG `test_device_policy_off=peer-label-title-advertising-set` removes it; the matcher is every phone until a stack is shown to mishandle two legacy sets). Self-claimed names are quoted beside the full handle; strangers always show the full handle (R5, also with the switch off). A self-claimed name whose UTS #39 confusable skeleton equals a contact's name or your own (for example an all-Cyrillic look-alike of "Alice", which the script check alone passes) is flagged on the incoming-call screen: "Looks like your contact Alice. Check the ID before you answer." The bundled table is the unmodified Unicode 18.0.0 `confusables.txt` MA data (`assets/uts39/confusables_ma.txt`, Unicode License v3 in THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES); a minimal Cyrillic/Greek table is the fallback if it cannot be read. Off: no hello bit, no type-10 record (wire unchanged). Since Naming v2 the public title pages are their own switch, "Show my stream title to everyone nearby", below; a user who had explicitly set this switch keeps that choice for the pages (one-time carry-over), and a DEBUG launch with `test_peer_labels` still switches the pages too. DEBUG extras `test_peer_label_name`, `test_stream_title`; lines `BENCHMARK peer_labels ...`. PROTOCOL "Peer labels". Not yet tested on phones. |
| Store-and-forward messages (Settings > Experiments "Chats (beta)"; DEBUG `test_store_forward` is the benchmark switch) | Off (one constant, `CallFeatureDefaults.MESSAGES`, false; flipped only after its phone test passes). With Messages on, chat uses Messaging v2 (see that section) | Intercom pillar, PLAN Design A v1 (direct neighbours only; the carrier role stays off). Home shows Messages with an unread count; the screen follows the shared monochrome theme. Copy your contact card or add one from text, accept cards received over a link after comparing their safety number, write text, a voice note (up to 2 min) or a photo (up to 1280 px) with a 1/3/7/14-day lifetime, and read an encrypted-at-rest inbox and sent list; unread inbox rows carry a dot until the screen is left. A new message after an exchange posts one quiet notification ("N new messages", never text or sender; VISIBILITY_SECRET, so nothing on a secure lock screen) only when the app and the Messages screen are not showing. A voice note cannot be recorded while a call or live stream holds the microphone. Messages move when both phones have Call open and one taps the other and chooses Exchange messages, or by themselves with "Sync messages automatically". A call tap or an incoming call pauses a running exchange within one chunk. The screen is FLAG_SECURE and the copied card is marked sensitive. Everything is stored under `noBackupFilesDir/store-forward` (excluded from backup and device transfer) plus a voice-note recording in `cache/` while capturing; the emergency wipe deletes both and both Keystore keys. Wire format: PROTOCOL "Store-and-forward messages" (hop type 11). DEBUG controls `sf_sync:PREFIX`, `sf_send:PREFIX:BYTES[:COUNT]`, `sf_accept_cards`, `sf_suspend`, `sf_status`, `sf_clear`, `sf_background` (measurement without taps, TESTING "Message transfer measurement"); lines `BENCHMARK store_forward session` and `BENCHMARK message_transfer session|bundle|app`. Not yet tested on phones. |
| Automatic store-and-forward sync (Settings > Experiments and the Chats screen, "Send chats automatically"; needs Messages; DEBUG `test_sf_auto_sync` is the benchmark switch) | Off (one constant, `CallFeatureDefaults.AUTO_SYNC`, false; flipped only after its phone test passes) | Starts the same BSF1 exchange without a tap. Local only: no wire change and the same responder limits (60 s cooldown, 10 per hour). Sightings from the Call screen's existing discovery trigger it; Home keeps advertising without scanning, and a Home phone takes part as the responder. It runs for an accepted contact when this phone has queued messages for them or carried copies (carrier role, hard-off in v1). Otherwise it pulls what they may hold, at most every 10-30 min. Backoff is per peer: 65 s x 1.5^failures, capped at 10 min, with +-15% jitter. The phone with the higher node prefix waits 10 s, and an exchange this phone answered also resets the wait. Live media wins: there is no start during a call, stream, relay, user selection or thermal block. A user tap or requested call cancels a pending start, and a running exchange suspends within one chunk. Outbox states are queued, carried, delivered and failed. Failed means the recipient was reached 8 times without taking the message; suspends and rate limits do not count. Attempts are sealed at rest in `outbox-attempts.properties`. A failed message stops triggering automatic sync, but a tapped Exchange still offers it. Re-implemented from Briar's backoff poller and Bitchat's outbox (patterns only, no code). Line `BENCHMARK sf_sync auto=feature|start|wait|skip|connecting|connect_failed|preempted|result`. JVM tests only; not yet tested on phones. |
| Raw advert observer (DEBUG `test_raw_observer=<hex substring>`, optional `test_raw_observer_seconds`, default 1800; `DebugRawObserver.kt`) | No trigger | Starts one extra unfiltered LOW_LATENCY scan (extended when supported) and logs each raw advertisement containing the hex (at most one line per address per second) plus a 60 s summary of the matched section and address first/last seen, to prove what a Windows PC puts on air (DECISIONS "Design QW"). Screen-on use; stops with the app or after the duration; DEBUG builds only, no UI. Any `test_` extra makes a benchmark launch. |
| Debug emergency wipe (`test_emergency_wipe=<token>`, DEBUG builds only) | No trigger | Lets a benchmark exercise the full wipe. It needs a one-shot 32-128 hex token that adb writes into internal storage with `run-as`; release builds have no external trigger. Panic and confirmed wipes are in-app only. |
| Block unknown callers (Settings > Your identity; preference `block_unknown_callers`; `CallBlocking.kt`) | Off | Declines an incoming private call without ringing when the caller is not a recognised verified contact: a stranger (one-time key), an unverified real key, and a contact whose presence lease expired (they too arrive as a one-time key). The caller sees the ordinary Ringing, then the ordinary Decline after 3-7 s; this phone shows nothing and records one "Blocked call" row in Recent. An unreadable contact store rings (fail open). Local only, no wire change. Untested on phones. |
| Blocked people (Settings > Blocked people; `BlockedPeople` store `identity-verification/blocked-people.bin`, sealed with the verified-contact Keystore key) | Empty list | A blocked person (full identity fingerprint) never rings and their own live streams are hidden from Watch: the route heard directly and attributed to them is hidden, and a copy relayed by another phone stays listed until it is opened, then playback leaves as soon as its source proves it is the blocked person and that route stays hidden. A stream's tag is chosen by its sender and not authenticated, so a claim never hides another sender's routes of the same tag (a blocked person cannot hide a genuine stream by copying its tag; review 2026-11-06). It matches only callers that present their real key before ringing (verified contacts with a current lease, phones that do not use pseudonyms); a stranger's one-time key can never match, which the block dialog says. A call you make to someone whose phone shows the real key of a person you blocked ends the ordinary way after 3-7 s (their phone rings, then sees an ordinary cancel, or an ordinary hang-up if they answered), with no name, camera, microphone or presence lease and without showing who they are (as Windows and Apple; before, only the name was withheld). Security fix, no switch. Erased by the emergency wipe. Local only, no wire change. Untested on phones. |
| Quick wipe gesture (Settings > Safety "Quick wipe: triple-tap the app name"; preference `panic_wipe_gesture`) | Off | Three quick taps on the home-screen app name start an emergency wipe after a 3 s Undo. Off by default so a stray tap cannot erase anything; Settings > Emergency wipe (type WIPE to confirm) is always available. Untested on phones. |
| Local reporting (`test_local_reporting=false`) | On | DEBUG control removes Report and restores the old lists. Report uses the existing local block path, hides that identity from chats, notifications, Recent and attributable streams (their own direct route, and a route whose source proves it is them, which is then left and hidden until the app restarts). It keeps no stream tag or route, because a stream's tag and advertised ID are chosen by its sender, so a copied tag cannot hide someone else's stream. "Hide this livestream" keeps a route (tag and advertised ID) only when this phone's link proved that fixed ID, otherwise it hides that phone's offer until the app restarts; tags older builds kept are no longer read (security fix, no switch). No submission; optional `report_url` is empty and opens only on a separate tap. One-time identities cannot be individually blocked; the existing explanation applies. JVM regressions added; not run on phones. |
| Nearby list UX (`test_nearby_list_ux=false`) | On | DEBUG control restores alphabetical order, no search and technical row details. Default keeps first-seen order per session, contacts first, search above eight people, and Call/Add contact/Block on long press. One-time strangers use the existing explained Block unknown callers option. |
| QL consent and result off (`test_ql_consent_ui=false`) [ql_consent_ui] | On | Control arm. On (Design QL revision 2, design decisions Q-QL2a-c): the scan confirmation has "Let <name>'s phone recognise yours nearby" (checked by default; unchecking writes "Can see when I'm nearby" off before any lease can leave, and a failed write withholds the lease), Add contact shows a plain result (callable from Contacts / declined / not finished / one scan) and Call now for 60 s after a lease was stored (an ordinary Contacts call). Off: today's dialog and texts. Turning "Can see when I'm nearby" off warns that contacts will need to call or scan again. Until the user answers here or on the contact screen, "Can see when I'm nearby" is not yet asked and counts as off: no OFFER and no lease go to that contact (design decision 2026-11-06 for all platforms, replacing the default on; no switch; answers stored by older builds keep their meaning). With this control arm (no choice shown at the scan) the contact stays not yet asked. |

## Naming and identity display (Design G, Naming v2)

Branch `feature/naming-v2`, built 2026-10-12 from DECISIONS "Design G" and PROTOCOL "Naming v2". Every row is a pure core with unit tests; what each row still needs from a phone is its check (N1-N9 in DECISIONS). The one precedence function is `DisplayIdentityResolver`; screens call it and draw `DisplayIdentity` (rank, title, note, mark, warning), never building a name string by hand.

| Switch | Default | What it does and why it is kept |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Friendly names for nearby phones (`generated_names`; no Settings switch since 2026-11-04; DEBUG `test_generated_names`; `NamingFeatureDefaults.GENERATED_NAMES`) | On for users, always (display only, no privacy effect; an "off" stored by 0.4.4 or older is ignored); off is only the DEBUG arm | A phone with a rotating ID shows as a two-word name such as "Blue Otter", derived from its public 6-byte rotating ID (or the throwaway key of a stranger call), so people can say it aloud and nothing stable is revealed. A name is never an authenticator; colliding names get digits from the ID. The DEBUG off arm shows "Nearby phone". The 256-word lists `assets/names/adjectives-v2.txt` and `nouns-v2.txt` and the pair blocklist `blocked-pairs-v2.txt` are original work, frozen and hash-pinned (a changed list is `names-v3`; `names-v1` is kept for vector history). A name from a blocked pair is never shown: the same ID is re-hashed with a counter byte until the pair is allowed. |
| Who sees my name (`names_audience`: People I call or who call me / Only my contacts / Nobody (labels before 2026-11-04: Everyone I call or answer / Only people I verified); Settings > Your ID (a row under the name, shown only when names are on and a name is set); DEBUG `test_names_audience=everyone\|verified\|nobody`) | Everyone I call or answer | Needs "Names and titles" on (on by default). Decides whether the kind 1 name is sent at all. "Only people I verified": the caller sends its name only to a callee known to be a verified contact, or after the callee's verified reveal proves it is one; the callee sends its name only to a verified contact at Ringing, or after the caller's verified reveal (a contact whose lease expired arrives as a stranger, so nothing is guessed before then). Declining or letting it ring reveals nothing. A peer without hello bit 5 (a 0.2.0-beta phone with names off) gets nothing and nothing breaks. `CallLabelPolicy`. Untested on phones. |
| Experimental: Show my name before they answer (`names_before_answer`; no Settings switch; DEBUG `test_names_before_answer` only; `NamingFeatureDefaults.NAMES_BEFORE_ANSWER`) | Off for every launch since 2026-11-06 (design decision "A caller's name waits for the reveal"; it was on for user launches from 2026-10-12 to 2026-11-06; a stored choice of an older build is ignored) | Off, the default: a stranger this phone calls gets the caller's name only after its `REVEAL` is verified and the revealed person passes this phone's block check, with "Who sees my name" applied again at that moment; a callee that showed its real key gets it unless that key is blocked (`CallerNameRelease`). The callee's ring screen shows the made-up name until then. On, the comparison arm (never in a release build): the name is queued before `INVITE`, signed by the attempt pseudonym, so the ring screen shows the claimed name ("~Rahul"); a person this phone blocked, calling or answering from a fresh throwaway, receives it. Kept by the rule never to delete an experiment. Untested on phones (check N2). |
| Verification codes `BVMID2` (`bvmid2`; on, no Settings switch since 2026-11-04, a stored choice is ignored; DEBUG `test_bvmid2`; `NamingFeatureDefaults.BVMID2`) | On by default since 2026-10-19 (design decision Q-QL1, after N5 and the Design QL phone test passed on two Android pairs); an explicit stored choice wins | Replaces the static BVMID1 QR with an expiring one-use code: a fresh secret `K_qr` plus the displaying phone's current role-0 ID, valid 300 s, reserved atomically for one connection's attempt (link and end-to-end handshake together), consumed only by a completed handshake, with a per-code cap of 3 attempts and a signing budget of its own (3, one more every 10 s; since 2026-10-27, before that the shared one). Needs Hidden identities (BMH3) as well as rotating IDs: with BMH3 off the BVMID1 flow remains (since 2026-10-27), and while only the Meet screen makes the phone reachable every link that did not match the code is closed, whatever handshake it spoke. The session opened with it is verify-only. The scanner confirms "Is this Rahul's screen you scanned?" before the shield; "scanned each other" needs both phones to scan. With it ON, a BVMID1 scan records only "Checked by code" (outline) unless the next switch is on, and a code-only contact gets no new presence leases until scanned both ways. Off: nothing changes. Bound on Android (branch feature/naming-v2-bind): Add contact > Show my code draws the code on the role-0 ID on air (FLAG_SECURE; screen-only reachability when Allow calls is off) and Scan their code dials the phone as a quiet verification scan (no call screen), asks "Is this X's screen?" and only then records the shield. JVM-tested core; the radio path passed N5 and the Design QL phone test on two Android pairs. Design QL (2026-10-19): the verification session also carries the presence OFFER/LEASE once each way (scanner after its confirmation, displayer only to a scanner it already verified), so after two scans Contacts can call at once; DEBUG harness (DebugMeetControl) drives N5 and the QL phone test without a camera in a normal (user-default) launch: `--es debug_meet open` opens the screen (no `test_` prefix, so the launch is not a benchmark launch), then the app-private file `files/debug-meet-command` (`show`, or `scan` + code + `yes`/`no`) drives it; the live code is in `files/debug-meet-code`. |
| Visible by name (`visible_by_name`; Settings > Experiments switch "Visible by name" and, when on, the row "Show my name nearby"; DEBUG `test_visible_by_name`; `NamingFeatureDefaults.VISIBLE_BY_NAME`) | Off until phone check N7 passes | An opt-in ten-minute window (for a meeting, a camp, a festival line) in which people nearby see a name you picked ("~Rahul, calls itself") next to a dedicated window ID (role 3, announcement version 8, a connectable offer carrying the 4-byte name hash) and name pages (version 7, a non-connectable set). The ordinary call-ready ID never carries the name, and the window ends after exactly ten minutes of elapsed time, shared with Visible-by-handle (starting one ends the other). The name is an unsigned claim: shown only while exactly one hash is heard for the ID, never as trusted, replaced by the signed label once a call authenticates the peer. With the switch off nothing is read or sent; 0.2.0-beta scanners reject versions 7 and 8 and show no row and no error, so they cannot see or call a phone through its window ID. Built: pages and offer codec, bounded scanner reassembly and board, rows ("~Name, calls itself - Gold Fox"), window state and exclusion, opening entries, and (branch feature/naming-v2-bind) the connectable role-3 window offer set and the name-page set owned by the call endpoint (rebuilt on the new window ID at each epoch boundary, closed while a call or exchange holds the endpoint, "This phone cannot show a name to others" without three free advertising sets), the call listener serving role 3, the ongoing notification with Stop now, the Settings row with name, cost text and the optional "allow anyone to call for these ten minutes". Untested on phones (N7). |
| Nearby-row and contact warnings (no switch) | Always on | "More than one phone is using this ID" (the same ID heard on two addresses at once, repeatedly: a second tap before the call), a neutral "Says they are Mum. The app will check when you answer." beside a window name that imitates a contact, and on the contact sheet "They call themselves X" with the one-time change note (Use it as my label / Ignore) and "Scan to confirm" for a contact only checked by code. Contact labels are unique (a rename that collides is refused). |
| Stranger recents (no switch) | Always on | An unverified stranger's Recent entry keeps the generated name and the time only (no node ID, no fingerprint); a person verified afterwards appears under Contacts. Review F20. |
| Text rules for person names (no switch) | Always on | A person name may not start or end with a tilde or look-alike, contain a tilde, shield or lock symbol or the app's separators, or reduce to a generated name; a receiver drops such a label (`reserved_form`). Needed so a claimed name cannot imitate the marks. Contact labels are also forced unique (case- and skeleton-insensitive). |
| Contact claims file (no switch) | Written only for verified contacts | `identity-verification/contact-claims.bin` (sealed, atomic) keeps what a verified contact last called themselves and whether both phones scanned. A downgrade loses only that note. Forget, Reset and the emergency wipe delete it. |

## Wi-Fi carriers and wider reach (Design WF; all off by default)

| Switch | Default | What it does and why it is kept |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Use Wi-Fi when it helps (Settings, top page, with the next two rows indented under it; DEBUG `test_wifi_carrier`) [set by the case "carrier" key] | Off (Experimental; `ConnectivityFeatureDefaults.WIFI_CARRIER`) | After a call or stream hop connects over Bluetooth, both phones may move it to Wi-Fi Aware when both support it; Bluetooth stays connected as the standby and takes over if Wi-Fi stops. Calls need the BMH3 handshake (always on since 2026-11-04; DEBUG `test_bmh3`); a call in which only one side uses a temporary identity stays on Bluetooth, because Wi-Fi needs both identities revealed. Turned on during a call or live stream, it works from the next one. Android 13 and newer asks for Nearby Wi-Fi devices when the switch is turned on; a refused permission leaves it off, and a switch whose permission was later removed shows "Not active". Wi-Fi is used only after the Bluetooth link has authenticated both phones; during each move to Wi-Fi both phones briefly advertise a one-time Wi-Fi Aware name and show a random Wi-Fi Aware address. While a Wi-Fi link is open, the other phone can reach other apps on this phone that accept network connections from any network. Phones you stream with, and the phones next to this one when it relays a call, can see which MeshLive version you use. On Android 11 and 12 it takes effect only together with Wider reach (the info sheet says so). Android 10 never uses Wi-Fi Aware. Kept off until the measurement plan passes (decision D5). |
| Wider reach over Wi-Fi (Settings, top page, under "Use Wi-Fi when it helps"; DEBUG `test_wifi_wider_reach`) [set by the case "carrier" key] | Off (Experimental). Requires `wifi_carrier` | Also publishes and subscribes to the same rotating discovery announcement over Wi-Fi Aware, so people can be found and called over Wi-Fi even when Bluetooth is blocked, and connects to a phone seen only over Wi-Fi. Adds battery use. Nearby Wi-Fi devices can see that this phone uses Wi-Fi Aware. Its Wi-Fi name changes at least every 30 minutes, with a new Wi-Fi Aware address each time it can; while a Wi-Fi link is open the address may not change. Any nearby phone can open a Wi-Fi link to it before proving who it is (the signed link handshake then decides), and while a Wi-Fi link is open the other phone can reach other apps on this phone that accept network connections from any network. Turning it off stops Wi-Fi discovery at once; a Wi-Fi link already open stays until its call or stream ends. On Android 11 and 12 it needs Location on and fine location permission and works while the app is visible (the Play build never runs it in the background); there the phone cannot read the band of a Wi-Fi link, and Wider-reach links are 5 GHz only, so it finds people over Wi-Fi but does not open Wi-Fi-only links, and with Bluetooth off it does not claim to be reachable over Wi-Fi. Not offered on Android 10. |
| Wi-Fi Direct for phones and PCs without Wi-Fi Aware (Settings, top page, under "Use Wi-Fi when it helps"; turning it on asks for confirmation that shows this phone's Wi-Fi Direct name; DEBUG `test_wifi_direct`) [set by the case "carrier" key] | Off (Experimental). Requires `wifi_carrier` | Lets the phone move a hop to a Wi-Fi Direct group when Wi-Fi Aware is not available. Nearby devices can see the phone's Wi-Fi Direct name and may recognise it across calls by its Wi-Fi Direct address, which is why it is a separate opt-in. While a group is open its network name has a pattern that identifies MeshLive, and a nearby device can send the phone an invitation to connect. While a Wi-Fi link is open, the other phone can reach other apps on this phone that accept network connections from any network. The group is temporary, 5 GHz only, and never persistent. Turning it off moves every call and stream hop on Wi-Fi Direct back to Bluetooth at once (a call whose Bluetooth link is gone or not heard lately stays on Wi-Fi until Bluetooth is back or the call ends, with a notice). On Android 10 to 12 it needs Location on and the fine location permission. |
| Wi-Fi probe (DEBUG `test_wifi_probe`) [wifi_probe] | Off (Experimental) | Census only: records what the phone's Wi-Fi Aware and Wi-Fi Direct stacks offer; never upgrades a hop. Works with or without `test_wifi_carrier` (it is not in the switch-inert table). |
| Wi-Fi Aware instant communication mode (DEBUG `test_wifi_icm`) [wifi_icm] | Off (Experimental) | A/B arm: asks for instant communication mode on phones that support it (Android 13 and newer): on the 5 GHz band for the sessions that carry a call or stream, and on the 2.4 GHz social channel for the Wider reach discovery sessions, to measure set-up time against battery. Requires / masked by: `test_wifi_carrier`. |
| Lower Bluetooth standby priority on Wi-Fi (DEBUG `test_wifi_ble_standby_low_power`) [reserved; not a runner tunable] | Off (Experimental; Reserved, not wired) | Planned A/B arm: while Wi-Fi carries the media, the Bluetooth standby link would ask for a lower connection priority. Requires / masked by: `test_wifi_carrier`. Reserved, no consumer yet: parsed and logged, no code reads it, and the benchmark runner refuses it (R-4). |
| Allow a 2.4 GHz Wi-Fi client (DEBUG `test_wifi_allow_24ghz`) [wifi_allow_24ghz] | Off (Experimental) | Measured arm of decision D9: a phone whose own Wi-Fi client is on 2.4 GHz normally declines a Wi-Fi upgrade (2.4 GHz Wi-Fi halved Bluetooth throughput on the M31), and a phone that joins a Wi-Fi Direct group below 4900 MHz, or whose frequency it cannot read, declines too; this arm does not decline, to measure what that costs. Requires / masked by: `test_wifi_carrier`. |
| Wi-Fi Aware derived port (DEBUG `test_wifi_aware_derived_port=false` is the control arm) [wifi_aware_derived_port] | On (promoted 2026-11-10 on mechanism; release builds too) | The host's Wi-Fi Aware server port is derived from the shared secret and the attempt ticket (PROTOCOL "Keys, names and passwords", `port`) instead of drawn at random, and a guest whose platform reports no port for the data path (`WifiAwareNetworkInfo.getPort()` is 0) connects to the derived one instead of ending with `no_port`; a nonzero platform port is used as given. The port is not a secret and no check is removed: the data path stays PMK-encrypted, the host accepts only the peer's data-path address and the BWU1 handshake still authenticates. The control arm restores the old behaviour exactly (random host port, a guest without a platform port ends `no_port`); both phones need the rule, since a host on a random port refuses a guest that falls back. A Wi-Fi-native (Wider reach) initiator likewise connects to the port of the responder's `MWA1` answer when the platform reports none (no switch: that port was already on the wire). Requires / masked by: `test_wifi_carrier`. |
| Wi-Fi Aware required channel (DEBUG `test_wifi_aware_require_channel`) [wifi_aware_require_channel] | Off (Experimental) | The 5 GHz channel in the data-path request becomes a requirement (`setChannelFrequencyMhz(mhz, true)`): the firmware must use it or reject the request. Without it the platform may add other scheduled channels (a 2.4 GHz entry made the band check end the attempt as `band_low`). The band check is unchanged: any channel below 5 GHz is still refused. Only on phones where `isSetChannelOnDataPathSupported()` is true. Requires / masked by: `test_wifi_carrier`. |
| Wi-Fi Aware band by the 5 GHz data channel (DEBUG `test_aware_band_strict` is the old-rule control arm) [aware_band_strict] | On (new default, design decision 2026-11-09) | An Aware data path whose channel list contains at least one channel >= 5000 MHz is accepted even when the 2.4 GHz NAN social channel 2437 MHz (channel 6) is reported beside it; a path with no 5 GHz channel, or any other channel below 5000 MHz, is still refused. The data path logs its channel list and verdict (`wifi_upgrade: event=band ... chMhz=5745,2437 verdict=ok`). While an Aware hop carries, delivered goodput is measured over 5 s windows against the hop's requested media rate: three consecutive windows below it (with media offered) are reported (`event=quality_fallback_suppressed reason=aware_goodput_low`) and, only under the Experimental arm of the row "Wi-Fi Aware goodput guard acts" below, return the hop to Bluetooth through the existing quality fallback and skip the next attempt in the call; a good window or a window without offered media resets the count. `test_aware_band_strict=true` restores the old all-channels-5GHz rule (the `MIN_5GHZ_MHZ` boundary, so 4900 MHz counts). No key, CAPS, CREDS, BWU1 or encryption change; the fallback is the existing cutover path. JVM tests only; phone A/B pending. **Requires / masked by:** the Wi-Fi carrier (`test_wifi_carrier` or the case `carrier` key); the goodput guard applies to Aware only. |
| Wi-Fi Aware on 2.4 GHz when the phone carries no other Bluetooth media (DEBUG `test_aware_24ghz_alone`) [aware_24ghz_alone] | Off (Experimental) | Design decision: Aware is preferred over Bluetooth whenever it is available, 5 GHz first. With the switch on, an Aware data path whose every channel is in the 2.4 GHz band is accepted when this phone carries no other Bluetooth media link, and an Aware attempt is allowed beside a 2.4 GHz Wi-Fi client connection under the same condition (Wi-Fi Direct keeps its 5 GHz group request). Once the media has moved to Wi-Fi the hop's own Bluetooth standby carries only small control records, so sharing the 2.4 GHz band costs that hop nothing; any other Bluetooth media on the phone would lose airtime, so the 5 GHz rule then applies. "Other Bluetooth media" is answered for a call only, from state the app already keeps (no radio query): a stream this phone serves, watches or forwards, a call it relays, the manual relay role. A stream or relay hop cannot see whether the phone is also in a call and keeps the 5 GHz rule, as does the Wi-Fi-native link. A path that mixes another low channel with 5 GHz (2412 beside 5745) and an empty or missing channel list keep the 5 GHz rule; `test_aware_band_strict` wins over this switch. Bluetooth media that appears beside a live 2.4 GHz hop does not move the hop mid-call: it is logged (`event=band_24_with_bt_media`), the next attempt follows the 5 GHz rule, and while the path is live Bluetooth capacity is learned under the 2.4 GHz coexistence key, not the clean-radio key. Every verdict logs its facts (`wifi_upgrade: event=band ... chMhz=2412 ... alone24=true otherBtMedia=false band=2g4_alone`; `event=sta_band` for the client-connection rule; `wifi_discovery: event=native_band`). Off = the 5 GHz rule exactly. No key, record format or encryption change; with the switch on, a phone beside a 2.4 GHz client connection that carries no other Bluetooth media sets the existing Aware bit of its `CAPS`. Off by default because no test pair can produce a 2.4 GHz-only Aware path yet, so the Bluetooth standby beside it is unmeasured. JVM tests only. **Requires / masked by:** the Wi-Fi carrier (`test_wifi_carrier` or the case `carrier` key); `test_aware_band_strict` (the strict rule wins); API 33 for the channel list. |
| Wi-Fi Aware goodput guard acts (DEBUG `test_aware_goodput_guard`) [aware_goodput_guard] | Off (Experimental; the guard only logs) | The goodput guard of the band row above compares what the hop sent with its configured rate, so a healthy link under an encoder that is still ramping up, or that sends less than configured, reads low. By default it therefore only measures: every 5 s window is logged (`event=goodput_window`) and three low windows log `event=quality_fallback_suppressed reason=aware_goodput_low`; the hop stays on Wi-Fi. With the switch on, the hop returns to Bluetooth through the existing quality fallback; the `carrier:` line and the skipped next attempt follow only a fallback that happened (`event=quality_fallback_noop` when the standby was stale and nothing moved). The queue rule (`reason=queue_slow`: the send queue's oldest record older than 300 ms for 5 s) is unchanged and remains the protection for a Wi-Fi hop that stops delivering. Kept as the arm for a redesigned guard. JVM tests only. **Requires / masked by:** the Wi-Fi carrier; Aware only. |
| Share the Wi-Fi Aware attach (DEBUG `test_wifi_share_attach`) [wifi_share_attach] | Off (Experimental) | Measured arm: at the Private level the phone may attach while another app already holds Wi-Fi Aware (normally it declines). Requires / masked by: `test_wifi_carrier`. |
| Force one carrier (DEBUG `test_wifi_force_carrier`: aware, direct or bluetooth) [set by the case "carrier" key] | Off (Experimental) | Benchmark arm: try only the named carrier and log it as `requested` in `BENCHMARK carrier:`, so a silent Bluetooth fallback shows as a failed Wi-Fi case. Requires / masked by: `test_wifi_carrier`. |
| Exact call bitrate on Wi-Fi (DEBUG `test_wifi_exact_kbps`, used with `test_rate_control`) [wifi_exact_kbps] | Off (Experimental) | Measurement arm for throughput on a Wi-Fi carrier (plan M2): the call asks for exactly this many kbps of video and the request is not clamped to the Bluetooth transport maximum; nothing is raised or lowered on the way, so a low result shows as a failed case, not a silent change. Ignored without an exact allocation (`test_rate_control` cbr, vbr or cbr_fd). Works with or without `test_wifi_carrier`. Not a user feature. |
| Cutover keeps the FIFO slot (DEBUG `test_cutover_keep_fifo_slot=false` is the control arm) [cutover_keep_fifo_slot] | On (promoted 2026-11-11, measured with the next row: Wi-Fi Direct calls S22 <-> A15s, 12 per arm) | A record sealed while Bluetooth is the send carrier keeps its place in that carrier's FIFO, ahead of a SWITCH marker queued later. Off (today): a producer that finds the Bluetooth carrier's content queue (2 records) full waits outside the lock and, when the switch to Wi-Fi happens meanwhile, goes out on Wi-Fi below its own SWITCH boundary, and the receiver drops it as `carrier_drop reason=stale` (one lost record, a broken frame until the next key frame). On: the record is queued at once (one over the capacity per blocked producer) and the producer then waits for room, so the record, then the SWITCH marker, travel on the old carrier in order. Measured together with the next row (package SW1): on the S22 <-> A15s pair 12 of 12 Wi-Fi Direct calls reached Wi-Fi with no failed switch and no freeze-gate failure, against 11 of 12 with 4 failed switches and 4 freeze-gate failures off; on ASUS <-> A15s and S22 <-> ASUS the failure did not occur in either arm. Release builds keep it on. The DEBUG `carrier_drop` line now carries carrier, seq, boundary, lastOpened and source (`arrive`, `release` or `send`). |
| Liveness stamp before the record lock (DEBUG `test_liveness_before_record_lock=false` is the control arm) [liveness_before_record_lock] | On (promoted 2026-11-11 with the row above) | The Bluetooth reader stamps the call's liveness time before it takes the record lock. Off (today): the stamp waits behind the consumer, which holds that lock while the video queue's back-pressure wait (up to 5 s) runs, so a healthy link can look silent and a false renewal starts. On: a record of the current link generation counts as heard at once. Known limit: the same reader thread still waits for the lock before it reads the next record, so the gain is the one record already in hand. Measured and promoted with the row above (package SW1); release builds keep it on. |

## Sharing the app

| Switch | Default | What it does and why it is kept |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Share app nearby (Settings > Features "Show Share app nearby in Help"; DEBUG `test_offline_app_share` is the benchmark switch) | Promoted 2026-10-11 (Help entry hidden until its phone test passes; one constant, `ConnectivityFeatureDefaults.SHARE_APP_NEARBY`; flipping it makes the entry a normal Help entry) | Experimental (2026-10-10; Briar/Bitchat pattern re-implemented from a description, no GPL code). Available in all three distributions (`BuildConfig.APP_SHARE_AVAILABLE=true`), with the same control and split-install refusal; outbound sharing is not self-updating (PLAY_RELEASE "Policy conclusions"). Adds Help > "Share app nearby": a Wi-Fi Direct group (`WifiP2pConfig.Builder` with `GROUP_OWNER_BAND_5GHZ` first where `WifiManager.is5GHzBandSupported`, then AUTO, then legacy `createGroup`, because 2.4 GHz Wi-Fi halved M31 Bluetooth; random `DIRECT-xy-...` name without the app name, random 12-character passphrase), and `OfflineAppShareServer`, a read-only HTTP server bound to the group IPv4 address that accepts only peers in the group subnet and serves only `/<20-character random token>/` (a static page with the APK SHA-256 and signing-certificate SHA-256) and `/<token>/BluetoothVideo-<version>-<code>.apk` (single Range for resume); everything else is 404, no listing. Two QR codes: `WIFI:T:WPA;S:..;P:..;;` and the URL. Stops after 10 minutes, when the screen is left, or when a stream, call, relay or reconnect exists (it refuses to start then). Needs `NEARBY_WIFI_DEVICES` (`neverForLocation`, Android 13+) or fine location with Location on (Android 10-12), requested only by that screen; adds the install-time `INTERNET` and `CHANGE_WIFI_STATE` permissions (an IP socket needs `INTERNET`; the share server itself makes no internet connection, and the only internet request the app can make is the opt-in update check below). Split installs and debug-key release builds are refused as in `AppShare`. DEBUG line `BENCHMARK offline_app_share`. No streaming case changes. **Phone test (S24 sharing, M31 and ASUS receiving; then the reverse), pass when:** the receiver joins from the Wi-Fi QR code, opens the URL, downloads the APK, and its SHA-256 and signing-certificate SHA-256 match the page and the release; the share stops by itself after 10 minutes and when the screen is left; it refuses to start during a call or stream, with the reason shown; a stream or call started after stopping runs as without the share; and with Wi-Fi Direct unavailable the screen says so. Not yet tested on phones. |
| Check for updates (Settings > About and updates, github flavour only; DEBUG `test_check_for_updates` is the benchmark switch; DEBUG `test_update_latest_url`, `test_update_pin_own_signer`, `test_update_release_number`, `test_update_release_key` stand in for GitHub and the release key in phone test) [check_for_updates] | Off until the one-time question is answered (`UpdateFeatureDefaults.CHECK_FOR_UPDATES`); the answer is stored as an explicit choice | Product feature (2026-10-11, design decision; patterns from Signal-Android `ApkUpdateJob`/`ApkUpdateInstaller` (PackageInstaller session, flavour-only manifest, digest check), NewPipe `NewVersionWorker` (only a copy signed with the release key checks), Molly's opt-in megaphone, Obtainium (`getPackageArchiveInfo` + signing certificates); none copied). The `github` flavour (what `tools/make_release.py` builds; `app/build.gradle.kts` `productFlavors`) adds `UpdateActivity`, a not-persisted daily `JobScheduler` job and `REQUEST_INSTALL_PACKAGES`; the `fdroid` flavour (the F-Droid recipe) has a stub `FlavourUpdater`, no job and the permission removed from the merged manifest (`tools/tests/test_flavour_manifests.py`). Offered only when the installed APK's only signer is `AppSharePolicy.PUBLISHED_RELEASE_CERT_SHA256` (the NewPipe guard). The first home screen after install asks "Check for updates when online?" once (yes or no, never again; a benchmark launch never asks; DEBUG `test_user_launch=true` makes a launch with test extras behave as a person's, so the question and its Yes path can be driven with the stand-ins above); Settings shows the same explanation before it turns the switch on. With yes: `GET https://api.github.com/repos/MeshLive/meshlive-android/releases/latest` (`UpdateSource.GITHUB`; a mirror is one more `UpdateSource`) at most once a day across the app, the job and a manual "Check now" (`UpdateSchedule`: 24 h after an answer, 1 h after an offline attempt, GitHub's reset time after a rate limit, 10 min between manual checks), minimal headers (`User-Agent: MeshLive-updater`, no cookies), redirects only to github.com, api.github.com or githubusercontent.com over HTTPS. A newer semantic version (`ReleaseVersion`) shows a quiet home note "Update available"; **Download** (a tap, only while the switch is on) fetches `MeshLive-<version>-<abi>.apk` for the installed ABI (never the `MeshLive.apk` alias) into `noBackupFilesDir/updates/` while hashing it, requires its SHA-256 to equal the API asset `digest` or the release `SHA256SUMS` (none: nothing is installed), then `PackageManager.getPackageArchiveInfo(GET_SIGNING_CERTIFICATES)` must show this package, a strictly higher release number, the installed ABI digit (or universal), exactly one signer equal to the pinned release certificate, or a signing lineage signed by it for a later key rotation (`UpdateVerifier`). On Android 10 the archive is read with `GET_SIGNATURES` as well, because that release collects no certificates otherwise. Install writes the same file into a `PackageInstaller` session while hashing again and aborts if the digest differs from the verified one (`HashedCopy.copyBoundTo`: no check-then-use gap), with `USER_ACTION_REQUIRED` (Android's own confirmation; never silent, never a downgrade). If "Install unknown apps" is off for MeshLive the screen explains and opens Android's settings page. The 120 MB limit, the host allowlist and the redirect rule are in `GitHubReleases`/`UpdateSource`. Serval's trick of a manifest inside the APK zip comment is not used (it breaks APK signing); a separate signed manifest was considered and left out because the APK signature already authenticates the bytes (PLAN "Distribution and field reports"). Log lines `BENCHMARK update_check`, `update_prepare`, `update_install`. No streaming case changes. **Phone test (github flavour, S24 and one more phone; test server on the host computer through `adb reverse` or Wi-Fi, `test_update_latest_url=http://127.0.0.1:8000/releases/latest`, `test_update_pin_own_signer=true`, a debug-signed "newer" APK with a higher release number), pass when:** with no answer or "No thanks" the server log shows zero requests across a day of app starts and reboots; with yes it shows one request per day however often the app, the job or Check now run, and Check now within 10 minutes is refused; a newer release shows the home note, Download progresses, Install shows Android's confirmation and the phone ends on the new versionCode; and each refusal case leaves the installed app unchanged and the file deleted: wrong SHA-256, no published hash, a file signed with another key, the same or a lower versionCode, another package name, an armeabi-v7a file for an arm64 install, a redirect to another host, a 403 rate limit, an oversized or truncated download, going offline mid-download. A release-signed build also shows the one-time question exactly once; build it with the release key only at the current base `versionCode` (never above the next release's, because a nearby phone would accept it as an update) and never publish it. The F-Droid-flavour APK has no "Check for updates" row, makes no request while the nearby note is on, and `dumpsys package` lists no `REQUEST_INSTALL_PACKAGES`. Final check after the first release that contains the updater: a phone on it sees the next release through the real GitHub API. Not yet tested on phones. |
| Tell me about newer versions nearby (Settings > About and updates, both flavours; DEBUG `test_newer_versions_nearby=true` is the benchmark switch) [newer_versions_nearby] | **On** (`UpdateFeatureDefaults.NEWER_VERSIONS_NEARBY`, design decision2026-10-11) | Product feature with a wire change (2026-10-11, PROTOCOL "Build tag in the link hello and update verification"). Link-hello transport-mask bit 7 says nonce bytes 0-1 are a build tag (bit 15 project release key, bits 14-0 the release number = installed `versionCode / 10`, so the CPU type is not revealed); no length, version or record type changes, so every older BMH2 build interoperates and simply sees no tag (hello decoding, tagged and untagged handshakes in both roles, signature binding and invisibility to the rest of the app: `BuildTagTest`). The tag is read only after the handshake verified it, handed to the notice only from links this phone opened (a stranger who connects to a listener cannot plant a note), kept out of `peerCapabilities`, never put in an advertisement or an end-to-end hello, and unset when the switch is off, in a benchmark launch without `test_newer_versions_nearby`, and for a debug or non-split build (so measured cases keep their exact hello bytes). It is a claim by the peer's identity key, not proof about its APK, so it only decides whether a quiet home note "Newer version nearby (release N)" is shown (`NewerVersionNearby`: newer than installed, at most 20 above, release-key phones only believe release-key claims, dismissal hides for a week, a claim fades after two weeks, nothing about the peer is stored, never during a call or stream). Tapping it opens the update screen's nearby card (github flavour: explains the steps, **Choose the file** copies the picked APK into the private directory and verifies it exactly like a download except that the signer must equal the installed one, then the same hashed PackageInstaller install) or a dialog (F-Droid flavour: update through F-Droid or share the file; nothing is installed by MeshLive). Needs the other phone's Help > Share app nearby or any other way of moving the file. **Phone test (two phones with debug builds, `test_newer_versions_nearby=true`, `test_update_release_number=1` on one and `2` on the other, `test_update_release_key=true` on both), pass when:** after a call and a stream join in each role over CoC, GATT and Classic the older phone shows the home note naming release 2 once the session is over, the newer phone shows none, and `BENCHMARK newer_version_nearby` / `newer_versions_nearby: ... sendsTag=` lines agree; a 0.1.0-beta release build connects to a tagged phone with the same setup success and time as a control run (no `SECURITY`, "Invalid handshake" or "Trailing handshake data" lines on either side); switching off on either phone removes the tag (`sendsTag=null`) and the note; a claim 21 or more above (`test_update_release_number=30`) shows nothing; Hide removes the note until a higher release; a benchmark launch without the extra sends no tag. **Phone test (after the share test passes; github flavour, a newer and an older build signed with the same key):** the newer phone shares through Help > Share app nearby, the older phone receives the file by the browser or any other route and picks it with **Choose the file**; the older phone offers Install with the new version name and Android's confirmation appears; the same flow with a file that is older, signed with another key, a different package, or for the other CPU type ends with the plain-language refusal and nothing installed; the picked file and `noBackupFilesDir/updates/` are empty after an install or a refusal on the next app start. Not yet tested on phones. |

## Audio-only live (radio; implemented 2026-10-12, on by default since 2026-10-17)

Design: DECISIONS "Design AL"; wire: PROTOCOL "Experimental audio-only live streams". Every row is off, or is the control arm, unless stated. With the feature off, the app is byte-for-byte unchanged on air.

| Switch | Default | What it does and why it is kept |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `test_audio_live_kbps` | 24 (Voice) | Overrides the preset: 16 Low data, 24 Voice, 48 Music. |
| `test_audio_live_batch_frames` | 10 (200 ms) | Opus frames per record (1-20). 10 gives 31.8 kbps on the mesh link at 24 kbps audio; 2 (the pair record) gives 52 kbps; kept for the airtime-against-granularity comparison. |
| `test_audio_live_fec` | Off | In-band Opus FEC with a loss hint. Off because every hop is a reliable ordered link and the only loss is whole-record age shedding, which FEC cannot repair. Arm kept. |
| `test_audio_live_dtx` | Off | Opus DTX. Off because it removes the steady cadence that liveness, the relay trickle test and the playout clock assume, and VBR already codes silence in a few bytes. Arm kept. |
| `test_audio_live_age_bound` | On inside the feature; `false` is the control arm | Sending hops drop their oldest whole records beyond 3 s (down to 1.5 s). Control: unbounded queues, as for video. A/B T-AL8 informs the user's decision. |
| `test_audio_live_max_children` | 4 | Direct listeners per source, below the bandwidth-derived count (11-18), until the fan-out ladder (T-AL4) measures a cap per transport. |
| `test_audio_live_link_priority` | `high` | `balanced` requests a 30-50 ms connection interval on audio-only GATT links (source to child and relay to child) to save power and avoid the equal-interval relay stage. Experimental; the weak-pair rate is the risk. |
| `test_audio_live_adaptive` | Off | Closed-loop 16/24/48 kbps from link and queue evidence, no wire change. Experimental. **Not wired (reserved only): the name is accepted and does nothing in this version.** |
| `test_audio_live_relay_children` | 1 | Children per audio relay; 2 is phase 2, Experimental, gated on the dual-role relay measurements. **Not wired (reserved only): the name is accepted, a relay keeps one child.** |
| `test_audio_live_autostart` (DEBUG; with `test_audio_live=true`) | Off | Starts an audio-only broadcast without taps, for phones that ignore injected taps. Skips the first-use disclosure in this launch only, logged as `BENCHMARK audio_live_autostart`. Optional `test_audio_live_preset=voice\|lowdata\|music` (default voice) and `test_audio_title=<text>`. Also works as a new intent on a running app. |
| `test_audio_live_digests` (DEBUG) | Off | One `BENCHMARK audio_live_units` line per record on both ends (`role=tx` at the source, `role=rx` at a listener after verification) with a short SHA-256 of every Opus unit; lines join on `seq`, and equal hashes show bit-identical delivery where nothing was shed. |
| `test_control=leave`, `audio_mute`, `audio_unmute` (DEBUG) | n/a | Listener side: `leave` returns to the Live tab (`end` does the same while listening, and also ends a broadcast that has no listener yet). `audio_mute` / `audio_unmute` mute the source or the listener. Always logged: `BENCHMARK audio_live_verify` (a signature-verification summary every 50 verified records and one line per rejected record), `audio_live_codec_choice` (native or MediaCodec, requested and effective, for encoder and decoder). |
| `test_audio_live_native_opus` | On | `false` forces MediaCodec encode and decode (no application type, no concealment) as the control arm. |

## Messaging v2 (chat threads, files of every format; phases 1a to 1d built behind the master switch)

Design: DECISIONS "Design M"; wire: PROTOCOL "Messaging v2". Coded so far: the master switch (`MessagingV2Feature`, `MessagingV2Defaults.ENABLED`, benchmark extra `test_messaging_v2`; a constant of the feature catalog: no Settings row and no stored choice), **on whenever Messages is on since the design decision of 2026-10-21** (control arm: DEBUG `test_messaging_v2=false`; v1 history stays readable as legacy rows, unsent v1 messages are resealed once, a contact on an older app shows "Their app is older") with the limits of `MessagingV2Limits` fixed at their defaults; the other rows below are built unless marked. Switches that exist only in the design (bulk-listed segments, a Chats-tab or Contacts-rows arm, the preview toggle, prekeys, the ratchet, message tags, the background check) are unbuilt work and are listed in PLAN. DEBUG controls while the UI does not exist: `sf2_send:PREFIX12:BYTES[:COUNT]` (text up to 8000 bytes, otherwise a generated file), `sf2_approve`, `sf2_read`, `sf2_status` (lines `BENCHMARK message_v2 ...`, sender and receiver SHA-256 side by side). Every row is off, or is the control arm, unless stated. With the master switch off, the app is byte-for-byte the v1 Messages behaviour on air and on the link.

| Switch | Default | What it does and why it is kept |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Messages v2 (no Settings row; `test_messaging_v2`) | On whenever Messages is on (`MessagingV2Defaults.ENABLED` is true, design decision 2026-10-21); Messages itself is off by default, so v2 is off by default too | Conversation threads with receipts, attachments of any format up to 64 MB, a thread link that stays open while the thread is on screen. A benchmark launch follows the same default (an absent extra means the release default; `--ez test_messaging_v2 false` is the v1 control). **Requires / masked by:** Messages. Untested on phones. |
| `test_mv2_chat_session` (built, phase 1d) | On inside the feature; `false` is the control arm | `false` exchanges per message as v1 does (wait for the next sync). Kept for the battery and latency comparison T-M7. DEBUG controls: `sf2_chat_open:PREFIX12` (the thread counts as on screen; discovery on the Call screen dials it), `sf2_chat_close[:PREFIX12]`, `sf2_chat_status`, `sf2_keys:PREFIX12:accept` or `dismiss` (resolve a key change). |
| Read receipts (`test_mv2_read_receipts`, built in 1c; Settings > Privacy "Read receipts", and a per-conversation choice in the thread info) | Off, also a user setting | A message asks for a read receipt only when the sender's switch for that contact is on (the conversation's own choice, else the global switch), and the receiver answers only when its own is on. A DEBUG benchmark launch reads only the extra. |
| Download files automatically (`test_mv2_autodownload_mb`, built in 1b; integer extra, clamped 0 to 64; Settings > Privacy row in 1c: Never, 1, 8, 25 MB, any size) | 8 | Largest segmented file fetched without a tap, from accepted contacts only. 0 never, 64 everything. A benchmark launch ignores the stored Settings choice. |
| Disappearing messages per conversation (thread info; not a launch switch) | Off | The timer new messages of that thread carry (1 hour, 1 day, 1 week, 30 days); the receiver's clock starts on read, the sender's on delivery (Briar's rule). Stored sealed per contact. |
| `test_allow_screenshots` (DEBUG launch extra) | Off | Lifts `FLAG_SECURE` from the main window while a thread is open, so benchmark screenshots of the conversation UI work. Release builds ignore it. DEBUG controls: `sf2_thread:PREFIX12` opens that saved contact's conversation (Contacts tab first), `sf2_thread_close` leaves it. |
| `test_mv2_max_file_mb` (built in 1b; integer extra, clamped 1 to 64) | 64 | Largest file the sender accepts to send; checked before anything is sealed. |
| `test_mv2_photo_edge`, `test_mv2_photo_quality` (built in 1b; clamped 320 to 4096 and 30 to 95) | 1600, 80 | Photo re-encode preset; "Original" on the attach sheet bypasses it (a JPEG keeps its pixels, loses every metadata segment). |
| `test_mv2_decode_isolated` (built in 1b) | On | Tap-to-decode of a received picture runs in an isolated-process service (own uid, no permissions, destroyed after the decode). Off is the control arm: the same bounds in the app process, also the automatic fallback when the service cannot be bound. Kept for the T-M9 comparison on the reference phones. |
| Message notifications (no switch of their own: inside Messages v2; built 2026-10-13) | On when Messages v2 is on | One notification per conversation (channel "Messages", default importance, the user controls sound in system settings), replacing the v1 quiet count notification while the master switch is on. Lock screen: app name and "New message" only (the contact's label too when Settings > Features > "Show names on lock screen" is on); unlocked: label and the text of one unread text message. Tap opens that thread; "Mark as read" only when the text was shown. Untested on phones. |
| `test_mv2_auto_thumbnails` (extra read in 1b; no code consumes it yet: **Not wired**) | Off | Decode manifest thumbnails automatically. Off shows a generic icon and decodes a picture only after a tap. May be turned on only after isolated, resource- and time-limited decoding is proven on every reference phone (Q-M12). |
| `test_mv2_progress_total` (2026-10-13) | On; `false` is the control arm | A plain (non-chat) Messaging v2 exchange keeps its 120 s total limit only while no transfer is moving: while at least 4 chunks per 10 s keep flowing in either direction the total is extended up to 15 minutes (`StoreForwardLimits.sessionHardCapMs`); the 10 s liveness and progress deadlines stay strict. Off restores the fixed 120 s, so a 1 MB or 5 MB file on a slow path (the A15s answering at about 89 kbps) is cut and resumed in a further session behind the cooldown. |
| `test_message_adaptive_phy` (2026-10-13) | On; `false` is the control arm | A message-exchange dial gets the call dial's 2M protections (`MessageDialPhy`): a peer remembered bad on 2M starts on 1M, a first 2M link whose hop authentication has not finished 3 s after it opened is closed (`BENCHMARK adaptive_phy: link=message ... reason=setup_stall`), one bounded 1M retry to the peer's current advertisement follows inside 15 s, and a peer that needed it, or whose authenticated 2M exchange died within 15 s with no HELLO and no chunk (`reason=early_loss`), is remembered as bad on 2M. Needs `test_adaptive_phy`, `test_adaptive_phy_memory` and `test_phy_setup_stall`. Why: six of seven A15s-initiated exchanges to the ASUS failed with an EOFException about 5 s after the 2M PHY update; the same signature as the call setup stall. Off is one attempt on the endpoint PHY, as before. |
